# `mr` (mr-cli) reference

<!-- cspell:ignore gylyp qqsqs qpzry tvdw khce HISTFILE -->

The standalone CLI for the Refugium mr1 format (mnemonic-refugium /
mr-codec). An mr1 card describes a whole set of wallets: one template
(a BIP-388 wallet policy), an epoch axis that moves its deadlines
forward, and a table of slots (rows) that change the template per
wallet. Each wallet of the set is a *member* (e, s): epoch e, slot s.
The card also carries the (0, 0) member as an md1 card.

This chapter mirrors mnemonic-refugium's `mr-cli`: the writers
`create` and `upgrade`, and the read-side verbs `verify`, `decode`,
`repair`, `member`, `address`, `current`, `units` and the heir's
`export`, with the two root-mode verbs `root` and `child`. Fold I adds a
card of several shapes (`--shape`, `create --shapes`) and root mode
(`mr root`, `mr child`, `create --root`); the shapes row of the
Conventions table, "Root mode" under `mr create`, "Root-mode
procedures" and "Root-mode texts" carry them.

:::primer
A *slot* is a row of the card's slot table: slot 0 is the template as
written, and each further slot changes some of its units (switches a
branch off, shifts a deadline, adds a wait). An *epoch* moves every
deadline of the template forward by the card's delta, so the same slot
at a later epoch is a new wallet with later deadlines. `current` tells
you which epoch to use now; `member` and `address` hand out that member.
:::

Every subcommand below accepts `--help` (`-h`) for inline help. Each Flags table quotes `mr <verb> --help` verbatim.

## Conventions

| Topic | Rule |
|---|---|
| Card input | The positional `<mr1>...` is one card: a single `mr1` string, or every string of a chunk set, in order. A chunk set is always one card, never several. It may come before or after the options: `--keys`, `--seeds`, `upgrade`'s `--sibling-keys` and `export`'s `--try-seatings` take every value up to the next flag, but an `mr1` string among their values (no key or seed starts `mr1`) is read as the card, in the order typed, also after two such flags in a row, so `mr verify --network testnet --keys mk1… mk1… mr1…` and `mr verify --network testnet --seeds ms1… --keys mk1… mr1…` work; `upgrade` reads an `md1` string there as its card the same way, and refuses an `mr1` string typed there as ``an md1 card argument is an mr1 string`` |
| Checksums | Every reading verb **verifies only**: a string that is not a valid codeword is refused (`the mr1 checksum does not verify`), never corrected silently; the refusal ends with the string's own length (``; the string has 72 characters, `mr1` included: a character left out or added fails the checksum and `mr repair` cannot correct it, so count the engraved characters and compare them with 72, and with the card length on your kit notes' digest line, where it has one (the `card length` line `mr create` or `mr upgrade` printed)``), the number to count the engraving against (its characters as the codeword reads them, `mr1` included: spaces and hyphens typed between groups are not counted, so a string typed in groups of 4 gets the same count as one typed whole; a card given as a flag value, `export --card` or `create`'s and `upgrade`'s `--sibling` and `--bundle-card`, is read the same way): a kit written before this release has no length on its digest line, and the engraving can always be counted against the typed length. On a chunked card each such refusal of a string adds which entry of the kit's `card length` line it is compared with (``; string 0 is the first string given, and `mr` takes a card's strings in any order: compare its length with its own chunk's entry in the kit's `card length` line (the chunks in the card's order, `2 of 64` being two chunks of 64), where the kit gives one; it is chunk 0 when the strings are typed in the card's order``), since the line lists every chunk's length in chunk order and a typed length can match the wrong entry. The string layer's two length refusals gain the same count and pointer, in every verb that reads a card and in `repair`: a string over 93 symbols after `mr1` (a character added to a full 96-character string meets this before its checksum is read) is refused ``the string has 94 symbols after mr1; the regular code allows at most 93 (80 data plus 13 checksum); the string has 97 characters, `mr1` included: a character added makes a string too long, and `mr repair` cannot correct it, so count the engraved characters and compare them with 97, and with the card length on your kit notes' digest line, where it has one (the `card length` line `mr create` or `mr upgrade` printed)``, and a string under 13 ``the string has 12 symbols after mr1; it needs at least the 13-symbol checksum; the string has 15 characters, `mr1` included, fewer than any card string has: check that the whole string was given, in one argument or quoted whole, and compare it with the card length on your kit notes' digest line, where it has one (the `card length` line `mr create` or `mr upgrade` printed)`` (every string ends in its 13-symbol checksum, so such a string was cut short or split; `mr1-` is counted 3, and a lone `mr1` keeps its own refusal, below). The refusal's "string 2" counts the strings as typed, from 0: typed in the card's order, string 2 is chunk 2, whose engraved length is at place 2 (from 0) of the kit line's runs, each run filling as many places as its count. Correction is `mr repair`'s job. A string that mixes upper and lower case is refused with its next step: ``the string mixes upper and lower case (BIP 173 forbids mixed case): type it again in one case (all lower case, as `mr create` prints it)``. The same holds for each string of an mk1 plate given with `--keys`, `--keys-file` or `--sibling-keys`, in every verb that reads one: a string that needs a correction is refused, ``key <k>, string <j> of <n>: the mk1 checksum does not verify: a character was typed or engraved wrong (`mk repair` corrects up to 4 substituted characters and names them); check what you typed against the plate, then type it again, or re-engrave the plate if the plate itself is wrong``, and one beyond correction ``… does not verify and cannot be corrected: more than 4 substitutions, or a character left out or added (which `mk repair` does not correct): count its characters and check each against the plate; `mk repair` corrects up to 4 substituted characters; string <j> has <N> characters, `mk1` included, spaces and hyphens not counted: count the engraved characters and compare them with the plate: each string of an mk1 plate but its last has 111``, one text for every string whatever order the strings were typed in (\<j\> counts them as typed; 111 is the length of every string `mk encode` prints for a plate but its last) |
| Network | `--network <NETWORK>` is global: `main` (the default; `mainnet` is accepted as an alias), `signet`, `testnet` or `regtest`. It picks the address format, `xpub` or `tpub`, the tip anchor and the coin type. Any other value is a usage error. A card on the path convention carries its purpose number (270028; its keys sit at `m/270028'/<coin>'/<32r + i>'`) but not its network: a run with no `--network` is a mainnet run, and `create` and `upgrade` mint mainnet cards. The one refusal for a missing `--network`: a hand-out or an import (`address`, `member` with or without `--for-spend`, and `export`) from a path-convention card that keys a seat by xpub (its Pubkeys TLV) is refused, exit 1, before any seat is filled (`export` refuses before it prints anything): ``this card keys seats @0, @1 by xpub, and neither the card nor its xpubs record a network: rerun with the network its kit notes name (`--network main`, `--network signet`, `--network testnet` or `--network regtest`)``. A card with explicit origin paths is not refused (the coin-type warning below covers it). A path-convention card whose purpose number is not 270028 is refused as undecodable: ``purpose number <v>: this version's cards carry 270028; a card a pre-release build minted at the provisional purpose number 499' reads this way, so this card is one of those, or is damaged: re-mint it with this release: `mr create` mints a new wallet on this release's paths, so move any funds with the old build first; `mr upgrade` of its (0, 0) md1 keeps that one wallet's own paths and keys, so coins at its (0, 0) addresses stay; move any coins at the card's other members with the old build first`` (a card a pre-release build minted at the provisional `499'` has no purpose field, so its bits read as another number, `0` or `1`, or as a non-minimal varint, which reads ``the header's purpose number varint is not minimal; <the same sentence>``, or a header that ends inside the field, which reads ``the card header ends inside its purpose number field; <the same sentence>`` in every verb; `mr repair` of such a string prints the same sentence and does not send you to check the engraving; when repair changed characters it keeps the caveat, ``: `mr repair` corrects up to 4 substitutions per string, and a string with more errors can be corrected to another valid string``). A card with explicit origin paths (an upgraded md1's) may record a coin type there (each path's second step `0'` or `1'`); read on a `--network` of the other coin type, given or the default, it is read on that network all the same, with a warning: ``warning: this card's origin paths carry coin type 1' (signet, testnet and regtest), but --network is main: the card is read on main, as given; if the wallet is on signet, testnet or regtest, rerun with …`` (with no `--network`, ``--network is main (the default): the card is read on main; …``); in a run of several cards `export` tags it with the card, as it does its other card-level warnings: ``warning: card 1 [5bdd396d]: this card's origin paths carry coin type 0' (mainnet), but --network is signet: …``, once per card. Such a step need not be a coin type (Core's `m/0'/0'/k'`), and read-side verbs work on any network; `upgrade` warns the same way when the md1's paths carry the other coin type, so a mainnet-path md1 upgraded on signet reads back on signet. A plate at a seat's path under the other coin type is set aside naming the network (``plate at m/270028'/1'/0': no seat of this card with `--network main`; it is seat @0's path on a test network: rerun with …``), and a seat left empty by it is refused the same way (`seat @0, letter A: no seed and no plate given; plate 1 at … is this seat's path on a test network: rerun with …`); the plate's own set-aside line is then folded into that seat's note, which names it, and is not printed beside it. When every set-aside plate is at a seat's path under the other coin type, the run also warns ``warning: these plates are for mainnet; rerun with `--network main` `` (for a test network's plates: ``… rerun with `--network signet`, `--network testnet` or `--network regtest` ``). `export` and `member --for-spend` give the same other-network sentence for a seat left empty by such a plate, not "perhaps a plate of an earlier set"; `export` keeps it in the seat's line on stdout and in the heir file too, not on stderr alone, so a saved stdout carries it (``…, plate 1 at m/270028'/1'/0' is this seat's path on a test network: rerun with `--network signet`, …``). Beside the ``these plates are for …`` warning, every verb that prints it (`export`, `verify`, `member` with or without `--for-spend`, and `address`) prints the rerun once: the seats' notes, refusal and warnings on stderr (`export`'s seat warnings and the stderr copy of its stdout line, the refusals of `verify`, `member` and `address`) end at ``… is this seat's path on a test network``, while `export`'s stdout line keeps the tail. When other keys given are at the run's network, a rerun with another `--network` would break them, so the odd key is named and the flag kept: ``warning: this plate is for mainnet, while the other keys are on a test network: keep `--network` as it is, and export it again at the seat's path on a test network`` (``these plates``, ``this record``, ``these records`` or ``these keys`` as they are; the reverse for a test network's key on a mainnet run), its set-aside line ends ``; it is seat @1's path on mainnet, while the other keys are on a test network: keep `--network` as it is``, and the empty seat's refusal ends ``…, and export this seat's key again at m/270028'/1'/1'``. `create` leads with ``every key is at its seat's path on mainnet: …`` only when no key is at the run's network; with a mix, the odd key's line ends ``; it is this seat's path on mainnet, while the other keys are on a test network: keep `--network` as it is``. When `--network` was not given (mainnet by default), or the odd keys outnumber those at the run's network, the run's network may be the wrong one, and each line that names the odd keys (the note, the refusal, `export`'s per-seat earlier-set warnings and each of `create`'s odd-key lines) ends with the other remedy: ``; or, if this wallet is on a test network, rerun with `--network signet`, `--network testnet` or `--network regtest` and export the other keys again there`` (on a test-network run, ``; or, if this wallet is on mainnet, rerun with `--network main` and export the other keys again there``); the per-key set-aside line keeps its one remedy. `units` reads a card on any network |
| Miniscript text | Every template, member, policy and descriptor `mr` prints spells md1's constants as rust-miniscript does, so that a Coldcard (whose parser has no bare `0` or `1`) and Bitcoin Core both read it: `and_v(X,1)` as `t:X`, its letter joined to the wrapper letters (`tv:older(144)`), `or_i(0,X)` as `l:X` (`sln:older(144)`), `or_i(X,0)` as `u:X`, and `andor(X,Y,0)` as `and_n(X,Y)`. A constant that no such form covers (`andor(X,0,Z)`) prints bare, and `create` and `upgrade` warn on it (see `mr create`). A member whose row adds a lock (`add-wait`, `add-deadline`) is written in its cheapest form, the lock joined to the path it guards: `andor(pk(@0),older(3),…)`, not `andor(pk(@0),tv:older(3),…)`, and `and_v(v:pk(@0),older(144))` where the path ends in a signature check. That written form is the member that `member`, `address`, `export`, `verify` and the recovery steps print and derive. The guardrails judge the row on its checked form, which has the same paths and locks; a member whose checked form fails its type or sanity check is not rewritten, and keeps its checked script. If the written form ever failed its type or sanity check where the checked form passes, that would be a bug in `mr`, never a refusal of the row. `mr` runs that check once per slot (types and sanity, the script limits aside, do not depend on lock values), so such a failure names the slot: `create` and `upgrade` exit 1, issuing nothing, with ``mr: internal error (a bug): the written form of every valid member of slot <s> fails its type or sanity check; please report it``; `export` (`--try-seatings` included) and `verify` skip every member of that slot and derive the other slots, and `export`, `verify` and `decode` print the same line on stderr as ``note: internal error (a bug): the written form of every valid member of slot <s> fails its type or sanity check; please report it``, and `verify` then exits 1; `member` (with or without `--for-spend`) and `address` exit 1 with the line for the member asked for, ``mr: internal error (a bug): the written form of the member at epoch <e>, slot <s> fails its type or sanity check; please report it``. `mr units` and `decode` mark an operation whose written form would fail, as a row's only operation, ``internal error (a bug): its written form fails its type or sanity check; please report it``, and list the other units as before |
| Tip | The tool never reads the machine's clock. Every verb that measures a deadline takes the chain tip as `--height H --time T` (the median time past, unix seconds; `--time` takes any u32) and prints `tip = <height>, ≈ <date>; this must be the current tip` first. On mainnet a tip whose height and time disagree with the release's anchor by more than 180 days is refused unless `--accept-tip`; off mainnet it only warns. On testnet (testnet3, whose min-difficulty blocks decouple height from time, and whose anchor is its genesis block) the check gives one line in place of that text, ``warning: test chains do not keep 10-minute blocks: the tip check and the dates are rough; check the tip by hand``, with no release advice and no `--accept-tip`. The line is printed on every run on a real testnet3 tip, since the anchor is the 2011 genesis block and a current tip is always years off it (only a tip within 180 days of the genesis block, which no card minted by this release can carry, draws nothing more than the tip line); `--accept-tip` is accepted there and changes nothing, because the check never refuses off mainnet and there is nothing to override. Signet keeps the check: its signed blocks are targeted at 10 minutes and the §7a rehearsal is built on its anchor, but whether a live signet tip stays within 180 days of that extrapolation was not checked against a node, so a signet tip may draw the mainnet text. When the tip's time is before the anchor's the second line reads ``the tip's time is N days before this release's anchor (2024-04-20): check `--time` `` in place of the anchor's age. On every network a tip no chain can have is refused, with no override: a height of 500,000,000 or more (`tip height … is not a block height: …`; from 500,000,000 an nLockTime is a unix time) or a time before the network's genesis block (`tip time … is before this network's genesis block …`), each naming `--height` and `--time`, since a swapped pair gives one; `export` only warns, and dates no height from such a tip (`warning: heights are printed without dates: this tip is not one a chain can have, so no date is estimated from it`). `member --for-spend` runs no tip check, so recovery is never blocked by it: it prints only the tip line. `export` has no `--accept-tip`, so its warning leaves out the advice to pass it |
| Numbers | Printed numbers carry thousands separators (`1,260,000`), except in script text, command lines, flag values, indexes, epochs and hex ids |
| Streams | Results go to stdout; warnings go to stderr as `warning: …`; errors print as `mr: <message>`. A reader that closes the pipe early (`mr decode --network testnet … \| head -1`) only stops the output: the exit code is the verb's own. Any other write error on stdout (a full disk, `> /dev/full`), from a verb or `--help`, prints `mr: cannot write the output: <error>` and exits 1. A stdout closed outright (`>&-`) behaves as `> /dev/null`: the output is discarded and the exit code is the verb's own |
| Printed placeholders | A placeholder `mr` prints in its own text (`MR1_CARD`, `THEIR_OWN_SEED`, `THEIR_KIT_MK1_PLATES`, `THEIR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS`, `EXECUTOR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS`, `HEIGHT`, `TIME`, `EPOCH`, `SLOT`, `INDEX`, `ADDRESS`, `LEAD`, `DESCRIPTOR`, `CARD`, and any other word of capitals, digits and `_` that holds a `_`; and the single letters `mr` prints for a number, `H`, `T`, `E`, `S` and `I`, each only as the whole value of the flag it is printed for: `--height H`, `--time T`, `--epoch E`, `--slot S`, `--index I`), pasted unchanged into the card (any of its strings), `--seeds`, `--keys`, `--lead`, `--funded-address`, `--witness-descriptor` or a numeric flag (`--height`, `--time`, `--epoch`, `--slot`, `--index`), is named and never read as input: ``mr: `MR1_CARD` is a placeholder from `mr`'s printed text: put the card's mr1 strings (a chunked card's strings in one pair of quotes) there``; for a seed ``… `THEIR_OWN_SEED` is a placeholder from `mr`'s printed text: put your seed's words or its ms1 plate, in quotes there``; for a key, ``… put the mk1 plates or `[fingerprint/path]xpub` records, each in quotes there``; for a seat-check placeholder (`THEIR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS`, `EXECUTOR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS`) wherever it is pasted (in place of a key, after `--seeds`, or in the card position), ``… `THEIR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS` is a placeholder from `mr`'s printed text: in its place put the `--seat-check` arguments the kit notes or bag sheet give, each with its flag, or nothing when they give none``, since it stands for those flags, not for keys; for a file name (`--seat-lines` and `--keys-file`), ``… `THEIR_KIT_SEAT_LINES` is a placeholder from `mr`'s printed text: put the name of a file holding your kit's "seat key:" lines there`` (``… put the name of a file holding the mk1 plates or records there``), unless a file of that name exists, which is read; for a number, ``… put the number there`` (a usage error, exit 2; the card, seed and key refusals are exit 1). A bare listed word is a placeholder in `--seeds` only as that flag's lone value (seed words in capitals, unquoted, may include one, and get the split-words text instead); a placeholder with a `_` is named anywhere. No placeholder is a valid input, so no valid value is refused (an upper-case ms1, mk1 or mr1 string, seed words and numbers all pass), and a number out of range keeps its range text (`not in 0 … N`) |
| Secrets | `mr` never prints a private key, entropy or an ms1 seed string. It prints seed words in one case only: for each seed that `--seeds` gives as BIP-39 words, stderr carries `seed <n> read as <language>: <words>` once, so the words can be checked against the backup. Treat stderr as secret whenever `--seeds` holds words. A card argument that does not start `mr1` is never echoed: seed words (spaced or comma-joined), an ms1 string, an mk1 string or an md1 string is refused by every verb but `units`, which reads an md1 card (below), naming only what it looks like, e.g. `a card argument looks like seed words (<n> words, not echoed): seeds go in --seeds, and a card is its mr1 strings`. `member`, `address` and `current` refuse any other non-`mr1` value the same way, quoting only a bare integer of at most 20 digits (the `--epoch`/`--slot` hint); `units` reads a non-`mr1` argument as a template: one with no `(` is never a template and is never quoted, only named by its kind (an extended private key, a WIF or base58 key, hex, seed words joined by any punctuation, or a bare word), and a template's parse error names a bad part by its character, never quoting it. A usage error never echoes a value at all (a phrase typed without its verb, as a flag's value, or as the `<lang>` of `--seed-language 1=<lang>`): it names the value by its position, and keeps the verb and flag names as they are. The command line itself (shell history, the process list) is outside `mr`'s reach |
| Shapes | A card holds 1 … 8 *shapes* (fold I; a card made without `--shapes` holds one): each a complete wallet set, with its own template (typed in card seats), slot table, deltas, anchors and own-wallet marks, while all shapes share one seat list, one seat map, one bundle list, one set id and one digest. `member`, `address`, `current` and `units` take `--shape <k>` (0 … K − 1, default 0); a shape past the last is a usage error (exit 2) that lists each shape, ``this card has 3 shapes: give `--shape` 0 … 2`` and one line per shape, ``shape 0 (id 3f2a9c01): seats @0, @1``. Every verb names a shape by its *display id*, ``shape k (id 3f2a9c01)`` (the first 32 bits of a hash of the shape's fields): the `member` line ends ``; shape 0 (id 3f2a9c01)``, the `address` line reads ``address (receive, index 0, shape 0 (id 3f2a9c01)): ADDRESS`` (the address its last token), and `decode`, `current` and `units` print the label on a line of its own. On a card of two or more shapes every other stdout line of those verbs names its shape too (a line that does not carry the label begins `shape k:`), and every `member`, `address` or `current` command that `create`, a kit or a refusal prints carries `--shape k`. `export` scans every shape's members, and `verify`'s checks and warnings run on every shape. A shape's plate stub is its own: each seat's mk1 plate is minted with the distinct stubs of the shapes that use the seat, so its `mk encode` line carries one `--policy-id-stub` for each (`decode` lists every shape's stub when they differ). Shape 0's stub is the (0, 0) md1's, so upgraded plates keep matching; a shape k ≥ 1's stub matches no md1 `mr` hands out. A card of several shapes is always chunked |
| Root mode | `mr root` makes a root from coin flips, dice rolls or pill draws and `mr child` re-finds its children, both on the offline computer; `create --root -` makes a new seed set's seeds from roots ("Root mode" under `mr create`). A root is typed only at a prompt on stdin; `mr` refuses it, and a root's passphrase, as an argument. The root is read line by line to the first empty line (its 24 words on one line or several, or its ms1 string, whitespace between lines ignored); every check that needs only the root runs next; only then are the passphrase and its repeat asked, one line each, so a refused root asks none. A line of the root is never read as a passphrase. Type them at the prompts, never pipe them from `printf` or `echo`, which puts them in the shell's history; a piped run (a test) gives `ROOT`, an empty line, `PASS`, `PASS`. Every secret (a root's words, a child's words, an ms1 string to engrave) and every root fingerprint goes to stderr only, never stdout; a root fingerprint is screen-only, never written down, since beside a plate it shows whether a passphrase exists. A root is made with letter A and a mask of the letters B … P it makes; a published test root is refused on every network. The texts are under "Root-mode texts", the procedures under "Root-mode procedures" |

### Exit codes

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `0` | success |
| `1` | refused or failed (a guardrail, a check, a key that does not fit; a value `mr` itself refuses after the parser has accepted it: a card string that does not decode, a slot the card lacks, an unreadable `--keys-file`, a `--template-preset` name, a value of a hot-family flag of the wrong form (`--hot-child`, `--first-hot-child`, `--retire-child`, `--bundle-account`, `--hot-on`, `--compromised`), a `verify --against` value that is not an md1 card; also a mandatory input `mr` checks itself and finds missing: `--delta-blocks`/`--delta-seconds`, `--anchor-height`/`--anchor-time`, a participant's `--role`, a `--sibling` answer, `--slots` or `--preset`) |
| `2` | usage error: one the argument parser finds (an unknown flag, a missing argument it requires, a value the parser itself finds of the wrong form or outside a flag's fixed choices, such as `--network bogus`, `--height abc`, a placeholder pasted into a numeric flag (`--height HEIGHT`) or an `--index` of `address` or `upgrade` above 2,147,483,647 (``not in 0 … 2,147,483,647 (an address index is an unhardened BIP 32 index)``); the hot-family flags above take free text, so their values are `mr`'s own check, exit 1), and the spec's closed list (§7 "Input and output"): no keys or seeds for a seat the Pubkeys TLV does not key, a malformed or repeated `@i=` binding, `verify --against` with an empty value, `--seed-language` or `--accept-seed-language` without `--seeds`, `create` or `upgrade` given no tip, and a seed, ms1, mk1 or md1 string typed where a card goes; also a missing tip in `member`, `address` or `current`; `mr repair`: nothing could be corrected |
| `5` | `mr repair` only: the strings were corrected |

A usage error's `Usage:` line is the verb's own, as `--help` prints it (`Usage: mr verify [OPTIONS] [mr1]...`), never clap's list of every flag the run gave as if each were required.

### Keys and seeds

`verify`, `member` and `address` take the set's keys in one of these
forms. On a card whose Pubkeys TLV keys every seat, `member`, `address`
and `export` need neither `--keys` nor `--seeds`: every key comes from
the card (`member --as policy` still needs a seed or plate for a seat,
since a signer registers only a key it holds). On a card with a seat it
does not key, `member` or `address` given neither is a usage error
(exit 2), and `export` refuses it (exit 1).

| Form | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `--keys <mk1…>` | an mk1 plate (a chunked plate's strings in one value, separated by spaces), typed as `mk encode` prints it and a plate engraves it or without its groups: each string's groups of characters separated by spaces, `-` or `,` (`mk1qp gylyp qqsqs …`) are joined to the string they follow, as mk-cli strips them; a value of groups alone (a grouped plate the shell split, typed unquoted) continues the string before it; and a chunked plate's strings given as values one after another (as the recipe's `--keys MK1_PLATES` invites), in any order, are one key while they are chunks of one set. A group is bech32 characters only, never a `1`, so an ms1 or mr1 string, a record or a word is never joined to a plate. A word after a complete plate is its own value, however its letters read: a string that already verifies takes no more groups, so `dave`, or the `seat` that an unquoted seat-key line begins with, is refused by itself (as seed words, or as a seat-key line to quote) and the plate before it reads. A lone value of 8 hex digits is refused as a fingerprint cut from its record: ``this value is 8 hex digits, a fingerprint on its own (not echoed): `--keys` takes mk1 plates or `[fingerprint/path]xpub` records, with the fingerprint inside the brackets``; a lone word as seed words, or a stray group of a plate: ``this value looks like seed words (1 word, not echoed): seeds go in `--seeds`, and `--keys` takes mk1 plates or `[fingerprint/path]xpub` records; if it is a stray group of an mk1 plate, give the whole plate, in quotes`` (on a `--keys-file` line, ``…, give the whole plate, its groups joined on one line``). A character outside the codex32 alphabet is refused with its string and its place counting `mk1`, from 1, with the plate's spaces and hyphens not counted: ``key 1, string 2 of 2: character 9 (counting `mk1`, not counting spaces or hyphens) is not in the codex32 alphabet (qpzry9x8gf2tvdw0s3jn54khce6mua7l; `b`, `i`, `o` and a `1` after the `mk1` never occur): check it against the plate``. A string of a chunked plate given without the plate's other strings is refused naming it: ``key <k>: string 1 of a 2-string mk1 plate, given without the plate's other strings: give every string of the plate: in `<flag>`, in one quoted value or as values one after another; in `--keys-file`, on the lines that follow it``, the `--keys-file` clause only when `<flag>` is `--keys` (`--sibling-keys` has no file). Every key text a value can draw names `<flag>`, the flag the value was given in: `--sibling-keys` for a value given there, `--keys` otherwise, a `--keys-file` line included |
| `--keys "[fp/path]xpub"` | a record: a key in `verify`; double-quote it (or write the path's `'` as `h`, still quoted in zsh, whose `[…]` is a glob): an unquoted `'` opens a shell quote in bash, zsh, sh and fish, so two records merge and lose their `'`s, which `mr` refuses with ``…; if a shell merged records, quote each record, or write the path with h: an unquoted `'` opens a shell quote (`<flag> "@0=[fp/270028'/1'/0']xpub"`, or `@0=[fp/270028h/1h/0h]xpub`)``; the record's xpub must be at its path, its depth the path's length and its child number the path's last step (as `mk encode` requires), in every verb that reads one: ``key <k>: the record's xpub is at depth 3, child 0', not at m/270028'/1'/1' (depth 3, child 1'): the device exported another path; export the key at m/270028'/1'/1'``, so a record whose `'`s a shell stripped (an even number typed unquoted) is refused ``key <k>: the record's path has no hardened step, but its xpub's last step is hardened: if a shell stripped its `'`s, quote each record, or write the path with h: an unquoted `'` opens a shell quote (`<flag> "@0=[fp/270028'/1'/0']xpub"`, or `@0=[fp/270028h/1h/0h]xpub`)``, or, for a `--keys-file` line, which no shell touched, ``…: a `--keys-file` line is read as written: write each hardened step with h or `'` ``; a key as `mr` prints a seat no seed or plate gave (`member --as policy`'s foreign key, depth 0 with no parent) is no device's export and is refused naming that: ``key <k>: the record's xpub is at depth 0 with no parent, not at <path>: a key as `mr` prints a seat no seed or plate gave (a foreign key, spec §7 `member`), not a device's export; give the key its device exports at <path>, or its plate``; a record whose xpub does sit at a path with no hardened step is read and warned of (every Refugium path `m/Pr'/coin'/(32·r + i)'` is hardened): ``warning: `--keys` value 1: key 1: the record's path has no hardened step, unlike every Refugium path `m/Pr'/coin'/(32·r + i)'` (spec §3); if a shell stripped its `'`s, quote each record, or write the path with h: an unquoted `'` opens a shell quote (`<flag> "@0=[fp/270028'/1'/0']xpub"`, or `@0=[fp/270028h/1h/0h]xpub`)``, or, for a `--keys-file` line, ``warning: `--keys-file` line 1: key 1: the record's path has no hardened step, unlike every Refugium path `m/Pr'/coin'/(32·r + i)'` (spec §3); a `--keys-file` line is read as written: if the path should be hardened, write each hardened step with h or `'` ``; a record that does not parse is refused naming the fault, never quoting it: ``…: the xpub is followed by a derivation suffix (such as `/<0;1>/*` or `/0/*`): give the account key alone``, `` …: the path begins with `m/` inside the brackets: write the steps alone, `[fingerprint/84h/0h/0h]` ``; an origin path where the master fingerprint goes (`[m/270028'/1'/3']`, `[270028'/1'/3']`) is refused naming the form that reads: ``…: the brackets begin with an origin path, not the master fingerprint: write `[FINGERPRINT/270028'/1'/3']`, or `[00000000/270028'/1'/3']` where the seat-key line gives no fingerprint (a card made with `--no-fingerprints`)``; in `member` and `address` it only confirms a seat that a seed supplies, and at a seat the card's Pubkeys TLV keys, a record that carries that seat's fingerprint or sits at a path no other seat shares is checked against the card's xpub there as a bound one is: one that derives another key is refused with the bound form's text below, and one with the card's key but another fingerprint ``…: conflicting fingerprint``, as is a record of the key a seed fills a seat with, at that seat's path, that carries another fingerprint than the card declares for it, and one of the key a plate fills a seat with, on a card that keys a seat at that path (it is not set aside as "no seat of this card", nor confirmed silently; on a card that keys no seat at that path, a record that only repeats a plate's key is passed without a word, since the plate already fills the seat); a record at a path several seats share (every keyed `upgrade` card) that is neither the card's key nor carries that seat's fingerprint is only named, ``record at m/48'/1'/0'/2': no seat of this card``, and the card's key is used; `member --for-spend` refuses one |
| `--keys "@i=<key>"` | the key bound to seat `@i`, in the card's stored numbering (double-quoted, as a record above). At a seat the card's Pubkeys TLV keys (every `upgrade` of a keyed md1), a bound key that is the card's xpub there (the same key and chain code) confirms the seat in every verb, so the `--keys "@0=…" "@1=…"` line `create` and `upgrade` print passes `verify`; any other key is refused in `verify`, `member` and `address`: ``seat <i>: the card carries its xpub and <plate k \| record k> derives another: a wrong plate, a wrong seed or a wrong passphrase; drop it or fix it`` |
| `--keys-file <file>` | one key per line (`@i=KEY` or the key alone), a chunked plate's strings on consecutive lines, each string in its groups or not, as `mk encode` prints it (a line of groups alone continues the string above it, so a plate copied row by row from its engraving reads, as `--keys` values do: a group of 1 or 2 characters on a line of its own too, the next string of a plate starting with its `m`, `k`, `1`); blank lines and `#` comments are skipped; a plate ends at its chunk count, and a plate's first string always starts a new one, so a string that does not verify is named within its own plate (`key <k>, string <j> of <n>`), its chunk header read as typed when the string is beyond correction (unless a header character itself is wrong, a plate whose every string is beyond correction no longer draws in the next plate's); strings that all verify and belong to two plates are read as two keys, so a plate missing a string is named as the values name it (``string 1 of a 2-string mk1 plate, given without the plate's other strings``) |
| `--seeds <seeds>` | ms1 plates or BIP-39 words, seed 1 first: a seed's words separated by spaces, seeds separated by commas (12 or more one-word seeds in a row, each a BIP-39 list word, are refused as one seed's words: joined by commas in one command-line word, ``seeds <a> to <b> are one word each, every one a BIP-39 list word: these look like one seed's words joined by commas; separate a seed's words with spaces, and seeds with commas``; or, when they arrived as separate command-line words (an unquoted phrase), ``seeds <a> to <b> are one word each, every one a BIP-39 list word, and arrived as separate command-line words: the shell split one seed's words; quote each seed's words as one value (`--seeds "SEED_WORDS"`, seeds separated by commas inside the quotes)``). A key or a card given as a seed is named by kind (``seed <n>: this value is an mk1 plate: keys go in `--keys`, and `--seeds` takes ms1 plates or seed words``), as seed words or an ms1 string given as a key are (``key <k>: this value looks like seed words (<n> words, not echoed): seeds go in `--seeds`, and `<flag>` takes mk1 plates or `[fingerprint/path]xpub` records``, `<flag>` the flag the value was given in, `--keys` or `--sibling-keys`). An ms1 plate may be typed as engraved: its groups separated by spaces or hyphens (`ms encode`'s engraving card), or by commas (`--separator comma`: each piece after an ms1 piece that is a run of bech32 characters with no space is read as one of its groups, until the pieces spell the next plate's `ms1`), at any group size, 1 and 2 included, all lower or all upper case (BIP 93: a plate that mixes them is refused ``seed N: the plate mixes upper and lower case (BIP 93: a codex32 string is entirely one or the other): type it again in one case: all lower case, as `ms encode` prints it``); a refusal never quotes it; a plate whose checksum fails is refused ``seed N: the ms1 plate does not verify: check it character by character against its engraving; `ms repair --ms1` corrects substitutions`` |

Reading the values: a plate of another set whose origin path is no seat's
is set aside and printed (``plate at m/270028'/1'/32': no seat of this card``),
never a stub mismatch (a record at no seat's path is set aside the same way in `member` and `address`: ``record at m/270028'/1'/97': no seat of this card``; the line is dropped in `member`, `address`, `member --for-spend` and `export` for a key the missing seat's earlier-set note names, and the network note says "this record is" / "these records are" / "these keys are" for non-plates, "these plates are" for plates); a plate at a seat's path with another card's stub is
a mismatch, and the remedy counts its plates (``re-mint that plate`` / ``re-mint
those plates``). A plate in groups of 2 or 1 characters (`mk-1q-pl-…`,
`m k 1 q p …`; typed unquoted, each group is a value of its own, `mk 1q px …`
or `m k 1 q p …`, and the groups `m` or `mk` after a complete string start the
plate's next string; a plate bound `@0=mk 1q px …` is read the same, in `create` and `upgrade` as in `verify`), a keys file with a plate's string 2 before its string 1, and
strings joined by `-` or `,` are read (also a separator inside the last group of a
plate typed in groups: `mk1q pef8 … xxxx,mk1q pef8 …`); a damaged string 1 or 2 is named by its
checksum, also unquoted in groups of 1 or 2 (a `1` is never a data character, so the groups `m`, `k`, `1` or `mk`, `1…` always start the plate's next string, whether or not the string before it verifies; and a string that reads as a later chunk, index above 0, continues a plate with a string that fails its checksum while every header that plate's strings still read names the same chunk set and count: when the damaged string's header is intact, a plate damaged past correction reads ``key 1, string 1 of 4: the mk1 checksum does not verify …`` for a 4-string plate unquoted as quoted, and another plate's later chunk is not drawn in; when the damaged string's header is itself damaged, the "string j of n" count can be wrong, counting another plate's later chunk or leaving out the plate's own, while the plate is still refused). Several plates in one `--keys` value are refused by name (give each
plate its own value); a card given under `--seeds`, `--keys` or `--sibling-keys` is named
(``…this value is an mr1 or md1 card string: a card is given as the verb's card, not in `--seeds` ``, with `--keys` or `--sibling-keys` in place of `--seeds` for the flag the value came from); a card that is the first value of `--seeds`, `--keys`
or `--sibling-keys`, with no other card given, stays that flag's value, so
the run (`upgrade` included, its card an md1, and in the `--seeds=…`
spelling too) is refused as a usage error (exit 2) that says so: ``no
card was given: the value of `--seeds` is an mr1 or md1 card string (not
echoed), and a card is the verb's own argument, not a value of a flag: give it
before the flags, or after them with another value``. A seed one hex
digit off a card's declared fingerprint names the card's fingerprint as the
likely typo. A plate with no origin fingerprint for a card that declares one
is refused in `verify`, `member` and `address` alike, never with the card's
fingerprint offered for it, since the card's fingerprint is the only one the
user sees and stamping it on another wallet's key would pass that key off as
this seat's: ``…carries no origin fingerprint, and the card declares F for
seat @i: md cannot seat it, and it may be another wallet's plate; its seat
check at seat @i is sc:…; see "If a seat's key is in doubt" in `mr --help` `` (`verify`: ``plate k
carries no origin fingerprint, and this card declares fingerprints: md
cannot seat it, and it may be another wallet's plate; its seat check at
seat @i is sc:…; see "If a seat's key is in doubt" in `mr --help` ``), the seat check the plate's key
gives at the seat beside the run's other keys, printed as data for the
procedure's step 2 (one per seat where several share the plate's path;
``its seat check at seat @i needs every seat's key: rerun with seat @j's
plate or seed`` where the run holds no key for seat @j); `export` and
`member --for-spend` warn. (One
exception: a fingerprint-less plate given beside plates that already fill every
seat at its path is a surplus plate, and is refused as ``surplus plate``, not
told to be re-minted.) A key
bound to the wrong seat names the seat that does declare its fingerprint
(on a keyed card, the keyed seat). `upgrade` and `repair` name a card whose
strings arrived as separate values and say how many strings the value holds
(give every string of a flag's card in one quoted value).

A seed of one of this card's seat letters must fill a seat or agree with
the plate, record or card key that fills it. It is checked against that
seat's plate or record even when a plate fills the seat, unless its master
fingerprint is another seat's, so a wrong seed or a wrong passphrase is
never passed in silence. `verify`, `member` and `address` refuse (exit 1):

- a seed that gives a filled seat another key: ``seat <i>: seed <n> and
  plate <k> give different keys: a wrong plate, a wrong seed or a wrong
  passphrase; drop one or fix it (`verify`, `member` and `address` take
  no passphrase yet: if seed <n> has one, leave it out, and plate <k>
  fills the seat)`` (`record <k>` and `a wrong record` when a device
  record fills the seat; on a card with fingerprints a parenthesis after "drop one
  or fix it" names the two: ``(seed <n> has fingerprint <fp>, not the card's
  <fp'> for seat @<i>; plate <k> carries <fp'>)``);
- a seed of a seat's letter that derives no seat's key: ``seed <n> (letter
  <L>, fp <fp>) derives no seat's key: a wrong seed, a wrong passphrase,
  or another card's seed of letter <L>? drop it or fix it, and verify each
  card with the seeds of its own seats (`verify`, `member` and `address`
  take no passphrase yet: if seed <n> has one, leave it out and give its
  seat's mk1 plate with `--keys`)``;
- on a card with fingerprints, a seat whose fingerprint no given seed has
  while a seed of its letter was given (a wrong seed, or the seat's seed
  without its passphrase): ``seat <i>, letter <L>: no given seed has its
  fingerprint; seed <n> has this letter but fp <fp>, not the card's <fp>:
  a wrong seed or a wrong passphrase? (`verify`, `member` and `address`
  take no passphrase yet: if seed <n> has one, leave it out and give seat
  <i>'s mk1 plate with `--keys`)``. The seed named is one of the seat's
  letter shown to fill no seat: no seat declares its fingerprint, its key
  is not a seat's (the card's xpub, a given plate or record, or the key
  the run already seated), it is not the hot child's parent (its child's
  fingerprint or key is the hot seat's, or the card gives nothing to
  check the child against) and the run has not already seated it. So a
  seed that fills another seat, the hot seat's parent included, is never
  named. When there is no such seed, or the card declares no fingerprint
  for the seat, the text ends at ``… has its fingerprint``;
- a seed of the hot entry's parent letter whose child is not the hot
  seat's (a wrong parent, or the right one without its passphrase):
  ``seed <n> (letter <L>, fp <fp>) has the hot entry's parent letter, but
  its child <len>/<i> gives no seat's key: a wrong parent, a wrong
  passphrase, another card's seed of letter <L>, or a card minted from
  another child's record? drop it or fix it, and verify each card with the
  seeds of its own seats (`verify`, `member` and `address` take no
  passphrase yet: if seed <n> has one, leave it out and give the hot
  child's own words in its place); for a card minted from another child's
  record, or when the child's words are refused too, run the parentage
  check of the creator's closing checklist (its `mr verify` line with
  `--hot-record <L>/<len>/<i>`, run with every flag that line prints,
  `--hot-parent-passphrase` and the history values among them, and with
  the hot child's records (the hot device's, or child <len>/<i>'s from seed <L>'s signer; the
  mint's transcript has them too, where it was kept)): it compares this
  card with child <len>/<i> of seed <L>, and a card that fails it is
  minted again``;
- a seed with a seat's fingerprint and another letter, a seat the card
  keys included: `card letter <M> for seat <i> is wrong: seed <n>, letter
  <I>, has its fingerprint; re-engrave the card before any deposit` (in
  `member` and `address`: `card letter <M> for seat <i>, seed <n> has
  letter <I> and this seat's fingerprint: the card's seat map is wrong
  for this seat; re-engrave the card before any deposit`);
- a seed that derives a seat the card gives another letter: `seed <n>
  (letter <L>, fp <fp>) derives seat <i>'s key, but the card gives seat
  <i> letter <M>: the card's seat map is wrong for this seat; re-engrave
  the card before any deposit`;
- a seed (or the hot seat's child) that derives a seat's key while its
  master fingerprint is not the one declared for the seat (the card's,
  or on a card without fingerprints the origin fingerprint of the plate
  or record that fills it; `00000000` is never called wrong): the
  fingerprint was mistyped when the card or plate was made, and signers,
  which match keys by fingerprint, would refuse the policy or not sign:
  ``seat @<i>: seed <n> derives its key, but its master fingerprint is
  <fp>, not the <fp'> declared for it: the fingerprint was mistyped;
  signers match keys by master fingerprint, so they would refuse this
  policy or not sign: re-mint the card and plates with fingerprint <fp>
  before any deposit (the seat's key record with [<fp>/…], and `mk encode
  --origin-fingerprint <fp>`)`` (``…: re-mint plate \<k\> with `mk encode
  --origin-fingerprint \<fp\>``` when a plate declares it). A seed is
  derived with its own passphrase, so a passphrase-protected seed given
  without its passphrase derives another key and is refused as a wrong
  seed or passphrase (above), never as a mistyped fingerprint;
- a `0x00` ms1 plate given without `--seed-language` whose English
  reading has no seat's known fingerprint (the card's, or a given plate's
  or record's) while its reading in another of the ten lists has one (an
  ms1 plate made before ms-codec 0.3.0 may hold any language's entropy):
  ``seed <n> matches seat <i> when read as <Language>: rerun with
  `--seed-language <n>=<language> --accept-seed-language <n>`; read in
  English, the default for a `0x00` ms1 plate, it is another seed (…)``.
  Only the list's name is printed. A plate given `--seed-language` (even
  `english`), typed words and `0x02` plates are never searched.

The hot parent warning ("seed \<n\> has letter \<L\> but its child … is not
this card's parent, or its passphrase is wrong") accompanies the refusal
about the hot parent (and the run in which no seed fills a seat); a
refusal about another cause (a seat missing, say) does not carry it, since
there it could name a seed that is the card's own right seed.

Another card's seed looks exactly like a wrong seed when it shares a
letter with one of this card's seats or with the hot entry's parent, so
those runs are refused as above: verify each card with the seeds of its
own seats.

Seating diagnostics (errata E53 batch N1). The refusals above name more
than they did:

- A missing seat is named with the plate that fills it: ``seat @<i>,
  letter <L>: no seed and no plate given: give seat @<i>'s seed with
  `--seeds` or its mk1 plate with `--keys`; kits that do not hold seat
  @<i>'s seed hold its mk1 plate; a set made without kits has an extra
  mk1 plate for seat @<i>, stored with another seed's ms1 plate (§1a): ask
  for a copy (an executor-mode heir's kit holds none: its executor's kit
  does)``. When the notes on that seat only name plates of seats a seed fills, the lead names the seat and the plate: ``seat @0, letter C: no seed and no plate of seat @0 given (plate 1 is seat @1's, which seed 1 fills too: seat @0's extra plate is labelled "seat @0, seed C"): give seat @0's seed …`` (with any other note the lead is "no seed and no plate of this set given"). That note is made only while some seat a seed fills has nothing given that agrees with it (a plate, a record, the card's xpub or another seed): when every such seat has one, the plates given are the right ones, and none is named. A missing hot seat is named for what it is, with its mk1 plate, which every kit that lists neither the parent seed nor `hot` holds (§5c's kit rule; an executor-mode heir's executor holds it for that heir):
  ``…: the hot seat is child 24/0 of seed A: give
  the hot child's own words, or its parent seed, if it has no passphrase (`mr` takes none here), with `--seeds`, or the hot seat's mk1 plate with `--keys`, which a kit that lists neither seed A nor `hot`
  holds (a child derived without the parent's passphrase, or with a wrong one, is another seed: re-derive child 24/0 from seed A with its passphrase, if it has one, at m/83696968'/39'/0'/24'/0')``; when the parent was given and its child 24/0 has another fingerprint than the card's, the lead is that: ``seat @0, letter C: seed 1 (letter A) was given, but its child 24/0 has fingerprint <fp>, not the card's <fp'>: another seed of letter A in seed 1's place, a passphrase on seed 1, another child index, or a card minted from another child's record: the hot seat is child 24/0 of seed A, which the given parent does not derive: give the hot child's own words with `--seeds`; for a card minted from the hot child's public record, or when the child's words are refused too, run the parentage check of the creator's closing checklist (its `mr verify` line with `--hot-record A/24/0`, run with every flag that line prints, `--hot-parent-passphrase` and the history values among them, and with the hot child's records (the hot device's, or child 24/0's from seed A's signer; the mint's transcript has them too, where it was kept)): it compares this card with child 24/0 of seed A, and a card that fails it is minted again …``; that check refuses a card minted from another child's record with ``hot child 24/0 of seed A does not match this card and its record: … mint again and engrave again``. When a record given in `member` or `address` sits at the missing seat's path, the refusal offers the plate minted from it only after the comparison, since nothing ties that record's key to this wallet: ``…, or, once its seat check matches the seat holder's (a plate minted from a record is only filled: nothing on this card ties its key to this wallet), mint it from the record: `mk encode --xpub <xpub> --origin-fingerprint <fp> --origin-path "m/…" --policy-id-stub <stub>` ``. The other
  missing seats follow in the same text (``seats @<j> (letter <M>) are
  missing too``) rather than one per run; a plate of an earlier set that
  names one of those other seats follows "missing too" (``(seat @1 (letter B)
  is missing too; plate 2 is a plate of an earlier set: …)``), never inside
  the first seat's sentence. When two or more missing seats, the hot seat
  not counted, each have a note on a key of theirs from one other round,
  all those keys of one round and all those seats at one round, the
  refusal leads with the round before the first seat's sentence: ``plate 1
  and plate 2 are round 0's, and this card's seats @0 and @1 are at round
  1: give this set's plates of those seats; seat @0, letter A: …``. The
  keys are named in the notes' order (``plate 1, record 2 and plate 3``,
  a seat with two such keys named once among the seats); the kind is
  "plates" when every such key is a plate, "records" when every one is a
  record, else "keys", and always "plates" in `member` and `address`,
  where a record only confirms a seed. One such seat gets no lead (its
  note says the same), and the hot seat is never among the seats, its
  own sentence asking for seeds. A key with notes at two seats (seats
  at one shared origin path, an upgraded md1's) is named once, and one
  key reads ``plate 1 is round 1's, …``. The lead counts the seats
  missing when the refusal is made, after any `@i=` binding of `member
  --for-spend`, and it stays when the refusal names a key of the other
  network at the first seat's path, as do the first seat's own notes, after its remedy, and the other missing seats. A plate or record given for
  another round of the seat (path last component `32 r + s`) is named in
  parentheses as "a plate of an earlier set" when its round is below the
  seat's and "a plate of a later set" when it is above it (``plate 1 is a
  plate of a later set: its path m/270028'/1'/33' is seat @1's in round 1,
  and this card's seat @1 is at round 0 (m/270028'/1'/1'); give this set's
  plate``). One carrying the seat's fingerprint at a path the card does
  not use is named ``… but its path m/… is none of this card's: another key
  of that seed, perhaps a plate of an earlier set; give this set's plate``
  (the seat's fingerprint, not a round, ties it to the seed). A plate or
  record at a path that is a seat's but for its purpose (`499'` in place of
  `270028'`), or carrying the seat's fingerprint at a `499'` path, is named
  for what it is: ``plate 1 is a plate of a pre-release build (purpose 499'):
  its path m/499'/1'/0' is seat @0's, but this release's paths begin 270028';
  re-mint it with the `mk encode` line `mr create` prints for this seat
  (m/270028'/1'/0'), and give that plate`` (a record, in `verify`: ``export the key again
  at <path> and give that record``; in `member` and `address`, where a record never supplies a key: ``re-mint this seat's plate with the `mk encode` line `mr create` prints for this seat (m/270028'/1'/1'), and give that plate (a record only confirms a seed here)``); when only the fingerprint matches, not
  the path, it reads ``… (purpose 499'): it carries seat @0's fingerprint at
  m/499'/1'/5', but this release's paths begin 270028'; re-mint it …``. A
  `499'` plate or record's set-aside line names it a pre-release one too, so
  one given when every seat is already filled by a seed, which no missing
  seat's line names, still reads ``plate at m/499'/1'/0': no seat of this
  card: its path is a pre-release build's (purpose 499'), and this release's
  path for seat @0 is m/270028'/1'/0': to keep a plate for seat @0, re-mint it
  with the `mk encode` line `mr create` prints for that seat`` (a record:
  ``record at m/499'/1'/1': no seat of this card: its path is a pre-release
  build's (purpose 499'), and this release's path for seat @1 is
  m/270028'/1'/1': to keep a record for seat @1, export the key again at that
  path``; at a path that past its purpose is no one seat's: ``plate at
  m/499'/1'/7': no seat of this card: its path is a pre-release build's
  (purpose 499'), and this release's paths begin 270028'``); a card any seat
  of which is not at purpose 270028' (an `upgrade`-issued card's explicit
  paths) gets the line without a tail. The lines of
  plates set aside are printed with the refusal. A record given beside the seat's seed is checked against the
  seat's path too: a record with the seed's own fingerprint but another
  key was exported at another path (``…have the same master fingerprint
  <fp> but give different keys: the record's key is not the key at seat
  @<i>'s path <path> …``); `member` and `address`, where a record only
  confirms, refuse such a record with the same text rather than pass it
  in silence; and `member` and `address` given a record
  whose fingerprint was mistyped use the mistyped-fingerprint text above,
  not "a wrong seed".
- A plate whose fingerprint no seat at its path declares is refused
  naming both and the likely causes, then the plate's seat check at the
  seat, as data, and the pointer to the procedure (``plate <k> at seat
  @<i>'s path <path> carries fingerprint <F>, but the card declares <C>
  for seat @<i>: most likely another wallet's plate, or the seat's seed
  exported without its passphrase; its seat check at seat @<i> is sc:…;
  see "If a seat's key is in doubt" in `mr --help` ``; ``record <k> …`` for a record); the seat
  check is the key's at that seat beside the run's other seats' keys, and
  when the run holds no key for another seat it reads ``its seat check
  needs every seat's key: rerun with seat @<j>'s plate or seed``. The
  procedure's step 2 compares it with the value the kit notes give, or
  with the seat check `mr verify --keys` prints for the holder's own
  export: key halves (the first 16 hex digits) that differ mean another
  seat's or wallet's key, values that match a mistyped fingerprint. Where
  several seats share the path it names the fingerprint each declares and
  the plate's seat check at each of those seats, then the pointer. On a
  card that keys its seats a plate that is none of their keys is
  refused, not passed. `create` names every key not at its seat's path,
  with the two paths and no rebind (see `mr create`). `export` and `member --for-spend` print the
  seed's fingerprint in the descriptors of a fingerprint-disputed seat
  (``\<fp\> (the seed's) is used in the descriptors, not \<fp\>, and the seat
  is fingerprint-disputed``). A `--for-spend` seed beside a plate that
  gives a different key adds "seed \<n\> signs nothing here: a wrong seed or
  a wrong passphrase?", and `export`'s warning adds the same with "(`export`
  takes no passphrase: leave such a seed out)". A key bound to the wrong
  keyed seat (`@0=` the plate of seat @1, on a card whose Pubkeys TLV keys
  both) is named by `verify`, `export` (its set-aside line, ending
  ``bind it `@1=`: set aside, not seated``) and `upgrade` (``… is exactly seat @1's key
  (the md1 card's xpub there): bind it `@1=`; `upgrade` keeps the md1's
  keys``) as exactly the other seat's key.
- A key bound `@i=` at another seat's path is named before any seat is reported missing, in every verb (`verify`, `member` and `address` said "no seed and no plate given" for seat i; `--for-spend` and `export` already named it): "`@2=`: plate 2 is at m/270028'/1'/1', not at seat @2's path m/270028'/1'/2'; it is seat @1's path: bind it `@1=`".
- `export` and `member --for-spend` take mk1 plates as keys and refuse a record (``record 1: `export` takes mk1 plates as keys; a `[fingerprint/path]xpub` record is a key only in `create`, `upgrade` and `verify`, and confirms a seed in `member` and `address`: give the seat's mk1 plate``). The ready `mk encode` line follows (``(`mk encode` mints it from the record, once its seat check matches the seat holder's: a plate minted from a record is only filled, and nothing on this card ties its key to this wallet): …``) only for a record at one of the card's seat paths: a plate minted at any other path would be refused in turn, and on a card without fingerprints the line ends `--privacy-preserving`, since its plates carry none. A record that is the key a given seed fills its seat with (a seed of that seat's letter or, on a card with fingerprints, of its fingerprint) is refused as such, ``record 1 is seat @1's key, which seed 2 already fills: drop record 1``, with no offer to mint. A record elsewhere gets a note in its place, ``: its path m/499'/1'/1' is a pre-release build's (purpose 499'), and this release's path for seat @1 is m/270028'/1'/1': export seat @1's key again at that path and mint its plate from that, the `mk encode` line `mr create` prints for the seat, and give that plate`` (``…, and this release's paths begin 270028': …`` when no one seat is meant), ``: its path m/270028'/1'/33' is seat @1's in round 1, and this card's seat @1 is at round 0 (m/270028'/1'/1'): a record of a later set; give this set's plate of seat @1`` ("an earlier" for a lower round), or ``: its path m/… is no seat's path of this card``.
- Where the seat's seed has a passphrase the read verbs cannot take, the
  text ends with the kit sentence above and the ready line to mint the
  plate, offered only after the comparison, since nothing ties the xpub
  of a record or plate given at the seat's path to this wallet: ``, or,
  once its seat check matches the seat holder's (a plate minted from a
  record is only filled: nothing on this card ties its key to this
  wallet), mint it: `mk encode --xpub <xpub> --origin-fingerprint <fp>
  --origin-path "m/…" --policy-id-stub <stub>` ``; a record where a key
  is needed is refused with the same `mk encode` command and the same compare
  clause. A seed matching a plate in
  more than one wordlist names every list.

On a card with fingerprints, `export` still tries a seed of a seat's
letter whose fingerprint no seat declares there, as a labelled candidate
(a recovery never blocks on a fingerprint): its warning adds ``seed <n>
has fp <fp>, not the card's <fp>: a wrong seed or a wrong passphrase?
(`export` takes no passphrase): if a seed has one, leave it out and give
seat @<i>'s mk1 plate with `--keys` ``, and the candidate's descriptor shows
the candidate's own fingerprint, never the card's. The seat is marked
on stdout and in the heir file too, one line per such seat: ``candidate:
seat @2, letter C: seed 1 (fp 7698a7e5) is tried there, not the card's
55b5e34b: a wrong seed or a wrong passphrase? (`export` takes no
passphrase)``.

`verify`, `member`, `address` and `export` take no BIP-39 passphrase yet
(`--passphrase` is a usage error there, and in `verify`'s card form clap's list of the
parentage form's required flags is followed by the note ``note: `verify`'s card form takes no passphrase; its parentage form (`--hot-record`) takes the hot child's parent's: leave a seed that has one out and give its seat's mk1 plate with `--keys`, and its device record``, the value never echoed; a prompt is a known open item),
so the right seed of a passphrase-protected seat derives other keys and
is refused as above. Leave such a seed out and confirm its seats by their
mk1 plates (in `verify`, also by their device records); for the hot seat,
give the hot child's own words in place of a passphrase-protected parent.
`create`'s closing checklist says so for each seed given with
`--passphrase`.

A seed of a letter that no seat of this card uses, other than the hot
entry's parent letter, is not refused. If the bundle list names its
letter (a Lightning seed, or a cheap wallet's parent), `verify`, `member`,
`address` and `member --for-spend` print `… fills no seat: the bundle list
names letter <L>; no seat of this card checks this seed` (`export` prints
no such line). Any other, such as another card's seed, is warned of in
every verb, `export` included: `seed <n> (letter <L>, fp <fp>) fills no
seat of this card: not checked here; verify it with its own card`. But
when seeds are given, none of them fills a seat or agrees with what fills
it, and at least one is not a seed the bundle list names, `verify`,
`member` and `address` refuse the run: `no given seed fills a seat of this
card or agrees with what fills it: a wrong seed, a wrong passphrase, or
another card's seed? verify each card with the seeds of its own seats`.
A run given only seeds the bundle list names, beside the plates, is not
refused. (The owner ruled for this reading on 2026-10-01, errata E50.)

On a card without fingerprints, a second seed of a keyed seat's letter
beside the one that derives the seat's key is only set aside, with its
own line (`seed <n>: seed <m> derives seat <i>'s xpub and is letter <L>'s
seed: set aside`), and does not fail `verify`.

Any warning prints before the refusal. `export` and `member --for-spend`
never refuse over a seed: they warn and keep it (`export` uses it for
`held` accounts). For a seed of a seat's letter that derives no seat's
key, `export` warns `… derives no seat's key: a wrong seed or a wrong
passphrase?` and `--for-spend` `… derives no seat's key: it signs
nothing here; a wrong seed or a wrong passphrase?`; a seat map engraved
wrong is warned of as `… the card's seat map is wrong for this seat; …`.
On a card without fingerprints `export` scans a disagreeing seed as one
more seating. A mistyped declared fingerprint (above) is a warning there
ending ``…: <fp'> (the seed's) is used in the descriptors, not <fp>, and the seat is fingerprint-disputed; re-mint
…`` (`member --for-spend` adds that the policy is offered for display
only). A `0x00` plate in another list (above) is warned of with the same
hint, ending ``…; this run reads seed <n> as <Language> (its seat is
filled and scanned so)``, with the language consent warning, and the run
reads it in that list (`--for-spend`'s printed rerun carries the
`--seed-language` flags); when two lists match, or that reading is
another given seed, it only warns.

### If a seat's key is in doubt

Where `mr` cannot prove which key a seat holds, or at which seat a key
belongs, its refusal (in `export` and `member --for-spend`, its warning)
names the seat or seats and the reason in one short sentence and ends
with ``see "If a seat's key is in doubt" in `mr --help` ``. It prints no
remedy of its own, no command line it fills in and no reading to choose
between, except where following its text literally cannot produce a wrong
card (a seed that disproves a declared fingerprint, which `upgrade`
answers with md's route filled in; a key that is exactly another seat's,
"bind it `@j=`"; a path one seat owns; the kit notes' re-mint check; every
`--seat-check` text). Every such refusal keeps its condition, its place in
the run's order and its exit code. `mr --help` prints the procedure after
its list of verbs, word for word as below:

```text
If a seat's key is in doubt

`mr` stopped because it cannot prove which key a seat holds. A
fingerprint, a letter, an `@j=` number or a seat map you type is not
evidence of a seat, so never change one of them just to get past the
stop. For each seat it names:

1. The seat's holder loads their own seed, with the passphrase the
   wallet uses, on their own signer (the seed itself; in root mode
   the root's child, loaded on your signer from the words `mr child
   … MR1_CARD` prints on the offline computer, never the root; a
   conventional BIP-85 child only for the hot seat) and exports the
   key at the seat's origin
   path: the path `mr`'s refusal names as the seat's ("this seat's
   path"), the role table prints, or `md inspect MD1_STRINGS` prints
   for an md1. That export, the `[fingerprint/path]xpub` record, is
   the seat's key and its master fingerprint. A path decides no key:
   take no key and no fingerprint for the seat from another card,
   plate, record or note, or from `mr`'s messages. An export made
   without the passphrase, or with a wrong one, is another key, and
   the holder's own `mr verify`, run the same way, passes it: in `mr
   upgrade` step 3's `pubkeys` check shows it, or at a seat the md1
   does not key the witness refuses it; in a first `mr create` nothing
   shows it, and the card is made for the key exported, so export it
   as the wallet will be used. A holder who cannot load the seat's
   seed (lost, or sealed for an heir) exports nothing. In `mr upgrade`
   of an md1 that keys every seat, step 3 encodes the md1 again with
   its own key there and no `--fingerprint` at all. On an md1 that
   does not key the seat, nothing replaces the export: the seat's
   plate fills it, and the witness checks it.
2. Compare the export with what the run used for the seat: the plate
   or record given, and in `mr upgrade` the md1's own key and
   fingerprint for the seat (`md inspect MD1_STRINGS` prints its
   fingerprint and origin path). Compare the keys, never xpub text:
   one key's xpub text differs from device to device and from md's (an
   xpub carries its parent's fingerprint, which md, not storing it,
   leaves 00000000). For an md1, the comparison is step 3's: encode it
   again with the export's xpub at this seat (at a seat whose holder
   cannot export, with the md1's own: nothing is compared there, the
   key being the md1's) and compare the `pubkeys` `md inspect --json`
   prints (at a seat the md1 does not key, only the fingerprint, the
   witness checking the key). For a plate or
   record, compare the seat check `mr` prints for it with the one `mr
   verify --keys` prints for the export. An heir who cannot ask the
   holder compares the plate's seat check, which `mr`'s refusal prints
   (or `mr verify --keys` beside the plate's seat where it is seated),
   with the `--seat-check` value the kit notes or bag sheet give. If
   their key halves (the first 16 hex digits) differ, the plate is not
   this seat's key: use the seat's plate from another kit, or its
   seed. If the values match, the plate holds the seat's key and only
   its fingerprint is missing or wrong: `mr export` and `mr member
   --for-spend` take it with a warning. Never mint a plate with the
   card's fingerprint.
3. Fix what differs, from the export (an heir without one stops at
   step 2's reading; an md1's seat whose holder cannot export takes
   the md1's own key, below), never by typing a value:
   - a plate or record: give the export in its place, or mint the mk1
     plate again from it with `mk encode --xpub XPUB
     --origin-fingerprint FP --origin-path "PATH" --policy-id-stub
     STUB`, XPUB and PATH the export's, FP the export's or the
     `00000000` the card declares for the seat, STUB the one `mr
     decode` prints for the card (on a card without fingerprints,
     `--privacy-preserving` in place of `--origin-fingerprint FP`);
   - an md1 whose fingerprint for the seat is missing or differs from
     the export's: encode the md1 again with md, `md encode "TEMPLATE"
     --key @i=XPUB_i --fingerprint @i=FP_i … --network NETWORK`,
     TEMPLATE the template `md inspect MD1_STRINGS` prints with each
     `@i` followed by its origin path without `m/`
     (`@0/48'/0'/0'/2'/<0;1>/*`), in double quotes, one `--key` for
     each seat the md1 keys, XPUB_i, for the seat being fixed, the
     xpub of its holder's export (where its holder cannot export, a
     lost or sealed seed, the md1's own, and then no `--fingerprint`
     at all), and for every other seat the md1 keys the md1's own as
     `md descriptor MD1_STRINGS --network NETWORK` prints it (on an
     md1 that keys only some seats, which md prints no xpub of, each
     such seat's holder's export; the check below proves each export
     is the md1's key), and each FP_i from that seat's holder's export
     (a declared `00000000` kept), or no `--fingerprint` at all. Then
     run `md inspect --json` on the old md1 and on the new: if their
     `pubkeys` differ in any entry, discard the new md1, since an xpub
     was not the md1's (for that seat, the next case applies); with
     the md1's own xpub at every seat they match by construction, and
     the new md1, given no `--fingerprint`, declares no fingerprints.
     When the `pubkeys` match, this changes no script and moves no
     funds;
   - an md1 whose key for the seat is not the export's: find whose
     export at that path it is, comparing keys as step 2 says (the
     `pubkeys` check above, with that holder's export at this seat).
     The seed whose export it is holds that seat (one seed in two
     seats takes one letter); a conventional (0 … 15) BIP-85 child's
     key at a cold seat cannot be kept (the hot/cold guardrail): move
     the funds with `mr
     create`; a key no holder's export gives is another wallet's: do
     not upgrade with this md1;
   - in `mr create`, a seat's holder is the one your template, as you
     typed it, puts there: bind each holder's export by that typed
     number (`--keys "@j=RECORD"`), exported at the path `mr`'s
     refusal names as that seat's path; a kit plate `mr` printed is
     bound by the typed number its kit line names ("seat @i (typed
     @j)"); a seat map gives one letter per stored seat, in the order
     `mr units` prints (a map from your kit notes is in that order
     already).
4. Rerun `mr` with each seat's key given as its holder's export, each
   record in double quotes, or with the holder's seed in `--seeds`,
   which also gives the seat's letter.
5. Before any deposit, check the card: each holder runs, with their
   own seed, the `mr verify` line the run's closing checklist or their
   kit notes give; a re-minted card must show the digest its kit
   notes give (`mr verify MR1_CARD --digest DIGEST`, never the digest
   the re-mint prints); and a first `mr create` is read against your
   own plan in its role table.
```

### Root-mode procedures

Fold I adds thirteen procedures to `mr --help`, after the seat-key procedure above and word for word as `mr --help` prints them. Every root-mode text of `mr` (below) ends in a pointer to one of them, ``see "NAME" in `mr --help` ``; the name is the first line of its block here. Section 9, "Recovery without mr", is also the no-`mr` sheet printed in the creator's kit notes, and the root-plate note's "also in the toolkit manual's `mr` chapter" means this section. Each procedure that types a root, a passphrase or a seed's words runs on the offline computer (the first procedure).

#### 1. The offline computer

```text
The offline computer

Everything that types a root, a root's passphrase or a seed's words runs
on the **offline computer**: `mr root`, `mr child`, `create --root -`, and
every closing-checklist line printed after "on the offline computer, in
the shell you started with history off … this line types seeds' words".
Never on your everyday computer, and never on a signer.

1. **Before typing a root or any words**, start a shell that keeps no
   history, as `mr` prints it: "on the offline computer, before typing a
   root or any words: start a shell that keeps no history (bash or zsh:
   `unset HISTFILE` as its first command; fish: start it as `fish
   --private`; dash keeps none), or use a live system with no persistent
   storage". Fish writes each command to its history file as it runs, so
   start the private shell *before* the first such command, never after.
   (The zsh and fish forms are owed a check, `mr-wipe-line-shells`.)
2. **Type secrets at the prompt.** The root (its 24 words or its ms1
   string), a root's passphrase and the draws of `mr root` are typed at
   `mr`'s prompts, never on the command line (`mr` refuses them there,
   but by then the shell has seen them) and never piped from `printf` or
   `echo`, which puts them in the shell's history. Each input ends with an
   empty line; the passphrase is asked only after the input is accepted.
3. **Where secrets appear.** `mr` prints every secret (a root's words, a
   child's words, every ms1 string `create` prints for you to engrave) and
   every root fingerprint on the screen's error stream (stderr) only. A
   saved report (`create … > report.txt`, or `| tee`) and the kit notes
   never hold them. A root fingerprint is screen-only: never write it
   down (beside a plate it shows whether a passphrase exists).
4. **Wiping**, as `mr` prints it (the wipe line): "wipe the offline
   computer: if its shell was not started with history off, close every
   shell first and only then delete its history file (bash writes the
   session back on exit); then delete every file this sitting wrote; a
   live system with no persistent storage is wiped by shutting it down".
   (Tested under bash 5: `unset HISTFILE` first leaves no history file;
   deleting the file before `exit` gets it rewritten.)
5. **When to wipe: once, at the end of the whole procedure, never in its
   middle.** A wipe in the middle deletes `create`'s report and the
   exported key records, or shuts a live system down, before later steps
   use them.
   - **A first card's sitting** ("The first sitting"): once, at its very end, after the
     card is engraved, the kit notes are printed, and the closing
     checklist and the destroy line are done (steps 8 and 9 of "The first sitting"). Every `mr child`
     run at the sitting (step 3, and the root check (`mr child --check`) at step 7) and every refusal
     during it says "do not wipe yet".
   - **A restore** ("Restoring from the root plate") or **recovery without mr** ("Recovery without mr"): once the words
     are loaded on their new signers and the printed words destroyed.
   - **A re-mint** ("Re-minting a lost card"): once its digest matches, never before.
   - **A rehearsal** ("Rehearsing the root"), a `mr child --check` run on its own: right
     after it, as `mr child --check` prints ("after this check on its own
     (the yearly rehearsal): now: <the wipe line>").
```

#### 2. Choosing a root passphrase

```text
Choosing a root passphrase

1. **Decide before the root is made** (sitting step 2), and give exactly
   the same passphrase, or none, at steps 3, 5 and 7 (`mr child`, `create
   --root -`, the root check (`mr child --check`)). A wrong passphrase gives a chain with the right letters
   and other keys, so a slip shows only as keys that do not match.
2. **What it costs.** Passphrases are hard to remember over years. Forget
   the root's and the root plate restores nothing: the seeds it made then
   live only on your signers (and heirs' plates), and are lost when those
   signers are. Nothing can recover it, not `mr`, not a plate, not a kit.
   Rehearse typing it every year ("Rehearsing the root").
3. **What it buys.** Only the bare root plate is deniable ("Where each item is stored"): a finder
```
