```text
   of the plate alone cannot tell which passphrase, or none, is meant. The
   plate together with a card copy, the first card's notes, a saved report
   or `mr child` output, the extra plates' seat checks, any other plate of
   a seed the root made, or a label carrying the set id, gives an offline
   test of every guess. Its strength then carries the protection.
4. **How to keep it.** Your secret alone: no heir needs it (the heirs'
   plates carry no passphrase), and with the root plate it spends every
   shape at once. `mr` writes it nowhere. Keep it apart from every plate
   and every kit, never in a bundle. Use ASCII characters only, or keep a
   note of how its characters are typed (the keyboard layout) **with the
   passphrase itself, never in a kit**; never write the passphrase or any
   part of it in a kit.
5. **The children carry none.** Every word set `mr child` prints is loaded
   with no passphrase and its keys exported with none. An heir who wants a
   passphrase brings an independent seed of their own, decided at
   sitting step 1, before the root is made.
6. **No checksum, no flag.** Nothing on the plate or the card records
   whether a passphrase exists. When a root does not match, `mr` says: "if
   this root was made with a passphrase, run again and type it; a mistyped
   one gives these same letters with other keys".
```

#### 3. The first sitting

```text
The first sitting

**Before the real one: rehearse on signet with a throwaway root only.**
Give every command of the rehearsal that takes `--network` (`mr child`,
`create`, the root check (`mr child --check`), the closing checklist's lines) `--network signet`: `create`
with no `--network` makes a mainnet card. "First card" is per network; a
signet root is never used on mainnet.

**A rehearsal engraves nothing.** Where a step below engraves (the root
plate, the card, the heirs' plates, the mk1 plates and extras), write the
string on paper instead, and type the root check (`mr child --check`) and the checklist's lines from that
paper. End the rehearsal before the real sitting: wipe every signer you
loaded with the rehearsal root's children (never a signer that holds
someone else's seed) and delete any hot wallet it set up; destroy every
paper and print it made (the throwaway root's words, every child's words,
its ms1 strings, card strings, kit notes, bag sheets and draw record) and,
if anything was engraved after all, every such plate (root plate, heir
plates, mk1 plates, extras, card: grind or cut through every character);
then wipe the offline computer ("The offline computer"). The real root plate is engraved only
at the real sitting, so no rehearsal plate, whose label would read word
for word as the real one's, ever lies beside it.

**What you need:** the offline computer ("The offline computer"), every signer, a sitting
signer for each heir whose seed the root makes, coins, dice or an Entropia
pill bag, paper, the plate tools, and every co-signer's and independent
heir's exported key.

1. **Settle the seat map.** Write down, for every seat, its letter and its
   holder: your letters, each heir's letter whose seed the root will make,
   and every co-signer's and independent heir's letter (their seeds exist
   already). The root makes only your letters and your heirs' (not a
   co-signer's). Keep this plan: steps 5 and 8 compare against it. The hot
   seat, if any, is `hot` in the seat map.
2. **Make the root.** Choose its passphrase or none ("Choosing a root passphrase"). On the offline
   computer, in the history-off shell, run `mr root --letters LETTERS
   --from coins|dice|pills`, LETTERS the letters the root makes (step 1).
   `mr` never draws randomness; it arranges yours:
   - **coins:** exactly 237 flips, H or T (or 1 or 0), in order.
   - **dice:** at least 92 rolls of a six-sided die, 99 recommended,
     digits 1 to 6.
   - **pills:** exactly 22 draws. One draw is one pill tossed: shake the
     bag, take one pill, toss it in your hand, type only the word facing
     up (never the other side), put it back. Typing both sides of one pill
     gives about 10 bits for two words (11 pills read on both sides give
     about 110 bits, below the 128-bit floor).
   Type the draws at the prompt over as many lines as you like, then an
   empty line. A biased coin, a draw without putting the pill back, or a
   side not chosen by the toss weakens the root; `mr` checks only for an
   all-equal input and for the spec's published example draws. Then type
   the passphrase twice (empty for none). Copy the 24 words onto paper.
   Compare later fingerprints with the "root fingerprint with this
   passphrase" it shows, on the screen only. **Do not engrave yet.**
   The hand procedure for ordinary seeds (word 1 from a block, a device
   completing word 24) is not used for a root: a device that completes word
   24 would load the root as its seed.
3. **Print the children's words.** `mr child --network NET`, the root's
   words typed at its prompt, then the same passphrase. Check that the
   root fingerprint it shows equals `mr root`'s and that the network is
   the one you mean. Copy each letter's words onto paper. Do not wipe.
4. **Load and export.** Load each child's words on its signer (a sitting
   signer for an heir's), **with no passphrase**, and export each key at
   its seat's path.
5. **Make the card.** On the offline computer, `mr create … --network NET
   --root - --keys @0=… --role … --seat-map … > report.txt`, NET the
   network of step 3 (with no `--network`, `create` makes a mainnet card),
   the root typed at its prompt, the same passphrase. `> report.txt` sends
   only `create`'s report (stdout: the card's strings, the kit notes and
   the closing checklist) to that file; never redirect stderr (no `2>`,
   `2>&1` or `&>`), where the prompts and every secret print, so they stay
   on the screen only. Steps 7 and 8 read the report from `report.txt`,
   which the wipe at the end of step 9 deletes. `create` checks every key against its child. If it
   refuses: "When create refuses a root". If you made the root on a signer by mistake, wipe it there
   before any deposit (`create` reminds you). Keep the command line you
   ran: a re-run at this sitting uses the same one ("When a day-0 check fails").
6. **Engrave the root plate**, only now: the ms1 string `create` printed
   for root N ("root N's plate: engrave this ms1 string: …", on the
   screen, never in a saved report). Label it as `create` prints.
7. **Check the plate.** `mr child --check --network NET MR1_CARD`,
   MR1_CARD the card's strings from `report.txt`, the plate's ms1 string
   typed at its prompt, the passphrase from memory.
   It must exit 0 (it prints no words). If not: "When a day-0 check fails". Do not wipe.
   **With two roots** (`--root -` twice), the two plates carry the same
   label and nothing else tells them apart: engrave them one at a time,
   and run the root check (`mr child --check`) on each just after engraving it, once per root plate; it
   prints "this root makes letters …", which must be the letters the
   notes list for that root. Add nothing to the labels; keep the plates
   apart, each at its own place, in the order the notes list the roots.
8. **Engrave and check the rest.** The card, from the strings in
   `report.txt` (`create`'s report, stdout, holds no secret), and in a root
   run a second copy for the extras' place; print the kit notes from the
   same file and keep them as "Where each item is stored" says: your kit's notes (the index line, the
   re-mint sheet, the digest and the "Recovery without mr" sheet among
   them) and each heir's kit notes and bag sheet. The heirs' plates, each
   from the ms1 string `create` printed for its kit; the mk1 plates and
   extras the report lists. Type MR1_CARD from the engraved card in the
   lines below, so they check it too. Then the closing checklist, every line that types words
   on the offline computer in the history-off shell: the `verify` line (export each
   seat's key again from its signer and run the `verify` line), the
   extras lines, each heir's plate check; then, anywhere, the per-shape
   signer-screen lines (register each shape's policy on a signer and check
   its first address on the screen). Any failure: "When a day-0 check fails". Deposit nothing
   until every line passes.
9. **Close.** Seal the bags; wipe the sitting signers of the heirs'
   children. **Only now**, with the root check (`mr child --check`) passed on each root's kept plate and
   every `verify` line passed, destroy:
   - the paper root, every paper copy of every child's words (the hot
     child's included) and the record of your flips, rolls or draws;
   - for each root, every plate of it but the one that passed the root check (`mr child --check`), a
     mis-engraved one included: grind or cut through every character (a few wrong
     characters are repairable: an ms1 string corrects up to 4);
   - every heir's plate that failed its check, the same way;
   - any copy of an ms1 string `create` printed that is written on
     paper, photographed or saved in a file (never a plate this step
     keeps: each root's plate that passed the root check (`mr child --check`), each heir's plate that
     passed its check, and the hot child's plate where a role lists
     `hot`).
   Each of these is the root or a seed it made. Then, with the card
   engraved and the kit notes printed (step 8), wipe the offline computer
   ("The offline computer"); the wipe deletes `report.txt` with every other file the sitting
   wrote. Store each item as "Where each item is stored" says.

**An unused root** (one `create` refused for good, or a draw abandoned) is
never engraved. Wipe every signer you loaded with its children, then
destroy its paper, the words `mr child` printed from it and its draw
record. No plate of it exists, so no unlabelled letter-A plate is ever
left.

**Worked example.** Alice and heir Bob: letters B (hers) and D
(Bob's) settled; `mr root --letters B,D --from dice` (99 rolls), its
passphrase chosen first; `mr child --network mainnet` prints B's and D's
words; B on her signer, D on a sitting signer, both keys exported; `mr
create "wsh(or_d(pk(@0),and_v(v:pkh(@1),older(52560))))" --network mainnet
--root - --keys
@0=REC_B @1=REC_D --role alice=creator:B,D --role bob=heir:D --seat-map
B,D > report.txt`; root plate engraved from root 1's printed string; the root check (`mr child --check`) exits 0; the
card and its second copy engraved and the kit notes printed; Bob's
plate, the mk1 plates and the two extras engraved; the `verify` line on keys exported
again exits 0 with `record 1 → seat @0`; the extras lines and Bob's plate
check pass; the signer-screen line for shape 0; the bag sealed, the
sitting signer wiped; only then papers and draw record destroyed and the
offline computer wiped. Then the first deposit.
```

#### 4. When create refuses a root

```text
When create refuses a root

Nothing is minted, and no plate of the root exists yet: the paper root is
its only copy. Keep it, destroy nothing, and do not wipe the offline
computer (the sitting goes on). Then take the steps in this order, as the
table at the end assigns them to each refusal; stop at the first that
resolves the refusal and run `create` again with the same root and
passphrase (steps 1, 2, 3 and 7), or start again from a new root (step
6).

1. **Where `create` printed a fact line** ("seat @I's key is root N's
   child M's; with this `--seat-map`, seat @I (path …) is letter L, listed
   by …, and letter M is at seat @J (path …), listed by …"; the key-check refusal or the root-key refusal),
   do the **step-1 comparison** first, and nothing else from this list for
   those seats:
   1. Compare `--seat-map` with the seat map you settled at sitting step 1
      (each letter at the seat whose path you meant for its holder). If it
      differs, correct it and run again: nothing to wipe.
   2. If it matches, **never change it to fit the keys**: the keys are
      crossed. If `--keys @I=` and `@J=` each name the record exported
      from the other seat's signer, correct them and run again: nothing
      to wipe.
   3. Otherwise the two seats' signers hold each other's words: wipe
      each, load on it the words `mr child` printed for its own seat's
      letter, with no passphrase, export again, and run again.
   A map typo, two swapped `--keys` arguments and two crosswise-loaded
   signers give `create` the same keys; fixing the wrong one crosses your
   heirs' paths with every check green. So the plan from step 1
   decides, never the keys.
2. **The root-key refusal only: the root and the network.** The root-key refusal shows this run's root
   fingerprint, network and base index.
   1. If `mr child` showed another root fingerprint, the root words or the
      passphrase differ. If its print is gone, run `mr child` again on the
      offline computer with the passphrase you gave `create`, and compare
      child L's first words with your paper copy: if they differ, this is
      the cause. If this root was made with a passphrase, type it again: a
      mistyped one gives these same letters with other keys. Then run `mr
      child` again with the root and passphrase you give `create`, load
      its words on the signers again, export again, destroy the first
      run's printed words, and run `create` again.
   2. If the fingerprints agree, compare the network and base index: if
      they differ, the network you mean decides. If `create`'s is the
      other one, give `create` `--network NET` and run it again; else run
      `mr child --network NET`, load its words, export again, and run
      `create` again.
   (The key-check refusal needs neither: its matching seat rules out root, passphrase and
   network.)
3. **A typo.** If a `--seat-map` or `--role` letter is mistyped against
   your step-1 plan, correct it and run again. Keep this root's paper.
4. **Whose seed is the failing seat's key?** (The key-check refusal and the root-key refusal; the other
   refusals go straight to step 5.)
   - **Someone else's seed** (an heir's own, a co-signer's, or anyone's
     own seed, not one of this root's children): **never wipe that
     person's signer**; their seed stays as it is. Go on to steps 5 and 6:
     it is the root that must change. No refusal asks a co-signer to
     change a seed (the mask refusal: a root makes no co-signer's seed).
   - **One of this root's children, on a signer you loaded,** with
     `--seat-map` right (step 1 or 3 found nothing): skip steps 5 and 6 and
     go to step 7.
5. **Wipe before destroying.** Before anything is destroyed, wipe every
   signer you loaded with this root's children.
6. **The root's remedy.** The mask is fixed by the root's words, so a root
   whose letters do not fit the card is replaced, never edited (the old
   draws with another mask would give the same tail):
   - **A new root from new draws**, `mr root --letters …` without the
     letter at fault (the mask refusal, the key-check refusal, the root-key refusal: without that letter; the mask refusal: with
     exactly the card's root-made letters; the disjoint-mask refusal: one
     of the two roots made again; the `--seeds`-in-mask refusal: either
     drop seed N from `--seeds`, since root N makes L, or a new root
     without L). Then destroy the old root's paper, the words `mr child`
     printed from it and its draw record (it was never engraved), and
     start again at sitting step 2.
   - **The only-letter remedy**, when the letter at fault is the root's
     only letter (the mask refusal's only-letter form, the mask refusal's no-seat form, or the root-key refusal and
     the key-check refusal on a one-letter root): if each seat of yours has a seed of its
     own, give `create` only keys exported from each seat's own seed, run
     `create` without `--root`, and destroy this root's paper (its words
     and the words `mr child` printed from it) and its draw record; if a
     seat of yours has none, make a new root from new draws without that
     letter, and destroy this root's paper and its draw record.
7. **Your own signer, with `--seat-map` right** (from step 4). The signer
   you loaded for that seat holds other words, a passphrase, or another
   letter's words: wipe it, load the words `mr child` printed for its
   seat's letter with no passphrase, export again, and run `create` again.
   Keep this root's paper; nothing is destroyed.

| Refusal | Steps |
|---|---|
| The mask refusal ("is co-signer …'s, and root N's mask lists L") | 3, then 5, 6 |
| The mask refusal, general ("mask lists L, which no seat of this card takes") | 3, then 5, 6 (a new root with the card's letters) |
| The mask refusal, no-seat ("no seat of this card is root N's") | 3, then 5, 6 (the only-letter remedy) |
| Disjoint masks ("roots N and M both make letter L") | 5, 6 |
| `--seeds` in a mask ("seed N has letter L … is not root N's child L") | 3, then 5, 6 |
| The key-check refusal (a seat fails beside a matching one) | with a fact line: 1 only; else 3, then 4 (someone else's seed: 5, 6; your own signer: 7) |
| The root-key refusal (no keyed child seat matches) | 1 for the seats a fact line names; then 2; then 3, then 4 (someone else's seed: 5, 6; your own signer: 7) |
```

#### 5. When a day-0 check fails

```text
When a day-0 check fails

Deposit nothing until every check passes. Destroy nothing: the paper root
and every printed word still exist until sitting step 9, and some branches
below need the paper root.

### A `verify` line fails

Export again first: each seat's key from its loaded signer now (a
co-signer from theirs), never the file given to `create`; a seat whose
signer is not at the sitting gives its mk1 plate. List every word set `mr
child` printed for this card in `--seeds` (leaving out an heir's gives
exit 1), and every other seed your role lists that has no passphrase of
its own (a seed of yours no root made: its ms1 plate, or its words); a
seed with its own passphrase is given by its seat's record in `--keys`.
Without that seed in `--seeds`, a signer that took a passphrase before
its key was first exported passes unseen.

- **(a) It exits 1 naming a record** ("the seating rule fails: record K at
  seat @I's path … carries fingerprint X, but the card declares Y for seat
  @I"): that signer holds other words or a passphrase.
  - If the seat is a root child: wipe that signer, load the words `mr
    child` printed with no passphrase, export again and run the `verify`
    line again. The card stays.
  - If it is someone's own seed (an heir's, a co-signer's, or yours from
    before): **never wipe it.** Its holder exports again from that seed
    with its own passphrase (or none if it has none), and you run the line
    again.
- **(b) It names a seed of `--seeds`:** retype that word set. If you
  printed it with `mr child` again and this root was made with a
  passphrase, run `mr child` again and type it: a mistyped one gives these
  same letters with other keys.
- **(c) It says seed N and record K "have the same master fingerprint …
  but give different keys"**, and you wrote that record's fingerprint in
  by hand: the signer holds other words or a passphrase, not another path.
  For a root child: wipe it, load the words with no passphrase, export
  again, run the line again. For any other seed: the seat-key procedure above
  (the holder exports from their own seed with the passphrase
  the wallet uses).
- **(d) It names a seed of yours that no root made, and that seed's
  fingerprint is not the card's for its seat** ("seed N and record K give
  different keys: … (seed N has fingerprint X, not the card's Y for seat
  @I …)", or "seat @I, letter L: no given seed has its fingerprint; seed
  N has this letter but fp X, not the card's Y"), and retyping its words
  or plate does not help: its signer took a passphrase (or held other
  words) when the key you gave `create` was exported, so the card itself
  declares the wrong key for that seat. Deposit nothing. **Never leave
  the seed out of `--seeds` to make the line pass**: `mr`'s "if seed N
  has one, leave it out" is only for a seed that really has its own
  passphrase. Never wipe that signer: its seed is yours from before.
  - First, on that signer with no passphrase entered, check its master
    fingerprint: if it is not X, the plate or words you gave in `--seeds`
    are not this signer's seed; stop, change nothing, and find the seed's
    right plate or words before going on.
  - If the seed has no passphrase of its own: turn the signer's
    passphrase off (never wipe it) and export that seat's key again with
    none. Then make the first card again at this sitting: destroy this
    card and every plate engraved for it but the root plate (its heir
    plates, extra plates and the mk1 plates of your seats; grind or cut
    through every character), as "The root check fails" does; keep the root plate, the
    paper root and the words `mr child` printed. Before the re-make,
    destroy everything printed or engraved from the first run's report
    but the plates kept: the card (both engraved copies), the kit notes,
    every bag sheet, the re-mint and no-mr sheets, any printed label,
    every mk1 plate made for that card and report.txt; keep each root
    plate, each heir plate that passed its check and each seed's own ms1
    plate; then print the notes and bag sheets again from the new report
    (step 8 of "The first sitting") and engrave the new card.
    Run `create --root -`
    again on the offline computer with the same command line, `--keys`
    giving that seat the new record, the same root and root passphrase;
    it prints the same root and heir ms1 strings. Engrave from its
    prints, and run the root check (`mr child --check`) and every closing-checklist line again. A card
    destroyed before any deposit is no existing card: the re-run is the
    same first card.
  - If you mean the seed to keep that passphrase from now on (you can
    recall it, and will rehearse it every year, "Rehearsing a seed's passphrase"): make the first card
    again the same way, giving `create` that seed as a seed with its own
    passphrase, by today's rules (`--seeds` and its passphrase, which
    prints the passphrase warning), beside its record. In the `verify`
    line it is then left out of `--seeds`, and its record in `--keys`
    fills its seat.
- **A signer that exports no fingerprint.** Write in place of `00000000`
  the fingerprint `create` printed for that seat ("seat @I: the key's
  fingerprint 00000000 is replaced by … FP"), as `[FP/PATH]XPUB`, or the
  seat's fingerprint `mr decode MR1_CARD` lists. A record left at
  `00000000` always fails; a wrong signer still fails with the right
  fingerprint, since its key differs (case (c)).

It passes when it exits 0 and prints, for your seat, a line beginning
`record K → seat @I` (a `, sc:…` suffix is the seat check, not a failure).

### The root check fails

- **The plate does not read** (the ms1 string's checksum fails, it lists
  letters other than the card's root-made ones, or `mr` refuses its
  letter: word 1 or 2 mis-engraved):
  1. Check that you typed the string as engraved.
  2. Re-engrave the plate from the ms1 string `create` printed for root
     N, if that print is still on the screen (it went to stderr, so a
     saved report does not hold it).
  3. If it is gone: on the offline computer, run `create --root -` again
     with the same command line (the same first card), typing the paper
     root, which still exists at this step, and its passphrase, and
     re-engrave from the string it prints for root N. No other route from
     the paper words to the plate's string exists.
  4. Run the root check (`mr child --check`) again. Once the new plate passes, destroy the plate that did
     not read (grind or cut through every character: with a few wrong
     characters it still gives the root back, since an ms1 string
     corrects up to 4), and engrave no further root plate.
- **The plate reads, and the root check (`mr child --check`) still fails:** check the network first. The
  card carries none and `mr child` takes any, so the root check (`mr child --check`)'s `--network NET` must
  be the one on `create`'s command line (kept at sitting step 5; none given
  is mainnet), and MR1_CARD this card's strings, never a rehearsal card's. If
  either was wrong, run the root check (`mr child --check`) again with the right one: never re-make the card
  for that. If both are right and the root check (`mr child --check`) still fails after the plate and the
  passphrase are typed again (`mr child`'s failure text says "if this
  root was made with a passphrase, run again and type it"): the passphrase
  is wrong, since `create` refused every other mismatch. Then:
  1. Remove the words loaded at this sitting from every signer, and delete
     the hot wallet set up at this sitting from its device.
  2. Destroy this card, its heir plates, its extra plates, the mk1 plates
     of your seats, every printed word and the printed label. **Keep the
     root plate and the paper root.** Destroy too everything else printed
     from the first run's report: the second card copy, the kit notes,
     every bag sheet, the re-mint and no-mr sheets and report.txt; after
     the re-make, print the notes and bag sheets again from the new
     report (step 8 of "The first sitting").
  3. Make the first card again with this root, from the plate, with the
     passphrase you will keep, and run every check again. A destroyed card
     whose printed words and plates were destroyed before any deposit is
     no existing card: the re-run is the same first card. It ends, as
     every sitting does, with the destroy line (which covers every paper
     copy of words its `mr child` run printed; the root plate stays) and
     the wipe.
- **The paper root is gone too and the plate still does not read:**
  destroy the plate with everything this sitting made, and make a new root
  from new draws with `mr root` (from step 2 of "The first sitting").

### An heir's plate check fails, or its string is gone

If `create`'s print of kit NAME's ms1 string is gone, or the heir plate
check (`mr verify MR1_CARD --network NET --set-id ID --seeds NAME_PLATE
--keys <that bag's mk1 plates>`) fails on a mis-engraved plate: on the
offline computer, before the destroy line, run `create --root -` again with
the same command line (the same first card), typing the paper root (it
still exists at this step) and its passphrase, and engrave a new plate from
the string it prints for kit NAME. Destroy the failed plate (grind or cut
through every character), then run the check again. `create`'s stderr
print is the only source of a root-made plate's string, and a text console
keeps no scrollback.

### The signer-screen lines

Before the first deposit to each shape, register that shape's policy on a
signer and check its first `mr address --shape K` on the signer's screen.
If the screen disagrees, deposit nothing to that shape.
```

#### 6. Where each item is stored

```text
Where each item is stored

**Your kit** (the creator's; a kit may be spread over several places):

| Item | Where |
|---|---|
| Root plate (each root's) | at a place of its own, apart from every card copy and from the first card's notes |
| The card, with the first card's kit notes | together, at a second place, apart from every root plate |
| A second card copy (root runs) | at the extras' place |
| Extra plates | all at the extras' place, apart from every root plate and every ms1 plate of yours |
| mk1 plates | one for each seat whose letter you do not list (a co-signer's, or an heir who brings their own seed) |
| ms1 plates | none for the letters a root made (the root plate stands in for them); one for each seed of yours no root made |
| Root's passphrase | in your memory; any layout note with it; never in any kit |

Why the second card copy at the extras' place: no one place then holds
every card copy and every re-mint sheet. Losing the notes' place leaves
that copy and the root plate, so the wallet and its keys are both kept and
nothing needs re-minting; losing the extras' place leaves the card and the
notes. The extras already carry values that move with the passphrase, so
this copy costs no deniability, and the root plate's place stays bare.

**An heir's kit:** the heir's child's own ms1 plate ("for NAME's kit:
letter L, no passphrase"), beside the sealed bag, as today. The bag sheet
names each of your seats' mk1 plates in that bag; the root is nowhere in
it. **A co-signer's kit:** unchanged.

**Letter A.** The root has letter A; another letter-A seed is allowed, and
`create` labels it "seed A (your own seed, not a root)". Tell the plates
apart by their labels. When the hot child lands on A, only the hot
child's plate goes on a hot device. Never load the root plate on a signer
or a hot device, and never decode it into one.

**The root plate** is kept while any card uses its seeds; it is no card's
plate and is never retired with one. It restores keys, not the wallet: a
card copy (any kit's) or your kit's re-mint sheet is also needed. The
first card's kit notes (its root-plate note) say never to give it to
`create` after its first card's sitting (a re-run at that sitting is the
same first card; anything later is a later version of `mr`'s), that `mr child` on the
offline computer reads it, never a signer, and that a wallet reading it
at standard paths shows nothing.

**Plates that look ordinary.** A plain random 24-word seed and a root,
or a seed a root made, look the same: 24 English words, or an ms1
string. Nothing on a plate says which it is. By default the root
plate's label reads only "may hold funds at nonstandard derivation
paths; keep it; never load it on a signer or online device": it does
not say it is a root, and reads the same with or without a passphrase.
A plate so labelled is a root plate; only the plate is ambiguous, and
the documentation, kept apart from it, says so. A root's words, loaded in a wallet (never do so: see
"Letter A" above), show an empty wallet, since a root holds no coins
itself; a child's words alone show none either, since its coins sit in
the card's wallets. So never judge such a plate worthless from an empty
wallet, and never destroy or give away a plate for that reason. Only
the kit notes and the labels record which plate is which: keep the
first card's notes as the table above says. With `create
--root-label-set-id` the label says plainly "a seed-set root; it holds
no coins itself and rebuilds the set's seeds", with the set id (and
the plate is then not deniable; below).

**One plate spends everything it made.** The root plate with its
passphrase spends every shape, every hot wallet of those seeds included.
Keep it as you would keep all your seeds together. Until destroyed at
sitting step 9, so do the paper root, the children's paper words and the
draw record.

**Deniability, and its limit.** Only the bare root plate is deniable. Each
of these, beside it, lets its holder learn whether the root has a
passphrase and test any guess offline:
- any copy of the card (every kit holds one, an heir's bag included);
- the first card's kit notes (each child's index, the set id and the
  digest move with the passphrase);
- a saved `create` report or `mr child` output;
- the extra plates' seat checks;
- any other plate of a seed the root made (an heir's or the hot child's
  ms1 plate, the mk1 plate of a root seat);
- the plate's own label, if you gave `create --root-label-set-id`.
So keep the notes with a card copy and every item above apart from the
root plate. If the root has a passphrase, its strength carries this; keep
it apart from every plate and every kit. `--root-label-set-id` puts the
set id on the label: the labelled plate is then not deniable either, but
the label might help with future spending if every card copy and the
first card's notes are lost. Without it the label carries no set id and
does not say what the plate is ("Plates that look ordinary", above). Recovery ("Restoring from the root plate", "Recovery without mr") brings plate and
notes together on the offline computer for its duration only.
```

#### 7. Restoring from the root plate

```text
Restoring from the root plate

**When:** every signer of yours is lost or broken (a stolen signer is not
this route: a later version of `mr`), your kit intact. Also whenever a text names "the
plate of the root that made it" as a seed's source.

1. On the offline computer, in the history-off shell ("The offline computer"), bring the root
   plate and the first card's notes together.
2. Run your restore line from the notes: `mr child --network NET
   --letters YOUR_LETTERS MR1_CARD`, the root plate's ms1 string typed at
   its prompt, the root's passphrase from memory (none if it has none).
   YOUR_LETTERS are your own root-made letters only, less every heir's, so
   no heir's words are printed (the chain still walks the whole mask). It
   exits 0 and prints the words of your own letters only.
   **With more than one root plate** (the plates read the same): try
   each plate in turn. To see which letters a plate makes, run `mr child
   --check --network NET MR1_CARD` with it: it prints "this root makes
   letters …" and no words. A plate whose letters are not the ones your
   notes list for the root you want is another root's plate, not a wrong
   passphrase; give each plate only the letters it makes. Only when
   every root plate fails is the passphrase in doubt.
   **If it exits 2** ("this root's mask does not list L"): this plate is
   another root's, or the line names letters of two roots; check as just
   said and try the other root plate.
   **If it exits 1** ("not a child of this root as read", or "this root
   made no seat of this card"): load nothing and destroy nothing; the root
   plate stays. Check `--network` against your notes' restore line and the
   plate's string as engraved, try the other root plate if there is one,
   and type the passphrase again ("if this root was made with a
   passphrase, run again and type it; a mistyped one gives these same
   letters with other keys"). If it still fails, or you cannot recall the
   passphrase: deposit nothing more to this card's shapes, and keep the
   root plate and your kit as they are (the plate restores your seeds in
   full if you recall the passphrase later). Every signer of yours is
   lost, so your own seats cannot sign until then: spend or move this
   card's coins through the card's other paths while they can (with your
   co-signers now, where the card has them, or by the heirs' path once
   its timelock opens, with that heir; a move to a new seed set is fold
   J's). If the card has no other path, its coins stay locked until the
   passphrase is recalled: keep everything, the plate, the kit and every
   card copy. Wipe the offline computer ("The offline computer"). (The paragraph in "A forgotten passphrase" on the
   root's passphrase is for a signer that still works; it does not apply
   here.)
3. Load each letter's words into its own new signer, one seed per signer
   as before, **with no passphrase**. Heirs' letters stay on their plates.
4. Heirs' and co-signers' seats come from your kit's mk1 plates (or the
   extras), or from their bags. Then `mr export` with the seeds and
   plates; it seats them by letter and fingerprint.
5. Destroy every paper copy of words this run printed (the root plate
   stays). Then wipe the offline computer ("The offline computer").

**Words printed without a card** ("not checked against any card"): at a
first card's sitting `create --root` checks the keys exported from them.
Otherwise never use them as printed: print them again with a card of this
set that carries fingerprints (every first card does), `mr child --network
NET --letters L MR1_CARD` on the offline computer, the root plate typed at
its prompt and its passphrase from memory. It prints a letter's words only
when they match the card: use those, and destroy every paper copy of the
print made without a card. (A `--check` run tests only what is typed in
that run, never words printed earlier.) With no card left, re-mint the
card and check its digest ("Re-minting a lost card"). A letter
printed under "not checked against this card (no fingerprint)" is checked
with `mr verify MR1_CARD --network NET --seeds <these words> --keys <that
seat's extra plate or exported key>`.

**A key source.** Where your kit names "the ms1 plate of L, or the plate
of the root that made it", the plate's words come from steps 1 to 2 with
`--letters L`; once they are used, wipe as step 5.

**Later cards** follow today's rules. Root-made seeds are then ordinary
seeds, given by their words (steps 1 to 2) or by their keys; wipe as step
5 once the words are used. Rotation, replacement and loss are a later version of `mr`'s.
```

#### 8. Re-minting a lost card

```text
Re-minting a lost card

**When:** every copy of the card is lost; your kit's re-mint sheet
survives. The sheet's first line: "a re-mint replaces only the card: check
its digest, then keep your kit exactly as the first card's notes list it;
engrave nothing else this run names: no ms1 plate of a root child (the
root plate stands in for them) and no new extra plate (the first card's
extras stay at the extras' place)".

1. On the offline computer, in the history-off shell, for each seat the
   sheet lists as "child of root N": `mr child --network NET --letters L`,
   the root plate typed at its prompt, its passphrase from memory; it
   prints the words with no card (or check them against any surviving
   copy). Do not wipe yet.
2. Give the words in `--seeds`, and the seat's key as its extra plate in
   `--keys` (or load the words on a signer with no passphrase and export
   the key at the seat's path). Other seats as the sheet says ("ms1 plate
   L", "mk1 plate"). The hot child's input is the sheet's own
   (`--first-hot-child`, `--retire-child` or `--hot-on`, as printed).
3. Run `create` with every input the sheet prints (the template or the
   shapes file in full, `--room`, the roles, the seat map), **never with
   `--root`**.
4. Check the digest against the one the kit notes give (`mr verify
   MR1_CARD --digest DIGEST`), never the digest the re-mint prints.
5. **If the digest differs, or `create` refuses a child's words:** never
   drop the seed, and never conclude from a mismatch that the backup is
   lost: the root plate and your kit stay as they are. First compare
   every other input with the sheet, character by character: the shapes
   file or template, `--room`, the roles, `--seat-map` and the hot child's
   input; each changes the digest. Correct any that differs and run
   `create` again. Only if they all match: run `mr child` again, the root plate typed at its
   prompt, retyping the root's passphrase first: if this root was made
   with a passphrase, a mistyped one gives these same letters with other
   keys. Check that it prints the network you re-mint on (NET, and the
   base index the sheet gives). Destroy every paper copy of words the
   earlier run printed (the root plate stays), and go back to step 2. Do
   not wipe before the digest matches.
6. Once the digest matches: destroy every paper copy of words this re-mint
   printed (the root plate stays), engrave the card, and wipe the offline
   computer ("The offline computer").

An heir's or co-signer's sheet names each root-child seat's mk1 plate in
that kit ("mk1 plate of seat @J, in this bag"), and never `mr child` or
the root.
```

#### 9. Recovery without mr

```text
Recovery without mr

**When:** `mr` is not available and your signers must be restored from
the root plate. The root plate is an ms1 string but no signer's seed:
never decode it into a signer, never type its words into one, and never
use a signer's codex32 import (a Coldcard's Import Codex32 or any other)
on it.

**Placeholders:** `N` is the root's number, as the first card's notes
name it ("root N"); no plate shows it, so with more than one root plate
try each plate in turn (step 4); `L` is a letter; `I` is the index those notes list
for letter L.

1. Bring the root plate and the first card's notes together on the offline
   computer, never a signer. Before typing the plate or any words, start
   a shell that keeps no history (bash or zsh: `unset HISTFILE` as its
   first command; fish: start it as `fish --private`; dash keeps none), or
   use a live system with no persistent storage.
2. **Decode the plate to the root's 24 English words**, in that shell:
   - with ms-cli: `ms decode -`, then type the plate's string and end
     with Ctrl-D on a line of its own (end of input, not an empty line).
     Never put the string on the command line: ms-cli refuses it (since
     0.17.0), after your shell has already saved it;
   - or by hand: ignore spaces and hyphens, read upper and lower case
     alike, drop the first 9 characters (`ms10entrs`) and the last 13 (the
     checksum); read each other character as 5 bits by its place in
     `qpzry9x8gf2tvdw0s3jn54khce6mua7l` (q = 0, l = 31); join them, read
     bytes of 8 bits and drop the 0 to 4 bits left over (all 0s). The
     first byte is 00 (a root is English); the other 32 bytes are the
     entropy. Read its bits 11 at a time, each a word's place in the
     BIP-39 English list, counting from 0. The 24th word is short: its
     remaining 3 bits are the start of its place, and the rest of its
     place is the first 8 bits of the entropy's SHA-256, computed on the
     offline computer. Checking the plate's own checksum also needs a
     computer.
3. Give a BIP-85 tool that takes an index, on the offline computer and
   never a signer, the root's 24 words and root N's passphrase, typed from
   memory (none if it has none; no kit holds it). Ask it, per letter, for
   the BIP-85 BIP-39 application, English, 24 words, index I: the index
   the first card's notes list for that letter ("root N made letters L
   (BIP-85 BIP-39 application, English, 24 words, index I, …)").
4. **Check each letter's words before loading any.** Word 1 of letter L's
   words must lie, in alphabetical order, in L's block of the BIP-39
   English list (both ends included): B `avoid` to `cable`; C `cactus` to
   `copper`; D `copy` to `divide`; E `divorce` to `exit`; F `exotic` to
   `garment`; G `gas` to `husband`; H `hybrid` to `lend`; I `length` to
   `morning`; J `mosquito` to `paper`; K `parade` to `quiz`; L `quote` to
   `say`; M `scale` to `soup`; N `source` to `that`; O `theme` to
   `useful`; P `useless` to `zoo`. If any letter's word 1 does not: load
   nothing and destroy nothing. If this root was made with a passphrase,
   type it again: a mistyped or forgotten one gives valid words of other
   wallets. Check also the index and your reading of the plate. With
   more than one root plate, the plates read the same: a plate that fails
   this check may be another root's plate, not a wrong passphrase, so
   decode each other root plate and check again from step 2; only when
   every root plate fails is the passphrase in doubt. This catches about
   15 slips in 16; passing it is no proof.
5. Load each of your own letters' words (never an heir's) on its own new
   signer, with no passphrase.
6. Only then destroy every paper copy of words this procedure produced
   (the root plate stays), and wipe the offline computer: if its shell was
   not started with history off, close every shell first and only then
   delete its history file (bash writes the session back on exit); then
   delete every file this procedure wrote; a live system with no
   persistent storage is wiped by shutting it down.

Signer menus do not reach index 270,028; a tool that takes an index is
needed. The indexes move with the root's passphrase, which is one reason
the notes are kept apart from the plate.
```

#### 10. Rehearsing the root

```text
Rehearsing the root

**Who:** the creator; only her kit's notes carry it. A heir never
rehearses the root and never needs its passphrase. Root-mode cards always
carry seat fingerprints, so any card copy of the set serves.

**Every year:**
1. On the offline computer, in the history-off shell: `mr child --check
   --network NET MR1_CARD`, typing root N's plate (its ms1 string) at its
   prompt and its passphrase from memory (none if it has none).
2. It must exit 0. It prints the per-seat lines and no words; it tests the
   passphrase too, if there is one.
3. Then wipe the offline computer, now ("The offline computer").

**If it fails:** type the passphrase again, and the plate's string as
engraved ("if this root was made with a passphrase, run again and type
it; a mistyped one gives these same letters with other keys"); check the
network. If it still fails, or you cannot recall the passphrase: keep
every signer, wipe none of them, deposit nothing more to this card's
shapes, and go to "A forgotten passphrase".
```

#### 11. Rehearsing a seed's passphrase

```text
Rehearsing a seed's passphrase

**Which seeds:** a seed with a BIP-39 passphrase of its own (`--passphrase
N=…` or the prompt), whose ms1 plate is in this kit. Never a root plate:
the root's check is "Rehearsing the root". Root children carry no passphrase and need no
rehearsal.

**Every year, on the signer that holds the seed:** enter the passphrase
there. **Never wipe or re-import the seed for this check.** Check the
master fingerprint the signer shows against your kit's reference, which
the kit's line prints by card mode:

| Your kit | Reference |
|---|---|
| holds its card loose (the creator, a co-signer, an open heir) | the fingerprint `mr decode MR1_CARD` lists for the seat; a hot child's parent that fills no seat: the one the notes give for it |
| a sealed or executor-mode heir's kit | the fingerprint this kit's seat-key line for the seat gives; never the card in the bag (or with the executor) |
| a card without fingerprints, a sealed or executor-mode heir's kit | the xpub the signer shows at each of this kit's seat-key lines' origin path, matched against that line |
| a card without fingerprints, a kit holding its card loose | export the record at the seat's origin path (the kit's "seat @I (letter L): origin path" line) with the passphrase entered; give it to `mr verify MR1_CARD --network NET --keys …` beside the kit's mk1 plates and the `--seat-check` values the notes give: it passes when it prints a line beginning `record K → seat @I` |

**If you cannot recall the passphrase:** deposit nothing more to this
card's shapes, and never power off or update a signer that still has it.
Go to "A forgotten passphrase" now: a session ends at the next power-off.
```

#### 12. A forgotten passphrase

```text
A forgotten passphrase

**The root's passphrase.** Nothing can recover it, not `mr`, not a plate,
not a kit. The root plate no longer backs your seeds, but your signers
still hold and spend them. Keep every signer and wipe none of them;
deposit nothing more to this card's shapes; make a new backup before a
signer fails. New seeds and a new seed set are a later version of `mr`'s: this release has
no designed route for that new backup.

**A seed's own passphrase.**
- **If your signer still has it** (a stored passphrase, or a session still
  open): while that signer still signs, spend or move this card's coins
  with it (on a card made without `--root`, by today's spending
  procedure; on a card made with `--root`, a move to a new seed set is
  a later version of `mr`'s), never powering it off or updating it meanwhile, and deposit
  nothing more to this card's shapes. A session ends at the next
  power-off, so use the window; do not wait it out.
- **Otherwise:** on most signers, which ask for the passphrase at each
  start, this seat can no longer sign. Spend or move this card's coins
  through the card's other paths while they can (in the same way), and
  deposit nothing more. The seed's plate restores another wallet without
  the passphrase.
```

#### 13. An heir spending

```text
An heir spending

The heir's seed (letter D for Bob) was made by the creator's root, but it
is an ordinary seed: Bob's plate carries no passphrase, and he never needs
the root or its passphrase.

1. **Before sealing** (the creator, at sitting step 8): `mr verify
   MR1_CARD --network NET --set-id ID --seeds BOB_PLATE --keys <the bag's
   mk1 plates>` must pass (exit 0, `seed 1 → seat @3`, "passed, but …
   only filled" is a pass).
2. **When the heir path opens** (its timelock passed), Bob opens his bag
   and types his plate only on an offline computer, in a shell started
   with history off (as "The offline computer": bash or zsh, `unset HISTFILE` as its first
   command; fish, `fish --private`): his plate alone now spends the heir
   path. There he checks the card with `mr verify MR1_CARD --digest
   DIGEST` (the digest his bag sheet gives), then runs `mr export --seeds
   BOB_PLATE --keys <the bag's mk1 plates>`. His plate seats by letter and fingerprint; on
   a card with several shapes, `--shape` is in every spend line it prints.
3. To put his seed on a signer: decode his ms1 plate to its words on the
   offline computer, in a shell started with history off, with `ms decode
   -` (type the string, end with Ctrl-D, never the string on the command
   line), or by hand (step 2 of "Recovery without mr", his plate's length), and enter the words
   with the signer's import by words, with no passphrase. Never use a
   codex32 import on an ms1 plate. A plate labelled "may hold funds at
   nonstandard derivation paths; keep it; never load it on a signer or
   online device" (or "root N, letter A …") is a root
   plate, never decoded into a signer (`mr member --for-spend`'s recipe says so).
   When he is done (after step 2, or after this step if he takes it), he
   wipes the offline computer as "The offline computer" says (the wipe line).
4. **An heir with a passphrase of her own** (Carol) brought an
   independent seed: her kit holds no root-made plate, and her passphrase
   lives in her memory, never in a kit ("Choosing a root passphrase"); she rehearses it as "Rehearsing a seed's passphrase" says.

A stolen sealed bag's plate spends the heir path when the timelock opens;
that is the accepted cost of a root-made heir seed carrying no passphrase.
```

The history-off and wipe lines were checked under bash 5.3, zsh 5.9 and fish 4.9 (2026-10-08; `mr-wipe-line-shells`). `unset HISTFILE` as the first command keeps every later command out of the history file in bash and in zsh (zsh with an incremental-history option still saves the `unset HISTFILE` line itself). `fish --private` writes no history file, while a plain fish writes each command to `~/.local/share/fish/fish_history`. Deleting a history file while its shell is open does not wipe it in bash or zsh, which write the session back on exit (so "close every shell first and only then delete" is right), and in fish a deletion while open does not bring the earlier commands back; in a new fish, `history clear` empties the file. The "never pipe from `printf` or `echo`" line stands in all three: a shell that keeps history saves the whole command line.

---

## `mr verify`

Re-read an engraved card and check it against ids, keys or seeds. With
keys or seeds it prints the seat each one filled (`plate 2 → seat @2,
sc:…`; a device record's line, `record 3 → seat @1, sc:…`; the plates that
filled a seat by then are listed before a refusal too), and for
each seed the seat it filled or agreed with, ending with that seat's
seat check (`seed 1 → seat @0, sc:…`, `seed 2 → seat @1 (agrees with
plate 2), sc:…`, `seed 1 → seat @2 (its child 24/0), sc:…`; a seed given
twice, or the hot child's own words beside its parent, agrees with what
filled the seat: `seed 3 → seat @1 (agrees with seed 2), sc:…`, `seed 3
→ seat @0 (agrees with seed 1's child 24/0), sc:…`; a seed refused at its
seat, or a run that fails a card rule, prints none), followed by ``your
seats' seat checks are printed for comparison where a kit asks for them;
never keep them beside your seed: with the seed they test the
passphrase``, and fails on a stub mismatch or a seed that
fills no seat (see Keys and seeds). A seat check covers every seat's key
(below), so a run that holds no key for some seat prints none, and says
once, on stderr, ``no seat check printed: seat @2 has no key in this run,
and a seat check covers every seat's key`` (``seats @1 and @2 have``); a
`verify` of the card alone prints the keyed seats' checks (``seat @0
(keyed by the card): sc:…``) only on a fully keyed card. On a stub mismatch it says ``…: re-mint
that plate before engraving``, unless the plate's fingerprint is none
this card declares either: then ``…: a plate of another card, or minted
wrong: plate <k> looks like another card's plate (stub <s>, fp <fp>):
check that you hold this card's kit; re-mint a plate only if it is this
card's`` (`export` warns the same `plate <k> looks like another card's
plate …` line beside its stub warning, and goes on; `member` and
`address` refuse it with the same words, ending ``…; if it is this
card's, pass `--accept-stub-mismatch` to `mr <verb>` to use them``,
"to use it" when the refusal names one plate).
A plate matches when it carries the card's (0, 0) stub: the template id
on a keyless card; on a keyed card its `WalletPolicyId`, the stub
`create`, `upgrade` and `decode` print for minting (``(0, 0) stub: 0aeaa2ea (the `--policy-id-stub 0aeaa2ea` of every `mk encode` line)``), or its template id,
which a plate minted from the keyless md1 of the same wallet carries
(this second form is a default the owner may still change). On a keyed
card every stub text names both forms (``its (0, 0) stub is <policy>
(the `WalletPolicyId`) or <template> (the template id); plate 2 carries
…``), as does the warning `--accept-stub-mismatch` prints in `create`,
`upgrade`, `member` and `address`.

A seed no key confirms is refused naming the seats, singular or plural:
``seat @1 is not confirmed: give its device record or mk1 plate with `--keys`, or your kit notes' `--seat-check` values``
(``seats @0, @1 are not confirmed: give their device records or mk1 plates
with `--keys`, or your kit notes' `--seat-check` values``; with no key at all, the warning ``seeds not confirmed against any key: add `--keys RECORDS_OR_PLATES` or your kit notes' `--seat-check` values``). On a card with no fingerprints a seed confirms no seat, so the refusal names where each device record comes from, "seat @1 is not confirmed: give its device record, exported at m/270028'/1'/1', or mk1 plate with `--keys`, or your kit notes' `--seat-check` values" ("…: give their device records, exported at m/270028'/1'/0' and m/270028'/1'/1', or mk1 plates with `--keys`, or your kit notes' `--seat-check` values").

A plate never confirms by itself a seat that nothing on the card ties to
this wallet. A keyless card's (0, 0) stub is its template id, which
every card of that template shares, and a keyed card accepts that stub
too (§9 Q40), so another wallet's plate of the same template at a seat's
path fills the seat. A seat is untied when the card keys no xpub for it,
whatever fingerprint it declares there, and a record or a plate alone
fills it, whatever stub the plate carries, with no given seed deriving
its key and no funded witness: a declared fingerprint confirms a seed
that has it, never a plate's or record's key, since it is a 4-byte label
any plate or record can carry. A keyed card's `WalletPolicyId` does not tie such a seat
either: it hashes only that seat's path, which every card of the
template shares, so two partly keyed cards without fingerprints that
key the same seats print one stub. Such a seat is reported ``seat @1:
filled by plate 2, sc:… (nothing on this card ties this key to this
wallet: give the `--seat-check` value your kit notes or bag sheet give
for set <id>, seat @1, or compare this seat check with the seat holder's
before any deposit)`` (``record 1`` when a record fills it, in `verify`), never
"confirmed by", in `verify`, `member`, `address`, `member --for-spend`,
`export` and the heir file. `verify` exits 0 and ends ``verify: passed,
but seat @1 is only filled: nothing on this card ties its key to this
wallet; compare its seat check with its holder's, and give your kit
notes' `--seat-check` value for it, or, for an extra plate, the value on
its label (if they give none, the value `mr
verify` with that seat's seed prints on its seed line, or on its device
record's line; for a sealed or executor-mode heir's seat, the seat check
printed by `mr verify MR1_CARD --seeds KIT_SEEDS --keys KIT_PLATES
"HEIR_RECORD"` (HEIR_RECORD the record form of the xpub on the heir's
seat-key line, brought from the heir's kit, and KIT_SEEDS and KIT_PLATES
this kit's seeds and plates), or re-minted kit notes; a line with no fingerprint, on a card made with `--no-fingerprints`, gives the record `[00000000/PATH]XPUB`, 00000000 standing for the missing fingerprint)`` (for several,
``verify: passed, but seats @0, @2 are only filled: nothing on this card
ties their keys to this wallet; compare their seat checks with their
holders', and give your kit notes' `--seat-check` values for them, or, for
extra plates, the values on their labels (if
they give none, the values `mr verify` with those seats' seeds prints on
their seed lines, or on their device records' lines; …)``). A seat whose
given seed agrees with its plate stays confirmed, by the seed: ``confirmed by seed 2 (plate 2 agrees)``. A card
with fingerprints refuses a plate that carries another fingerprint (see
above), but one carrying the card's own is only filled, like any other.
In `export` and `member --for-spend` such a seat whose plate carries
another fingerprint, or none on a card that declares one, is reported
``seat @1: filled by plate 2, sc:… (fingerprint-disputed: compare this
seat check with the seat holder's)``, and, tied by a matching value,
``seat @1: filled by plate 2, tied by `--seat-check` (fingerprint-disputed)``.
A plate bound to a seat with `@i=` in `export` or `member --for-spend` is
reported the same way, ``filled by plate 2, sc:…``, never "confirmed".
`export` prints each plate of an undecided group at such a seat, ``plate
2: undecided (nothing on this card ties this key to this wallet)``, with
no seat check: the plate has a seat, and so a seat check, only once a
seating places it (``no seat check printed: seats @0 and @1 are
undecided, and a seat check covers every seat's key``).

**The seat check** is `sc:` and 32 hex digits in eight groups of four, a
key half and a wallet half of 16 digits each. The key half is the first 8
bytes of SHA-256 over `mr1/sc-key`, the card's set id (8 bytes), the seat
number (1 byte) and that seat's chain code and compressed key; the wallet
half the first 8 bytes of SHA-256 over `mr1/sc-wallet`, the set id, the
seat number, the card's seat count (1 byte) and every seat's chain code
and key in seat order, each the key that fills it in the run (the card's
xpub at a keyed seat). Every card of one template can share a set id, so
the wallet half is what ties a value to this wallet's keys; the key half
says which seat's key differs. Two values compared: both halves equal,
the same keys at every seat; the key half different, a different key at
this seat, or another seat's or card's value; the key half equal and the
wallet half different, a different key at another seat, or a value made
for another wallet of the template. Test vectors: a card of three seats,
set id `17df92c526428c7b`, holding BIP 32 test vectors 1, 2 and 3's
master keys, gives `sc:fe28-2294-e1d5-c4f0-146d-9d55-9651-902f`,
`sc:5d5a-945b-d8e7-459d-a7ff-0c47-ac45-da99` and
`sc:47a4-fda6-7e26-fb8d-ae6a-0285-b6d3-94f9`. Whoever holds the other
seats' keys can compute a value for a key of their own, so only the
comparison with each seat holder's own value catches a forged key.

**`--seat-check [<set-id>:]@<i>=sc:<32 hex>`** (`verify`, `member`,
`address`, `export`, repeatable) gives a seat's seat check as its holder
compared it, the value the kit notes give (``set <id>, seat @1's seat
check: `--seat-check <id>:@1=sc:…` (give it to every `mr member`, `mr
address`, `mr verify` and `mr export`; compare it with the seat holder's
before the first deposit)``). The set id, full or its 8-hex-digit
prefix, names the card; it may be left out on a run given one card. A
matching value ties an untied seat for the run (``seat @1: filled by
plate 2, tied by `--seat-check` ``); when every such seat is tied,
`verify` ends ``verify: passed``. A value that differs, at any filled
seat, is refused by the half that differs, the value named by its
position, never quoted. When its key half differs: ``seat @1: plate 2
has sc:…, not `--seat-check` value 1: its key half differs, so this
seat's key is not the one the value was made from, or the value is
another seat's or another card's (or this card's on another network);
check the value against the seat holder's (and the plate against its
label)`` (``(and the record against
its device)``, ``seed 1 has … (and the seed's passphrase)``, ``the
card's xpub has … (the card keys this seat: the value is wrong)``, which
`verify` also gives with no keys or seeds on a fully keyed card), and
`member --for-spend`, and `export` with a single candidate seating, add
``; or leave the value out: this verb runs without it``. When only the
wallet half differs: ``seat @1: this run's keys give sc:…, not
`--seat-check` value 1: the key half matches, but a key differs at
another seat, or the value was made for another wallet of this template,
or it was copied wrong[; no value given here names seat @2 (plate 2): a
wrong seed or passphrase there, or another key there than the one the
value was made with, would also do this]; compare each seat's value with
its holder's, and look for the seat whose key half differs; if none does,
do not deposit until each holder's full value matches``, the bracket
naming each seat no given value names (``seats @0 and @2 (seed 1 and
plate 2)`` for several), as causes only: it names nothing as wrong and
offers no "leave the value out". A value that equals in both halves the
seat check another seat has in this run is that seat's, given under the
wrong seat: ``seat @1: `--seat-check` value 1 is seat @0's seat check, not
seat @1's: give it as`` `@0=` (the message ends with that binding). A run prints every key-half refusal, and the wallet-half ones only
when no key half differs. On a run with no key for some seat a value
checks nothing: `` `--seat-check` value 1 checks nothing: seat @2 has no
key in this run``. In `export`, where several candidate seatings are
tried (`--try-seatings`, an undecided plate group), the values are
checked against each, the ones they differ at are dropped and counted on
stderr (`` `--seat-check`: 5 of 6 candidate seatings dropped: a value
differs``), and the run refuses only when none is left: ``
`--seat-check`: all 6 candidate seatings dropped: no seating of the
given plates gives these values; they were made for another wallet, or a
plate is not this wallet's; compare each seat's value with its
holder's``, followed by ``; or leave the value out: this verb runs
without it`` when every dropped seating differs in a key half, and
otherwise by ``; some seating matches every given key half, so a key at
another seat may be forged: do not deposit until each holder's full
value matches``. When every candidate leaves a seat without a key, no value
can be checked, and each is ignored with a ``checks nothing`` warning, once:
the first form above (`` `--seat-check` value 1 checks nothing: seat @2 has
no key in this run``) for a value on a run that holds no key for some seat,
or, for a value whose own seat no kept candidate fills, the other
(``seat @2: nothing fills it, so `--seat-check` value 1 checks nothing``).
A value of 20 hex digits, the form of kit notes printed
before this release, is never compared: naming a given card it is
refused at any seat, before the other checks below, ``mr:
`--seat-check` value 1 is a seat check in a former form (20 hex digits):
re-print your kit notes from the kit's re-mint note (`create` or
`upgrade` again, which gives the same card), and give the new value``
(`export` and `member --for-spend` adding ``; or leave the value out:
this verb runs without it``); naming no given card it is warned of,
``…is a seat check in a former form and names no card given here:
re-print that card's kit notes from its kit's re-mint note``. The seat
takes no leading zero (`@01=` is not of the form). A value that does not read, a
second value for one seat of one card (``a second value for one seat``),
or a value with no set id on a run of several cards (``give each value
its card's set id, since this run has several cards``) is a usage
error, exit 2, never echoed: ``invalid value for '--seat-check
<[SET_ID:]@I=sc:HEX>' at position <n> (not echoed): it takes the card's
set id and a colon (optional on a run of one card), then @<i>=sc: and
the seat check's 32 hex digits, as your kit notes give them``. Once the
card is read, a seat it lacks is refused, ``mr: `--seat-check` value 2:
the card has no seat @7``, as is a set id prefix two given cards share
(``mr: `--seat-check` value 1: its set id prefix is that of cards <id>
and <id>: give the full set id``). A value for a seat nothing fills
warns ``seat @1: nothing fills it, so `--seat-check` value 1 checks
nothing``, and one naming no given card warns `` `--seat-check` value 1
names no card given here, so it checks nothing``. A value that matches in
both halves covers every seat's key, so it confirms each seat a given seed
(or that seed's hot child) fills that nothing else confirms, reported
``seat @0: confirmed by `--seat-check` value 1`` (the first such value
names it): `member` and `address` hand out, and `verify` passes, with the
kit notes' values alone on a card with no fingerprints. A seat a plate or
record fills still needs its own value, and with no value a seed stays
unconfirmed: `--accept-unconfirmed-seeds` stays the seeds' consent.

`member` and `address` refuse an untied seat that no `--seat-check`
ties, exit 1, one line per such seat, then the seat check of each seat
a given seed fills and, when there is one, the keep-apart note:
``seat @1: filled by plate 2, but nothing on this card ties this key to
this wallet: give the `--seat-check` value your kit notes give for set
<id>, seat @1 (for an extra plate, the value on its label); if seat @1 is your own seed's, give the seed with
`--seeds`, which ties it; otherwise, if the kit notes give no seat check
for seat @1 nor for any other seat of set <id>, they predate seat checks:
re-print them from the kit's re-mint note (`create` or `upgrade` again,
which gives the same card); if they give none for this seat only, compare
this plate's seat check, sc:…, with the seat holder's, and give the
holder's (for a sealed or executor-mode heir's seat, which prints none:
give the record form of the xpub on the heir's seat-key line, brought
from the heir's kit, as HEIR_RECORD, with KIT_SEEDS and KIT_PLATES this
kit's seeds and plates, to `mr verify MR1_CARD --seeds KIT_SEEDS --keys
KIT_PLATES "HEIR_RECORD"` and read the seat check it prints, or re-mint
the kit notes; a line with no fingerprint, on a card made with `--no-fingerprints`, gives the record `[00000000/PATH]XPUB`, 00000000 standing for the missing fingerprint)``, ``seat @0:
filled by seed 1, sc:…``, ``your seats' seat checks are printed for
comparison where a kit asks for them; never keep them beside your seed:
with the seed they test the passphrase``. A run with an untied seat and
an unconfirmed seed-filled seat is refused once, the untied lines first,
then ``seat @0 is unconfirmed: nothing on this card checks its seed; give
its plate or device records, or your kit notes' `--seat-check` values, or
pass`` `--accept-unconfirmed-seeds` (the message ends with that flag), since the values tie the plates and,
matching in both halves, confirm the seeds; `--accept-unconfirmed-seeds`
lifts only that half and does not lift the untied seats' refusal. `export` and `member --for-spend` print the filled
line and do not refuse; `member --for-spend` refuses a differing value,
and `export` drops the seatings it differs at (above).

`--set-id` and `--digest` take the full 16 hex digits; any other length,
such as `export`'s 8-digit `[card …]` prefix, is a usage error (exit 2)
that says so and does not quote the value (for `--digest` it adds that
`export`'s `[card …]` shows only the first 8). A full-length value that is the
card's other id is named, and the card is not called another set: `--set-id`
given the card digest fails ``the set id is <id>, not <value>: <value> is
this card's digest, not its set id: give it with `--digest` ``, and
`--digest` given the set id the reverse (``the card digest is <digest>, not
<value>: <value> is this card's set id, not its digest: give it with
`--set-id` ``); any other value fails ``…: this card is not that set``
(``…: this is not that engraving``). `--against` compares like
with like: when both cards carry xpubs and fingerprints at the same
seats, their policy ids, which cover every seat's origin path,
fingerprint and xpub (``(0, 0) matches the md1 card``); otherwise (two
keyless cards, one card keyed and the other not, keys at different
seats, or one without fingerprints) the template ids, then each seat by
its xpubs where both carry one, else by the fingerprints both declare
(``member (0, 0) does not match the md1 card at seat @<i>: its
fingerprint is <fp>, the md1 card's <fp>``) and their origin paths,
since the same seed at another path is another wallet (``member (0, 0)
does not match the md1 card at seat @<i>: its origin path is <path>,
the md1 card's <path> (the same seed at another path is another
wallet)``; this is checked on every seat the two cards do not key
identically, every seat of two keyless cards, one with neither an xpub
nor a fingerprint on either side included). The first seat that differs
is named, xpubs and fingerprints before origin paths, and no xpub is
echoed. It is not a byte comparison: a card `upgrade` issued with
fingerprints it added passes against its fingerprint-less md1. A pass
between two keyless cards prints ``(0, 0) matches the md1 card: the
same template, and each seat's origin path, and its fingerprint where
both declare one``; a pass across forms prints ``(0, 0) matches the md1
card: the same template, and each seat's xpub where both carry one, else
its fingerprint, and its origin path (the two carry xpubs or
fingerprints at different seats)``. Where the md1 card carries an xpub at a seat and this card none, a key the
run seats there (a seed's, a hot child's, a plate's or a record's) is
compared with it, chain code and key alone: a difference is refused,
``member (0, 0) does not match the md1 card at seat @0: the key this run
seats there is not the md1 card's xpub``, and a match counts as compared
by xpub (a forged upgrade's plates, which carry the right fingerprints,
passed "by fingerprint only" while the run held every seat's key; on a
card without fingerprints the owner's own keys were refused as not
comparable). A seat the run holds no key for is compared by its
fingerprint alone, or, with none, refused as below. When no seat has a fingerprint or an
xpub on both cards, so that any wallet of the template would match, the
run ends ``verify: passed, but the md1 card was compared by template and
origin paths only: no seat has a fingerprint or xpub on both sides, so
any wallet of this template matches`` in place of the bare pass. When the
md1 card's xpub at a seat was compared by fingerprint alone, since this
card carries none there, the run ends
``verify: passed, but the md1 card was compared at seat @1 by fingerprint
only: the md1 card's xpub there is not checked, since this card carries
none; compare that seat's seat check with its holder's`` (``at seats
@0, @1 and @2 … the md1 card's xpubs there are not checked … compare
those seats' seat checks with their holders'``): a forged md1 with the
right fingerprints and another wallet's xpubs passes such a compare. The
line names those seats even when another seat was compared by xpub: on a
card that keys only seat @0, a forged md1 with the right xpub at @0 and
another wallet's at @1 and @2 ends ``… compared at seats @1 and @2 by
fingerprint only …``, and so does the honest md1, since the run cannot
tell them apart (this goes beyond the spec's "When no seat was compared
by xpub", a spec-thread item). Two cards that carry no xpub keep the bare
pass after a fingerprint compare.
When the run also ends with an untied seat's line, that line stays last
and either text is printed before it as a warning (``warning: the md1
card was compared by template and origin paths only: …``). The parentage
form (`--hot-record`) prints either text as a warning too, before its
own pass line. A seat one card keys that the other
neither keys nor declares a fingerprint for is refused, not passed:
``member (0, 0) cannot be compared with the md1 card at seat @<i>: the
md1 card carries its xpub, and this card neither keys it nor declares
its fingerprint``.

**Seat-key lines, no card.** `mr verify --network <net> --seat-lines
<lines> --seeds …` is the check of an heir whose card is sealed or with
an executor (see `mr create`, Heir card modes): it reads no card and
checks the heir's own seeds against the "seat key:" lines in their kit
notes (`seat key: seat @2 (letter C): origin path m/270028'/1'/2', xpub
tpub…, fingerprint 6d4a0b8f`; the hot seat's `seat key: seat @1 (hot
child 24/0 of seed A, letter K): …`). `<lines>` is a file (read up to 1
MiB; a larger one is refused), or the text itself when no file of that
name exists; only lines that begin "seat key:" are read, so the whole
kit notes may be given, and with none it fails ``no seat-key line
given`` (for a value that is no file, ``no seat-key line given: `--seat-lines`
names no file that exists here, and its value holds no line that begins "seat
key:"; check the file name``; for an existing file with no such line, ``no seat-key line given: the
file `--seat-lines` names holds no line that begins "seat key:"; only a sealed or executor-mode
heir's kit notes carry them: with the card, check a seed with `mr verify MR1_CARD
--network N --seeds "THEIR_SEEDS" --keys "KEY_1" "KEY_2"`, KEY_1, KEY_2 and so on the plates of
the seats those seeds do not fill``). Every printed command quotes it, `--seat-lines
"THEIR_KIT_SEAT_LINES"`: a seat-key line holds spaces, `(` and a `'`,
which unquoted end the command or open a shell quote; a file name (the
kit notes saved as a file) is the safer value, since lines pasted onto
one line are not read as several. For each line it takes the given seed of the line's letter (for
the hot seat, the hot child's own words, of the child's letter); when
two given seeds have that letter, the line's fingerprint picks one, and
without a fingerprint the line passes when either agrees. It derives the
xpub at the line's origin path and prints ``seed <n> → seat @<i> (agrees
with its seat-key line)``; it fails on ``seat @<i>: seed <n> derives
another xpub: a wrong seed or passphrase, or a line copied wrong (`verify`
takes no passphrase: if your seed has one, compare the xpub your signer shows at
the line's origin path with the line)``, ``seat @<i>, letter <L>: no given seed
has this letter; give this kit's own seed for this letter (its ms1 plate is
labelled <L>)`` (when a given seed has the line's fingerprint and derives its xpub, whatever its letter, it says so instead: ``…no given seed has this letter; seed <n> (letter <M>) has the line's fingerprint and derives its xpub: the card, or this copy of its line, gives seat @<i> the wrong letter <L>, and that seed's letter is <M>: `mr decode MR1_CARD` with this run's `--network` prints the card's seat map (MR1_CARD the card's strings; a sealed heir's card stays in its bag: ask the creator); if the card gives seat @<i> letter <L>, the creator mints the card again with `--seat-map` giving that seat letter <M> (and that seat holder's `--role` with letter <M>), and replaces it in every kit before any deposit; if it gives <M>, this copy of the line is mistyped: give the line as the kit prints it``; on a line with no fingerprint, ``seed <n> (letter <M>) derives the line's xpub``) (for the hot seat: ``…; seat @<i> is a hot child: give the hot
child's own words (from its ms1 plate in your kit), not its parent seed's``), a
fingerprint the line gives that no seed of its letter has (with the same
passphrase sentence), and an xpub or an origin-path coin type of another
network than `--network` (``…: give the network of the kit's card (a kit with
no card, an executor-mode heir's, names it on its `network:` line)``); these come
first, over every line, and then a line that no given seed agrees with
fails once for the seed its fingerprint picks, or, without one, once per
seed of its letter. A given seed that agrees with no line, whatever its
letter, is skipped, not failed, with ``seed <n> (letter <L>) agrees with
no seat-key line: not checked here; if it is one of your kit's seat
seeds, a seat-key line is missing`` on stdout: the lines list only the
kit's seats, so a missing line is the one case this check cannot see,
and the card form (with the whole card) is where a stray seed is
refused. A passing run ends ``verify: passed for the seeds these lines
name``. A line that begins "seat key:" but does not read as one is named by its
number, never quoted, with the part that fails (``its xpub does not read (a
character copied wrong; its checksum fails): compare it with the kit notes,
character by character``; the origin path, the seat number against the path,
the fingerprint, the letter and the hot-child words are named the same way); so is one whose seat number differs from its
origin path's (a path of the form `m/270028'/<coin>'/<32r + i>'` names seat
i) or whose hot-child words do not read "hot child \<len\>/\<i\> of seed
\<P\>, letter \<C\>". A seed plate whose language is not recorded is
searched as the card form searches it, with the line's fingerprint in
place of the card's, or on a line without one its xpub (see Keys and
seeds). A card, `--keys`, `--keys-file`, `--digest`, `--set-id` or
`--against` given with `--seat-lines` is refused (exit 1), with the
message ``` `--seat-lines` reads no card: give it with `--seeds`
alone ```. It proves the heir's seeds, not the card, which the creator's
own `mr verify` proved before sealing the bag. While `mr verify` takes
no passphrase, the holder of a seed with one leaves it out and compares
the xpub their signer shows at each line's origin path with that line.

**Parentage form.** `mr verify <mr1> --network <net> --hot-record
<L>/<len>/<i>[=<word>] --hot-key <record> … --hot-parent-passphrase
yes|no [--hot-parent-fp <fp>] --seeds … [--passphrase <n>=…]` is the closing sitting's check
of a hot child minted from its public record (see `mr create`, Minting
from the hot child's public record): `create` prints this command in its
closing checklist with the record's values filled in. It takes the card,
the mint's record (the hot child's one `--hot-record` value, its word 1
optional, and no tried values; its `--hot-key` records at the paths the
mint required, one at another path refused), the history inputs as the
mint was given them (`--sibling` and `--bundle-card`, each with its
`--sibling-keys`, `--retire-child`, `--compromised`,
`--first-hot-child`, `--hot-on`), and seed L with every other seed your
role lists that has no passphrase of its own (a seed with its own
passphrase is given by its seat's plate or record in `--keys`).
With `--hot-parent-passphrase yes`, seed L's passphrase is asked for on
