table and each row's meaning, the scan sizes, the card length (``card length: 70 characters, `mr1` included, in 1 string``, or for a chunked card ``card length: 550 characters, `mr1` included, in 6 chunks: 5 of 93 and 1 of 85``: each chunk's length in chunk order, as runs of equal lengths, `mr1` counted in; every kit's digest line ends with the same words, so whoever holds a kit can count an engraving against it), the set id and card digest,
the role table (each seat's typed `@j` beside its stored `@i`, and each
spending path it is on: `alone` or `with @1 + @2` when every key of the
path signs, `2 of @0, @1, @2` or `@0 + 1 of @1, @2` for a quorum, and
`one of @0 + @2 or @1 + @3` when no threshold fits, cut after eight sets
with their count; past 4,096 signing sets a `multi` still prints
`8 of @0, …, @15` and any other path `a quorum over @0, … (too many
signing sets to list)` (a `thresh` of single keys with more than 4,096
spending paths is refused before the slot table, at slot 0: "the member
has more than 4,096 spending paths … write a k-of-n of single keys as
`multi(k, …)`"); its last line
repeats the keys as records for `mr verify` and `mr upgrade`, each
double-quoted (`--keys "@0=[587b44d1/270028'/1'/0']tpub…" "@1=…"`) so the
line pastes as printed in bash, zsh, sh and fish, while
`member`, `address` and `export` take each seat's key from its mk1
plate or seed, and `member` and `address` take a record only to confirm
a seed), the card,
the (0, 0) stub and md1 card, the bundle list, every kit with its notes,
the `ms encode` line for every kit ms1 plate (``to mint it, run `ms encode --phrase -`, type seed A's words on its standard input and end with Ctrl-D on a line of its own (end of input, not an empty line; they are not printed here)``; a hot child's plate reads ``type the hot child's words (child 24/0 of seed A, letter K) on its standard input and end with Ctrl-D on a line of its own (end of input, not an empty line; they are printed on stderr with the hot-child line, not here)``), the `mk encode` command for every kit mk1 plate (one per seat, naming
the kits that hold it: ``kit mk1 plate for seat @1: `mk encode …` (kits
bob, carol: one plate each)``) and for each extra plate its `mk encode`
command, its label (``extra plate, set <id>, seat @1, seed A, sc:…: keep away
from seed A``, the `sc:…` its seat's seat check as the kit notes print it, to
give as `--seat-check <id>:@1=sc:…` where no kit holds the value) and its
placement line (``extra mk1 plate for seat @1 (seed A): store it with the
creator's copy of seed B's ms1 plate, never with seed A's; …``, which ends
``; this plate's seat check, with seed B, tests seed B's passphrase offline:
use a strong passphrase`` where the seats seed B fills and the extra plates
stored with it are every seat of the card, since the label's value, kept with
seed B's ms1 plate, then tests seed B's passphrase offline there as the card's
fingerprint does (§1a); a one-seed card, whose extras go to places of their own, has no such ending),
the spending cost, the
signer notes and, last, a closing checklist (the `mr verify` steps, the
Jade registration cap where Jade can register and sign
the set, the Coldcard line (a Coldcard signs as the first registered
wallet with the same keys, so a member it refuses with "spk mismatch" or
shows under another member's name is signed from that member's own
wallet menu or by wallet name over USB, one member per PSBT), migrate on
spend, on a card with an untied seat possible the seat-check step
(below), and, last, the signer check, once for each shape: ``before the first deposit to
shape 0 (id 3f2a9c01), register shape 0's policy on a signer and check its first `mr address
--shape 0` on the screen``, a `--root` run's checklist adds the lines of "Root-mode texts"). Each kit's notes say the card's network
("network: testnet; every `mr` command for this card takes `--network
testnet`") and when each path of member (E, 0) opens, E the first epoch
receivable at the run's tip ("member (0, 0), the first receivable at
this run's tip: @0 any time; @1 from height … (≈ …)"); and what re-mints
a lost card ("to re-mint this card if it is lost (`mr create` is
deterministic: the same inputs give the same card, set id …; check the
re-minted card with `mr verify MR1_CARD --network testnet --digest …`
before you use it: … is this kit's digest, never the one the re-mint
itself prints): the card does
not record its inputs, so keep with this kit the horizon in years
(`--horizon 20`, or ``(no `--horizon`: 10 years, the default)``), the key of every cold seat (`--keys`: its mk1 plate where one was minted, else its key
record; a seed never supplies a cold seat's key, and the hot seat's comes
from the hot child; a seat whose declared fingerprint came from its seed,
§2, by its mk1 plate minted with that fingerprint, else by its record with
that seed given in `--seeds` from the kit that holds it, never stored with
this one; on a template typed out of first-appearance order the clause adds ``; on this template the typed numbering is not the stored one: give each mk1 plate or seat-key record `@j=` by its seat's typed number, the `(typed @j)` of its kit line, not the number the line leads with (a line without `(typed @j)` has the same number in both)``) and these: …", then the template (`` `--template-preset
owner-heir` `` when the run took one, else ``the template
(`wsh(multi(2,@0,@1,@2))`)``, a template over 300 characters as ``the
template as it was given to `create`: keep it with this kit``), the
epoch axis flags, the slot table
(`--preset classic` by that name, else the `--slots` rows, or ``a card of
slot 0 alone (`create` takes `--preset none`; it has no default slot
table)``), the roles (each `--role`), on a run with an heir the heir card
modes (``the heir card modes (`--heir-card bob=executor:carol`; an heir
role not named is sealed)``, or ``the heir card modes (no `--heir-card`:
an heir role not named is sealed)``), which change no card byte but
change every heir's kit, the seat map (`--seat-map`, the
letters the run seated when none was typed), `--own-wallet`, the seats' rounds (each `--round`), `--no-fingerprints`,
the bundle by its entries' flags (``the bundle (2 entries:
`--bundle-card`, `--bundle-account`)``; none for a bundle of the hot
child's entry alone), the hot child (`` `--hot-child A:24` with `--hot
@1` ``, then, when the run had a history input for it, ``, hot child
24/0 of seed A: re-mint with `--first-hot-child A` ``, each of the run's
`--first-hot-child`, `--retire-child`, `--hot-on` and `--compromised`
values in its flag, and "the cards given as `--sibling` or
`--bundle-card`, never a later card" when it took any, joined by ", "),
the tip and the release (`` `--height H --time T`, this run's tip, with
`mr` <version>: re-mint at that tip with that release, kept with this
kit; a later release may refuse that tip, a mainnet tip below its anchor
with no override``), the consent flags the run was given (each with
its values, in the order `--open-slot`, `--accept-deadline`,
`--small-delta`, `--accept-tip`, `--same-seed`, `--lockup-slot`,
`--lockup-no-window`, `--reorder-slot`, `--far-slot`, `--accept-set-end`,
`--accept-anchor`, `--accept-typed-letter`, `--accept-stub-mismatch`,
`--hot-cold-seed`, `--heir-holds-hot-path` (only when the kits' hot-path
decision needed it); not `--accept-12-word` or
`--accept-seed-language`, which name a seed by its position in `--seeds`, shape no
byte of the card and would refuse a keys-only re-mint), `--check-all-epochs` when given, and last
"`--sibling` with the cards on these paths (with `--sibling-keys` for a
keyless md1), or `--no-other-cards-on-these-paths`"); a slot whose
unmarked row is open at that epoch needs `--open-slot` at hand-out, and
its note says so. A path with a relative lock opens at each deposit's
confirmation plus its wait, so its opening line adds "for a deposit at
the tip: each UTXO's confirmation + N blocks". Without the tip
(`--height`, `--time`) `create` and `upgrade` exit 2, a usage error.
A bare `sh()` template holds only what Bitcoin Core scans there: a plain
`pk`, `pkh`, `multi` or `sortedmulti` body with no lock (the nested
`sh(wsh(…))` and `sh(wpkh(…))` are not bare, and take locks as `wsh()`
and `wpkh()` do). Any other body is refused before any row, ``slot 0: the
template's bare `sh()` body is not a plain `pk`, `pkh`, `multi` or
`sortedmulti`, so Bitcoin Core cannot scan it: write the template in
`wsh()` `` (`upgrade`, of such an md1: ``…: `upgrade` makes no card of
it; move its funds to a set whose template is in `wsh()` ``); a lock row
on a plain one is refused, ``` `--slots` line 1 (slot 1): its bare `sh()`
body would not be a plain `pk`, `pkh`, `multi` or `sortedmulti`, so
Bitcoin Core could not scan it: a bare `sh()` takes no lock; write the
template in `wsh()` ``` (`upgrade`: ``…; use `--preset none`, or move to
a set whose template is in `wsh()` ``), and `--preset classic` on it ```
`classic` gives this template no locks: use `--preset none`, or write it
in `wsh()` to add locks ```. With `--preset none` a plain bare `sh()`
mints, and an md1 `sh(pk)`, `sh(pkh)`, `sh(multi)` or `sh(sortedmulti)`
wallet upgrades. A bare `sh()` path's spending cost counts its whole
scriptSig at 4 WU a byte (the signatures, the redeemScript and their
pushes): `sh(multi(2,…3))` 1,188 WU, 297 vB; `sh(multi(1,…2))` 748 WU,
187 vB; `sh(pk(@0))` 600 WU, 150 vB; `sh(pkh(@0))` 696 WU, 174 vB. A
nested `sh(wsh(…))` or `sh(wpkh(…))` path counts its 35- or 23-byte
scriptSig the same way beside the witness: `sh(wsh(pk(@0)))` 414 WU,
103.5 vB; `sh(wpkh(@0))` 364 WU, 91 vB.
`--preset classic` on a template with no `older()` repeats slot 0 in rows
1-14, and with one lock-holding unit row 16 is row 15 (spec §5b keeps
them); `create` and `upgrade` both warn (never refuse), and say why the
warning matters: "`--preset classic` repeats rows on this template:
slots 1-14 repeat slot 0, slot 16 repeats slot 15; a repeated slot has,
at every epoch, the same spending paths and locks as the slot it repeats
at that epoch, on other keys of the same seats, so funds moved to it from
that slot within one epoch can be spent by the same seats at the same
times, though it looks like a different wallet; the heir scans every
repeat like any other slot (the default scan: \<D\> descriptors for 17
slots, \<time\>, of which 2 slots are distinct rows); spec §5b keeps these
rows as they are: `--preset none` gives one slot, and `--slots` a table
whose rows differ".
`create`'s scan times are `export`'s estimate (the quick window and the probe
span over as many calls as `export` would make), so the two verbs agree. Slots
whose spending cost is the same print one block, "spending cost of members
(0, 0-14):" with a deposit line for the group. A `--role` that lists a letter no
seat uses ends "…; the seats of this card use letters A, B, C: a seed's letter
is the block its word 1 falls in (`letter X: word 1 …`)".
Every 12-word seed, every typed letter nothing confirms and every seat
colliding with a `--sibling` is named in one refusal ("seeds 1 and 2
have 12 words: … pass `--accept-12-word 1 --accept-12-word 2` only for a
seed whose word 1 came at random"). Every `mr`
command `create` and `upgrade` print for the new card (the checklist's
`mr verify` steps, the typed-letter and extra-plate checks, the order of
a move, the plate check of a non-English seed, and ``choose your
receiving epoch with `mr current MR1_CARD --network signet --height
HEIGHT --time TIME --lead LEAD` ``) names the card as `MR1_CARD` (a plain
word in every shell, where `<mr1>` would read a file) and the run's
`--network`, so it runs as printed once its placeholders are filled in.
Every placeholder is in capitals (a `--seeds` placeholder double-quoted, since the seed's words that replace it are the shell's to split; a `--keys` value is one key, so the checklist prints `"KEY_1" "KEY_2"` and says they stand for one quoted value per seat or per extra plate, not several keys in one pair of quotes, which `--keys` refuses) (`--seeds "SEEDS_YOU_HOLD" --keys
"KEY_1" "KEY_2"`, `--seeds "EVERY_SEED_BUT_B" --keys
"EXTRA_PLATE_1" "EXTRA_PLATE_2"`, `--seeds "SEED_B,SEED_C"`, `--seeds "THEIR_SEED" --keys
THEIR_KIT_PLATES THEIR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS` (the kit's plates bare, a device record quoted:
`--keys THEIR_KIT_MK1_PLATES "THEIR_DEVICE_RECORD" THEIR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS`; the co-signer and open-heir checklist line adds ", leaving `--keys` and THEIR_KIT_MK1_PLATES out when their kit holds no mk1 plate," where such a kit needs no device record (a co-signer whose kit holds every seed, on a card with fingerprints), since a bare `--keys` is a usage error; the typed-letter check prints `--keys` only where the holder's kit holds an mk1 plate or needs its device record, and `--seeds "THEIR_SEED" THEIR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS` alone, where the holder's kit holds no mk1 plate and needs no device record (on a card with fingerprints, a kit that holds every seed), since a bare `--keys` is a usage error; a sealed or executor-mode heir's kit is not counted, as it runs no such line; one line for several kits that differ prints `--keys` and adds ", leaving `--keys` out when their kit holds no plate and no value,"; THEIR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS, and EXECUTOR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS in an executor's lines, stand for the kit's `--seat-check` arguments, each with its flag, and stay bare: a value holds no space and no quote), `--seeds MS1_PLATE`, `upgrade`'s sweep `--keys
MK1_PLATES`), never `<…>`, which is a redirection in every shell; so is
a flag in a refusal's remedy whose value you fill in (`--seed-language
1=LANGUAGE`, `--keys @1=KEY`, `--funded-address ADDRESS --index I`,
`--accept-12-word N`, `--child N=A/24/0`). A seed named by its letter
is `SEED_B`; one named by its position in `--seeds` (`export`'s stems)
is `GIVEN_SEED_1`; a remedy whose seat is to choose names it `@j` (or
`@i`) and capitalizes the value (`--round @j=R`, `--word1 @j=WORD`,
`--word1 @i=WORD` in `upgrade`). Only a grammar keeps `<…>`: a synopsis,
alternatives (`<12|24>`), optional parts, or the statement of a value's
form (`expected <r> or @j=<r>`, `@i=<key>`). The remedies that named a
value in `<…>` are capitals too: ``member needs the keys: --keys MK1_PLATES
or --seeds SEEDS``, `` `--first-hot-child A` needs `--hot-child A:WORDS`
(WORDS: 12 or 24) ``, ``a target is an entry (`held:PURPOSE:N`,
`L:PURPOSE:N`, `L/WORDS/I:PURPOSE:N`; …) or a member (`[PREFIX:]E/S`, …)``
and ``… `--next K:PREFIX:E/S` ``, and `` `AB` is not a letter A … P,
`LETTER#FINGERPRINT` or `hot` ``. A print that
names a seat carries the typed `@j` beside the stored `@i` when they
differ. Seed words, a seed's word 1 and the hot child's words go to
stderr only; passphrases are never printed, and a passphrase typed at
the prompt is read with the terminal's echo off (where the terminal
cannot turn it off, as in mintty, a warning says so before the
question). A refusal comes before any of this is printed.

A given seed whose letter is a seat's but that derives no seat's key (a
wrong seed, or a missing or wrong `--passphrase`) is refused, exit 1
(`seed <n> (letter <L>, fp <fp>) derives no seat's key: a wrong seed or
a wrong passphrase? drop it or fix it`); `upgrade` refuses it the same
way. Without `--seat-map`, a seat that no given seed derives has no
letter; when a given seed derives no seat, it is named rather than
`--seat-map` offered (which would mint a letter nothing checked): ``seed
<n> (letter <L>, fp <fp>) derives no seat's key, and seat @<j> has no
letter: if seed <n> is seat @<j>'s seed, its passphrase is missing or
wrong (give `--passphrase <n>=…`); if not, give seat @<j>'s seed in
`--seeds`, or `--word1 @J=WORD` ``, J the seat's number in the numbering the template was typed in (`--word1` takes the typed number, not the stored one; `upgrade` the md1's). A `create` with no key for a seat is refused with ``create needs a key for every seat: --keys @j=KEY … (KEY: an mk1 plate or a [fingerprint/path]xpub record), in the numbering the template was typed in, …``; with keys given, a seat with none is refused ``typed @1 (stored @0) has no key: give it as `--keys @1=KEY`, KEY its mk1 plate or the `[fingerprint/path]xpub` record its signer exports at m/270028'/1'/0'; `mr` never generates a seed`` (``@1 has no key: …`` when the typed number is the stored one); and `address`, `export` and `member` run without keys or seeds with ``address needs the keys: --keys MK1_PLATES or --seeds SEEDS`` (`export needs the keys: …`, `member needs the keys: …`), every placeholder in capitals. A key given without its `@j=` is
refused with the form: ``plate <k>: create takes every key as
`@j=KEY`, in the numbering the template was typed in: `--keys @0=KEY
@1=KEY` `` (`upgrade`: ``… upgrade takes every key as `@i=KEY`, in the
md1 card's numbering: `--keys @1=KEY` ``, one `@i=KEY` per seat the md1
does not key). A seat the md1 does not key is refused without a key even when a seed is given: ``@1 has no key: the md1 card carries no xpub for it, so give it as `--keys @1=KEY`, KEY its mk1 plate or the `[fingerprint/path]xpub` record its signer exports at m/48'/1'/1'/2'; a seed given in `--seeds` checks a key's letter and supplies no key: `upgrade` keeps the md1's keys`` (with no seed given, the refusal ends "`mr` never generates a seed"). At a seat the md1 declares `00000000` for, KEY reads ``its mk1 plate or the record its signer exports at m/48'/1'/1'/2', written `[00000000/48'/1'/1'/2']XPUB` with 00000000 in place of its fingerprint, as the md1 declares``; a record with its real fingerprint there is refused ``…: its key does not satisfy seat @1 (the card declares fingerprint 00000000 for it): give it as `[00000000/48'/1'/1'/2']XPUB`, the record its signer exports at m/48'/1'/1'/2' with 00000000 in place of its fingerprint``, and the `00000000` record that passes draws no "give a record with the seed's real fingerprint" warning there, since the card keeps the md1's `00000000`. For each seed given with `--passphrase`, the closing
checklist adds a records-only step, since `mr verify`'s card form takes
no passphrase yet; it follows every `mr verify --seeds` line it covers (the
extra plates' `EVERY_SEED_BUT_<L>` runs included): ``seed <n> (letter
<L>) has a passphrase, which `mr verify`'s card form cannot take yet: leave seed <n>
out of `--seeds` in every `mr verify` run above and confirm seat @<i> by
its device record in `--keys`; its holder runs `mr verify MR1_CARD
--network <network> --keys THEIR_KIT_MK1_PLATES "THEIR_DEVICE_RECORD"
THEIR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS` at their kit`` (for the hot child's parent: ``… in every `mr verify` run
above give the hot child's own words in place of seed <n> (they fill hot
seat @<i>)``; a parent that also fills a cold seat gets both clauses). A given seed with a seat's master fingerprint whose key at that
seat's path is not the key given for it is refused, exit 1: ``seed <n>
(letter <L>, fp <fp>) has seat @<j>'s fingerprint, but its key at <path>
is not the one given for that seat: the key was exported at another
path, or with another passphrase; export the key at <path> again and
give it with `--keys @<j>=` ``. In `upgrade`, for a seat whose xpub the
md1 carries (which `upgrade` keeps, so `--keys` cannot replace it), the
text ends ``… is not the md1's key for that seat: the md1 was made from a
key at another path, or with another passphrase than the seed was given;
`upgrade` keeps the md1's keys, so check the seed and its passphrase, and
that this md1 is the wallet the seed belongs to``, and where the seed
derives another seat's key ``… has seat @<i>'s fingerprint, but it
derives seat @<k>'s key, not seat @<i>'s: the md1's fingerprint for seat
@<i> may be the wrong one; see "If a seat's key is in doubt" in `mr --help` ``.
In `upgrade`, once the
letters are known and before any line of the card, a given seed that
derives a seat's key, at a seat the md1 keys or not, and whose master
fingerprint differs from the one the md1 declares for that seat (neither
`00000000`) is refused, exit 1, at the lowest such seat, a fingerprint the
md1 declares for another seat compared too: ``seat @<i>: seed <n>, which
derives its key, has master fingerprint <F>, but the md1 card declares
<C> for it: the md1 was encoded with a wrong one, and signers match keys
by master fingerprint, so a card declaring <C> would fail at every
signer: re-encode it, giving <F> for seat @<i> and every other seat its
declared fingerprint: `md inspect MD1_STRINGS` …`` with md's route filled
in, as `mr upgrade` describes below (an md1
without the Fingerprints TLV is not refused: the card carries the seed's
fingerprint, with a warning). When the letter-reuse check finds such a seed of a sibling seat's
letter, its warning adds ``seed <n> fills no seat of this card: if it is
that seat's seed, its passphrase is missing or wrong (give
`--passphrase <n>=…`)``. When that seed is the hot child's parent, given with
no passphrase, and the given card's seat of its letter declares another
fingerprint, `create` warns first ``warning: hot child 24/<i> is derived from
seed <n> with no passphrase, but set <id>'s seat @<j> has letter <L> with
fingerprint <fp>: if that is seed <n>, its passphrase is missing, and
this child is the child of seed <n> with no passphrase, which its words,
as printed, spend: anyone holding seed <n>'s words or plate can now
derive this child without the passphrase; rerunning with `--passphrase
<n>=…` gives the child of seed <n> with its passphrase, a child the
passphrase guards`` and goes on, exit 0 (the card's
seat decided by a key, not a fingerprint: ``… has letter <L> and a key seed
<n> does not derive: …``); the line above follows. Given with a passphrase
that the card's seat disproves (a mistyped one), it warns first ``warning:
hot child 24/<i> is derived from seed <n> with the passphrase given here
(master fingerprint <fp>), but set <id>'s seat @<j> has letter <L> with
fingerprint <fp′>: if that is seed <n>, the passphrase given is wrong, and
this child is another seed's: rerun with the right passphrase`` (``… and a
key seed <n>, with that passphrase, does not derive: …`` when a key
decides), and goes on, exit 0; the passphrase is never echoed. After that
warning the creator's kit notes leave out the parent's fingerprint line
(``seed <L>, with its passphrase, has master fingerprint <fp>``), which the
rerun with the right passphrase prints. When the seed's own BIP-85 child fills the hot
seat (a hot parent sharing a seat's letter), `upgrade` refuses it as
`seeds of letter <L> are told apart only by fingerprint, and the hot entry
names its parent by letter: choose a parent whose letter no other seed
has`. A given seed whose letter no seat takes, and that no bundle entry or
`--role` names by letter, is refused too: ``seed <n> (letter <L>, fp <fp>)
fills no seat of this card, and no bundle entry or role names letter <L>:
a wrong seed, a wrong passphrase, or another card's seed? drop it, or
name it with `--bundle-account` ``.

A seed's non-child bundle entry covers that seed only, not its BIP-85 children: the printed bundle list says so in full at the seed's first entry (and the first `held` entry), and its later entries say what they cover and point back (``covers seed A only, not its BIP-85 children (the caveat is printed at the first such entry above)``). A seat in a refusal of the family "seat N, letter L" is written `@N`, as the rest of the output numbers seats (``seat @3, letter K: no seed and no plate given: give seat @3's seed with `--seeds` or its mk1 plate with `--keys` ``).

Every message `mr` writes, in every verb (a refusal, a warning, clap's
own usage error, a template's parse error), quotes a value you typed only
when that value parsed as a public typed value: an `mr1`, md1 or mk1 card
string, an `xpub`/`tpub`, an 8-hex-digit fingerprint, a 16-hex-digit set id or
card digest, an `export --next` digest prefix (8 to 16 hex digits), a
derivation path (at most 255 components) or a number that fits its flag
(at most 20 digits); a verb, a flag name, a row operation, a
language name or one of a flag's listed values; a `--role` name or hot
device name that `create` took; or a template, printed from its parsed
form. Anything else is named by its flag and position, never quoted:
`` `--role` value 2: unknown role (creator, heir, cosigner or helper) ``,
`` `--slots` line 3: operation 2 is not a row operation ``, `` `--slots`
line 4 (slot 2): the row has 1 operation for 2 units (`mr units` lists
them) ``, `invalid
value for '--min-lead <L>' at position 5 (not echoed): …`, `unexpected
argument at position 21 (not echoed)`. A line that says "not echoed"
never holds the value: a number out of a flag's range is refused with
the range only (`not in 0 … 4,294,967,295`; `--index` says `not in 0 … 2,147,483,647` for any value past its range), however it was typed (a
leading `+` or zeros included); a value with stray whitespace is no number and keeps the parser's `invalid digit found in string`. Every typed value that is
quoted has a bounded shape, so a secret pasted into its slot is never
quoted back: a digest prefix longer than 16 digits (a card digest has
16) is a usage error (exit 2), and a longer number is named by its
count of digits. A file is named by its flag, not
its path (`` `--slots`: cannot read the file ``, `` heir file written
(the `--heir-file` path) ``). A template's parse error names the bad part by its
character (`template: at character 17: …`) and keeps the parser's own
words only when none of them comes from the template; a value with no
`(` is named by what it looks like (`seed words (24 words, not
echoed)`, `an extended private key`, `a bare word`). The report itself
(cards, descriptors, digests, the plan) is printed as it is. What is
shown on purpose, the only exceptions: the seed words and word 1 that
`create` and the read verbs print on stderr, the hot child's words, the
nearest list words a `--word1` refusal suggests, and the `--role` and
device names `create` took. `repair` prints the characters it corrected
(`'was' -> 'now'`), as the spec requires. A `--role` name and a hot
device's name are printed on the kits, so `create` bounds them: a name
may hold at most 5 words and 40 characters, and no word longer than 12
characters. Its words are the pieces between spaces, `-`, `_`, `.`,
apostrophes or any other punctuation (`bank-box-7` is 3, `Alice's
phone` is 3), with words of a BIP-39 list run together counted one by
one, past any stray character among them (`camp1calm` is 2), and each
Chinese character counted one. A longer name is refused, never echoed:
`` `--role` value 1: the name is too long (not echoed): a name may hold
at most 5 words and 40 characters, and no word longer than 12
characters; … ``. So `alice`, `home-safe`, `aunt-mary`, `ledger nano`,
`Alice's phone`, `Ledger Nano S Plus`, `Papa's Trezor Model T`, `李明`
or `张伟的手机` are fine, and `the blue ledger nano in the fire safe at
the lake house` is refused, as is a single word of more than 12 letters
(write it in two, with a separator): keep a name short.
A name that is key-shaped is refused by its kind whatever its length:
an extended private key, a WIF, an ms1 string or hex of 32 digits or
more (with `0x` or in groups). A `--role` name is checked before
`create` asks anything on stdin; the device's name is the last question
and is checked as soon as it is read, so a refused device name means
answering the questions again. The bound is about a name's size, not a
promise about every secret: a name it takes shows at most 5 words of a
phrase as typed, each a whole list word or a Latin list's 4-letter
prefix, glued or not; a stray or mistyped character in a glued run
hides none of the run's other words, which are counted past it. What
the bound does not count are words typed with a mistake or cut short below 4 letters (not list
words): glued into tokens of at most 12 characters, a label of 40
characters can hold 12 3-letter words each with one letter wrong or cut to their first 3 letters. A
name taken is printed on the kits and in a refusal that names the
participant (`role carol=helper lists seeds`). The command line itself
(shell history, the process list) is outside `mr`'s reach.

With `--hot-child <L>:<12|24>` it derives a BIP-85 child of seed L (given
in `--seeds`) as a cheap hot wallet, and with `--hot @j` that child fills
the hot seat too. A hot-child run needs a history input for seed L: your
newest card with a child of L (`--sibling` or `--bundle-card`),
`--retire-child`, or, only for L's first hot child, `--first-hot-child L`.
When the only given card naming a child of L is one seed L does not
derive (seed L given without its passphrase, with a wrong one, or another
seed of letter L), the refusal names it and the passphrase, and offers no
`--first-hot-child`: ``seed <L>'s hot-child history is missing: set <id>'s
child entry <L>/<len>/<i> is not seed <n>'s child, so it is no history for
seed <L>: seed <n> is not that card's parent, or its passphrase is
missing: give it with `--passphrase <n>=PASSPHRASE`; give the right seed
and passphrase, or your newest card with a hot child of seed <L>
(`--sibling` or `--bundle-card`)`` (``… or the passphrase given for it is
wrong; …`` when one was given). `--first-hot-child L` beside such a card
is not refused (it is no history input), but warned of on stderr: ```
`--first-hot-child <L>`: set <id>'s child entry … is not seed <n>'s
child: if that card is yours, seed <n> is not its parent or its
passphrase is missing (…), and this run makes a child of another seed
than that card's; stop and check seed <n> and its passphrase ```, and the
card's history line reads ``set <id> given on other origin paths; its
child entry … is not this seed's child, so it is not seed <L>'s hot-child
history``. Beside the child's words `create` prints ``this child is
derived from seed <L> with its passphrase: …`` only when seed L was given
one; otherwise ``this child is derived from seed <L> with no passphrase:
if seed <L> has one, anyone holding seed <L>'s words or plate can derive
this child without it: rerun with `--passphrase` for the child of seed
<L> with its passphrase, a child the passphrase guards; …``. A
`--hot-on` naming a card not given, a card also given as `--compromised`
(``set <id> is compromised``) or a card with no hot seat (``set <id> has no hot
seat``, with `--hot`, or without it when the card has no child entry at
all) is refused as such before the history
check and before any passphrase is asked for. With `--seeds`, a seed that derives a seat's key while the record
given for it carries another master fingerprint is warned of
(``typed @<j>'s key record gives master fingerprint <fp'>, but seed <n>,
which derives its key, has <fp>: the card carries <fp>; …``). A record
or plate whose fingerprint is `00000000` and whose seat a given seed fills
is declared with that seed's fingerprint on a card with fingerprints, and
`create` (and `upgrade`, for a seat the md1 does not declare) prints, in
place of the `00000000` warning, ``seat @i (typed @j): the key's
fingerprint 00000000 is replaced by seed <n>'s <fp>; mint any mk1 plate of
this seat with `mk encode --origin-fingerprint <fp>`, as the `mk encode`
lines printed here do, never from the record``; never for an md1's
declared `00000000` (which `upgrade` keeps) or the hot seat. On such an
`upgrade` the role table, its `--keys` records and the seat's `mk encode`
line carry the `00000000` the card keeps, so a plate minted as printed
satisfies the card's seating rule.
When the search lands the child on a given card's cold seat, the refusal's
remedy is `--retire-child L/24/i`, and beside `--first-hot-child L` it
says to pass it in place of that attestation. It asks on stdin, after the template's own refusals (the guardrails and
the spending cost), in this order: a no-seat parent's passphrase a second
time (when the parent fills no seat and has one), `is set <id> your own
card …?` for each given card whose parent it cannot confirm (answer
`yes` or `no`), and, unless `--hot-on` is given, the name of the device
that will hold the child (a free label). With no answer to the passphrase
or the device name it refuses; no answer to the own-card question counts
as no. Without `--hot-on` it prints ``load child <len>/<i> of seed <L> on
a device that holds no other `mr` hot child; to serve several sets from
one device, use `--hot-on` `` beside the words, writes the device line
``device <name>: child <len>/<i> of seed <L>; sets <id>`` into the
creator's kit notes and prints ``device <name>: if an earlier line names
this device, copy its children and sets into this one``. With `--hot-on`,
in either form, the child is already on a device: it prints no words, no
load line, no device line and no merge line, asks no name, and prints, on
screen and in the creator's kit notes, ``child <len>/<i> of seed <L> is
already on a device: load nothing; add set <id> to each device line in
your kit notes that lists child <len>/<i> of seed <L>`` (a record run
given tried children adds ``, and add `; tried, not used: <L>/<len′>/<j>, …`
to it``, the device line's own tail); its other lines beside the words stay, the "shares
child" line among them. It prints the
reservation of indexes 0 … 15 before the child's words, the child's path
and index, the cheap hot wallet's descriptor and first addresses (BIP 86,
84, 49 and 44), and puts the hot device check in the closing checklist.

After a hot device is lost or stolen (§7a), give each compromised card
with `--compromised <set-id>` and as `--sibling`. A compromised card
given as `--bundle-card` is listed on the new card's bundle for good,
with a warning (``set <id> is compromised and is listed on this card's
bundle for good (§3); §7a step (3) gives a compromised card as
`--sibling`, not `--bundle-card` ``); either way `create` prints the
compromised line for it, by what the card holds of the run's hot child's
parent (`--hot-child A:…`): on a card whose hot seat that parent's child
fills, ``set <id> is compromised: move every funded member by §7a
(deadline: its hot path's opening, or now when that path is open
already)``; on a card whose child entry of that parent fills no seat (a
cheap-wallet-only card), ``set <id>'s hot child is compromised: its
members stay yours, since the child spends none of them; sweep its cheap
hot wallet (§7a steps (2) and (3))``; and on a card with no child entry
of that parent, ``set <id> is compromised: move every funded member by
§7a soon, by a path that does not need the lost child, and by its
deadlines at the latest (it has no hot path of that child, so no
race)``. The first two go on with the cheap hot wallet the thief can
drain at once (``; the thief can drain the cheap hot wallet A/24/0:86:0
at once: see what it holds with `mr export MR1_CARD --network testnet
--seeds "HOT_CHILD_WORDS" --child 1=A/24/0 --height 1000000 --time
1900000000 --next 0:A/24/0:86:0`, MR1_CARD being the strings of
compromised set <id>'s card and HOT_CHILD_WORDS the lost child's own
words, re-derived from seed A with its passphrase, if it has one, by
BIP 85 at m/83696968'/39'/0'/24'/0' on a clean signer, never taken from
the lost device's backup (§7a)``), the hot-seat form ending ``, and
sweep it first (§7a step (2))``. The run's network and tip are filled
in (run later, give a fresh tip); the child's own words, declared with
`--child`, scan it whatever the parent's passphrase, which `export`
cannot take. The `--for-spend --compromised` runbook's cheap hot wallet
line carries the same command with its own run's tip. When several
compromised sets share a device, its cheap hot wallet is named once: a
later set's line leaves out the scan clause (and, on the hot-seat form,
``, and sweep it first``). `create` names
the compromised card's retired child once (when several compromised
sets share a device, one child reads ``A/24/0 (the hot child of
compromised sets X, Y)``). The creator's kit notes carry ``set <id> is
compromised: never pass `--hot-on <id>` `` for each compromised card, or
``set <id> is compromised`` alone for a card with no child entry, which
`--hot-on` refuses anyway.

A `create` or `upgrade` run with `--hot-child` resolves and checks its
`--hot-on` and `--compromised` values (a malformed id, a given card's card
digest, a card that is not given) before it reads the seeds, so no
passphrase is asked for first.

A `--seat-map` letter that disagrees with the seed deriving the seat's key
names a hot child by its parent (``seat 0: `--seat-map` says letter D, but
child 24/0 of seed 1, which derives its key, has letter C; see "If a seat's key is in doubt" in `mr --help` ``),
never as "seed 0".

A given md1 is named by its own id, not as a set: ``md1 <id> given on other origin
paths …``, with the order of a move keeping it ``until a scan of its descriptor finds
nothing (`mr export` reads an mr1 card, not an md1)`` (an md1 has no digest to verify).

The letter check compares no `00000000`, in `create` or `upgrade`: two
seats that declare or carry it may take any letters (equal real
fingerprints still need one letter, and different ones different letters).

At a seat a card with fingerprints declares `00000000` for and does not key
(a `[00000000/…]` record given with no seed in `create`, or an md1 that
declares it in `upgrade`), a wrong typed letter cannot be corrected at
recovery either, since recovery compares no `00000000`: `create` and
`upgrade` refuse such a letter no given seed checks, ``seat 1: letter B
typed, not checked against a seed (block `avoid` … `cable`): on this card a
wrong letter cannot be corrected at recovery; give its seed, or pass
`--accept-typed-letter @1` `` (no `--word1`, which lifts nothing there; on
a card without fingerprints the remedy reads "give its seed or `--word1`,
or pass …"), and the seat's "seat map unverified" check names the record
in the form the card declares, whatever the holder's kit values, since no
seed and no `--seat-check` value fills such a seat: ``the holder of that
seed must run `mr verify MR1_CARD --network testnet --seeds "THEIR_SEED"
--keys THEIR_KIT_PLATES "[00000000/270028'/1'/1']XPUB"
THEIR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS`, XPUB the xpub their signer exports at
270028'/1'/1', the record written with 00000000 in place of its
fingerprint, as the card declares, at their kit before the first deposit;
on this card a wrong letter cannot be corrected at recovery``. When a
given seed checked such a seat's letter, the re-mint note names its
`--accept-typed-letter`, which a re-mint without that seed needs.

A consent flag nothing needed is said, not taken in silence: ``warning: `--accept-typed-letter
@0`: seat @0's letter is checked by given seed 1; the flag changes nothing`` (when no given
seed checks it and the card declares a fingerprint other than `00000000` for the seat, `--word1` given or not, ``warning:
`--accept-typed-letter @0`: this card carries fingerprints, so a wrong letter of seat @0 can
be corrected at recovery; the flag changes nothing``; at a seat the card keys and declares
`00000000` for, on a card with fingerprints, ``warning: `--accept-typed-letter @0`: the card
carries seat @0's xpub, which seats its seed whatever its letter; the flag changes nothing``
(on a card without fingerprints a keyed seat's consent is needed and taken); on a card with no fingerprints, when
`--word1 @0=…` gives the letter, ``warning: `--accept-typed-letter @0`: seat @0's letter is
given by `--word1 @0`; the flag changes nothing``; and for the hot seat of a record run on
such a card, whose letter the device record gives, ``warning: `--accept-typed-letter @0`:
seat @0's letter is given by `--hot-record`; the flag changes nothing``),
``warning: `--hot-cold-seed`: no seed sits both at a seat that alone
spends within the short-delay threshold and in a multi-key quorum; the flag changes nothing``,
and, for `--reorder-slot`, `--far-slot` or `--open-slot` when no row needs it, ``warning:
`--far-slot`: no row of this card needs it at `create`; the flag changes nothing`` ("at
`upgrade`" in `upgrade`). A value of `--accept-typed-letter` or `--hot` written without its
"@" is read as the seat (`--accept-typed-letter 1` is `@1`), and a consent so written that the
run needs prints ``note: `--accept-typed-letter 1` read as @1: seat @1, not seed 1``, in
`create` and `upgrade`. The `--far-slot` refusal leads with each far path, ``the @1 path opens
5.4 years later than at slot 0 (≈ 2040-01-20), epoch 0``, before the row's opening dates.

A key that fills no seat is named for what is wrong with it: a bare xpub is refused ``an xpub
without its `[fingerprint/path]` origin: give the device's full record, or its mk1 plate``; the
same key bound to two seats, ``it is the same key as typed @0's: one key fills one seat; give
each seat its own key``; a key of an earlier round, ``a round-0 key: typed @0 (stored @1) is
at round 1, whose path is …`` (the typed and the stored numbering both, when they differ). An
empty seed (a stray comma in `--seeds`) is ``seed 2 is empty: a stray comma? remove it, or give
the seed's words``, and an ms1 plate of a length no ms1 plate has says so (``the ms1 plate has
N characters, and no ms1 plate has that many: a character is left out or added; …``).

With `--same-seed`, a seed that fills enough seats of a path to meet
its quorum alone is named: `seats @0 and @1 hold one seed (equal master
fingerprints), which alone meets the quorum 2 of @0, @1, @2: whoever
holds that seed spends there with no other signer`. A group is named
at a path only when the fewest signatures it needs to sign there alone
are two or more: a group one of whose seats signs alone is not named.
The check runs past 4,096 signing sets too, and with a fingerprint
unknown it takes each largest group of seats that are pairwise one
seed.

**The ms1 restore note.** `create` and `upgrade` print, once, in the
notes of every kit that holds an ms1 plate (a seed's, or the hot
child's own), and in no other kit, how to put a seed on a signer and
how to read an ms1 plate by hand, word for word:

> to put a seed on a signer, enter its words, never its ms1 string: decode the plate to its words offline (on the offline computer, in a shell started with history off, `ms decode -`, then type the plate's string and end with Ctrl-D on a line of its own (end of input); never the string on the command line, which ms-cli has refused since 0.17.0 after the shell has saved it; or by hand, below) and use the signer's import by words (a Coldcard takes 12, 18 or 24 English words; a 15- or 21-word seed, or one in another list, needs another signer). Never use a Coldcard's Import Codex32, or any codex32 import, on an ms1 plate: it reads an ms string as a raw master secret, not as words; Coldcard refuses these plates' lengths, and a string it took would restore another wallet. By hand: ignore spaces and hyphens, read upper and lower case alike, drop the first 9 characters (`ms10entrs`) and the last 13 (the checksum); read each other character as 5 bits by its place in `qpzry9x8gf2tvdw0s3jn54khce6mua7l` (q = 0, l = 31); join them, read bytes of 8 bits and drop the 0 to 4 bits left over (all 0s). A first byte of 00: the rest is the seed's entropy, read in the BIP-39 English list unless this kit's notes give a language for this plate's letter (then decode with `ms decode --language LANGUAGE -`, type the plate's string and end with Ctrl-D, LANGUAGE the language those notes give). A first byte of 02: the second byte names the list (0 English, 1 Japanese, 2 Korean, 3 Spanish, 4 Chinese simplified, 5 Chinese traditional, 6 French, 7 Italian, 8 Czech, 9 Portuguese) and the entropy starts at the third byte. Never write the words in another list. The entropy is 16, 20, 24, 28 or 32 bytes for 12, 15, 18, 21 or 24 words; read its bits 11 at a time, each a word's place in the list, counting from 0. The last word is short: its remaining 7, 6, 5, 4 or 3 bits are the start of its place, and the rest of its place is a checksum that needs a computer (the first 4 to 8 bits of the entropy's SHA-256); without one, use the signer's last-word tool where it has one: from a Coldcard's list of valid last words take the one word whose place starts with those bits, or, for 12 or 24 words, give a SeedSigner's Calc final word those bits. Checking the plate's own checksum also needs a computer. Where a plate carries a Standard SeedQR, a signer that scans one (SeedSigner, Coldcard Q) reads each English word as its place in the list, counting from 0, written as 4 digits ('12' as 0012).

A `0x00` plate kept with `--seed-language` adds, in every kit that
holds the seed, "give the plate of letter \<L\> as `--seeds PLATE
--seed-language N=<language> --accept-seed-language N`, N being its
position in `--seeds`; without `--seed-language` `mr` derives another
seed", \<L\> the seed's letter.

### Shapes (`--shapes`, `--room`)

`--shapes <file>` gives the card's shapes in place of the positional template and its table
flags (it is exclusive with them): one block per shape, the blocks separated by an empty
line, a line that starts `#` a comment, every other line `key value`. The keys are
`template <template>` (typed in card seats) or `preset <name>` (a template preset; exactly
one of the two), `slots none|classic|<rows file>` (required: there is no default slot table),
and, as `create`'s flags of the same names for this shape, `epochs` (no value),
`delta-blocks N`, `delta-seconds S`, `anchor-height A`, `anchor-time A` and
`own-wallet s,…`. Block k is shape k; a card holds at most 8 shapes, a shape at most 32 slots
and a card at most 256 slots in all. A fault names its line and block (``` `--shapes` line 3
(block 1): `foo` is not a block key: template, preset, slots, epochs, delta-blocks,
delta-seconds, anchor-height, anchor-time, own-wallet ```).

Shape 0's template uses exactly the card seats `@0 … @(n₀−1)`, so `upgrade`'s md1 stays member
(0, 0); a later shape's template names any card seats, and its own `@j` map to them. `create`
sorts a `sortedmulti` node's keys and the tap branches into the card's canonical order and
numbers each shape's own `@j` by first appearance. Every shape's guardrails run against its
own slot 0. The hot seat is in exactly one shape (T13, exit 1: ``seat @3 is the hot seat and
appears in shapes 0 and 1``); cold seats are in any number. Shapes need the path convention.

`--room <n>` reserves n slots, past the shapes' own, for later shapes (fold J): the chain of
shape k, epoch e, slot s is `2·(S_total·e + σ)`, σ the shape's slot base plus s, so the room
moves no member. `create` reserves 16 by default, `upgrade` 0. A card with one shape and no
room is written without a shape table.

On a card of several shapes the plate stubs are per shape (the Shapes row of the Conventions
table), and the `(0, 0) stub` line lists them. Every `member`, `address` or `current` line that
`create` or a kit sheet prints carries its `--shape k`.

### Root mode (`--root`)

Root mode makes a new seed set's own seeds from one or more roots, and is the recommended
route (the sentence that opens this section). Every step that types a root (`mr root`, `mr
child`, `create --root`) runs on the offline computer: a `--root` run's report starts ``root
mode: run this only on the offline computer, in a shell with history off, and end with the
wipe line``. The order of a whole sitting is the procedure "The first sitting", and the
checks that fail are "When create refuses a root" and "When a day-0 check fails" ("Root-mode
procedures", above).

**Giving the roots.** `--root -` is repeatable, one root per occurrence in command-line order.
Anything but `-` after `--root` is a usage error (exit 2) that names the value by its place
only (the texts under "Root-mode texts"), and so is a root's passphrase as an argument.
`create` reads stdin line by line: the per-seed prompts first, then, for each root, the root
itself (its 24 words on one line or several, or its ms1 string, to the first empty line), the
checks that need only the root (the root-form refusal, T4, T4m, the published-tail
refusal), its passphrase and the repeat (T7 on a mismatch; T22 after a non-empty one), and
then the rest of the questions (a no-seat parent's second passphrase, the own-card
questions, the device's name). A refused root asks no passphrase. Only English roots are
read, as 24 words or one `0x00` ms1 string of 32 bytes. `--no-fingerprints` is refused in a
`--root` run (T2), since a wrong root passphrase gives children with the right letters and
other keys.

**The seat map.** `--seat-map` is required in a `--root` run (T16) and gives every cold
seat's letter. Its `hot` token names the hot seat, a plain word that no shell expands; `create`
accepts it, in any case, in every run that has a hot seat, with or without `--root`, only at
the `--hot` seat's position (elsewhere, or in a run with no hot seat, it is a usage error,
exit 2: ``` `--seat-map`: item 2 is `hot`, but that seat is not this run's hot seat ```). The
hot seat's letter is then the one the search takes, as `create` prints it, and the re-mint
sheet prints `--seat-map` as given (``--seat-map B,D,hot``), so the re-mint parses it again
and gives the same digest. A seat is a root child when it is not the hot seat and its letter
is in a root's mask. The root itself fills no seat (T5), no root makes a co-signer's seed (T6)
and no letter is made by two roots.

**Keys and the day-0 signer check.** A root child is a seed like any other: its words come
from `mr child` on the offline computer, and its key comes from the signer those words are
loaded on (with no passphrase), given to `create` with `--keys`; `mr child`'s words are never
given to `create` as a seat's key. `create` re-derives every child and checks each root-child
seat's key against it (fingerprint and xpub; a `00000000` record is compared by xpub and takes
the child's fingerprint). A mismatch is refused: T20c beside a matching child seat of the same
root, T20r when no child seat of the root matches (with the causes, in order: the root words or
passphrase differ from `mr child`'s, the network differs, or the signers hold other words, a
passphrase or each other's words, and the remedies of "When create refuses a root"). The
mask checks refuse a mask letter no seat takes (T11m, two forms), two roots that share a
letter, and a `--seeds` seed whose letter is in a mask and is not that child. Every refusal
ends with the root-refusal tail: nothing is minted, keep the paper, destroy nothing.

**What `create` prints.** Per shape, which seats each root makes, ``root 1's children alone
spend shape 0 at once`` and ``after <delay>, root 1 alone spends shape 0's <path>`` where
true; with one root, the one-root line; ``root 1 has now made letters B and D; it makes no
other card``; the plate lines of the heirs' children (and the hot child's, where a role lists
`hot`) on stderr's seed-print channel; the kit lines; the checks (T8 and the closing
checklist); the root's passphrase line; and, once every check has passed, the root's ms1
string to engrave. Every secret print goes to stderr only. There is no root question and no
record `mr` reads back. The root plate is engraved only after `create` accepts it, as the ms1
string `create` prints for it; `mr root` prints no ms1 string, so nothing is engraved before.

**The plates and kits.** The root plate is an ms1 string, labelled either with the default label
or, with `--root-label-set-id`, with the plain label that carries the set id and the warning
T24 (both under "Root-mode texts"). The root plate stands in for the ms1 plates of every letter
the creator lists that a root made, so the creator's kit holds the card, each root's plate at
a place apart from the card and from the first card's kit notes, the first card's kit notes
kept with a card copy, a second card copy at the extras' place where the run mints extras,
and an mk1 plate for every seat whose letter she does not list. In a `--root` run every extra
plate goes to the extras' place, apart from every root plate and every ms1 plate of the
creator's. The kit notes carry the root-plate note, the index line, the T8 line, the root
rehearsal line, the offline-guessing note, the layout note, the history-off, wipe and destroy
lines, the J11 line, the re-mint sheet and the later-card line; "Recovery without mr" is
printed as the no-`mr` sheet after the root-plate note. An heir's kit holds the heir child's
own ms1 plate (``for <name>'s kit: letter <L>, no passphrase``) beside the sealed bag as for any
heir; co-signers' kits are unchanged. Later cards follow today's rules: root-made seeds are
ordinary seeds given by their words or keys. Rotation, replacement and loss are fold J's.

**Not covered.** K-of-N shares of a root (they wait on ms1's K-of-N), and a root given to any
read verb but `mr child`.

### Minting from the hot child's public record

In place of seed L, `create` (and `upgrade`) can take the hot child's
public record, read from the child on seed L's signer:
`--hot-record <L>/<12|24>/<i>=<word>` (the parent letter, length and
index, and the child's word 1, whose letter is computed as `--word1`
does; repeat it for each child tried and not used, the highest index at
`--hot-child`'s length being the hot child), `--hot-key` with the child's
`[fingerprint/path]xpub` records (its BIP 86, 84, 49 and 44 accounts and
the hot seat's path), `--hot-parent-passphrase yes|no` and, when the card
has a seat of letter L other than the hot seat, `--hot-parent-fp <fp>`
(seed L's master fingerprint, eight hex digits in either case, printed in
lower case). A value without its word, a missing `--hot-key` or
`--hot-parent-passphrase`, a malformed record and a malformed or repeated
`--hot-parent-fp` are usage errors (exit 2). Seed L in `--seeds` beside
its record is refused: ```
`--hot-record` takes the place of seed <L>: give seed <L> in `--seeds` or
its hot child's public record, not both ```. `--hot-parent-passphrase
yes` mints: the closing sitting's `mr verify` takes seed L's passphrase
(see `mr verify`, Parentage form).

The record is checked online, refusing (exit 1) with, among others: ```no
`--hot-record` value is at `--hot-child`'s length <len>```; ``tried child
<len′>/<j> of seed <L> is above hot child <len>/<i>: …``; the paths
(for a record at another path, ``` `--hot-key` record <n> is at <path>, a path
the record does not use: give the hot child's records at <paths> only ```; for
a path with none, ``` `--hot-key` has no record at <path>: give the hot child's
record at each of <paths> ```, or, for a `--hot-on` card's hot seat path, ```
`--hot-key` has no record at <path>, set <id>'s hot seat path: that card
carries no fingerprint there, so its hot child is confirmed by its key:
give the hot child's record at <path> ```) and one master
fingerprint across the records, not `00000000`. Then the parent's
fingerprint: ``` `--hot-parent-fp 00000000` decides nothing: give seed
<L>'s master fingerprint as its signer shows it ```; ``this card has
seat @<j> of letter <L>, the hot child's parent's letter: give
`--hot-parent-fp <fp>`, seed <L>'s master fingerprint with the passphrase
the child was made with, read from seed <L>'s own signer or plate, so
that `mr` can check that seat's seed is seed <L>``; ``seat @<j> has
letter <L> but fingerprint <fp′>, not seed <L>'s <fp>
(`--hot-parent-fp`): another seed of letter <L>, or seed <L> with
another passphrase, fills it, and the hot entry names its parent by
letter: choose a parent whose letter no other seed has, or read
`--hot-parent-fp` again from seed <L>'s own signer or plate (never from
this seat), and check the passphrase`` (in `upgrade` ending ``; `upgrade`
keeps the md1's hot child, so this md1 cannot be upgraded from this
record: move its funds with `create` ``); ``seat @<j> has letter <K> but
seed <L>'s fingerprint <fp> (`--hot-parent-fp`): one seed has one letter:
check seat @<j>'s letter (`--seat-map` or `--word1`) and
`--hot-parent-fp` `` (``set <id>'s seat @<j> …`` for a given card's
seat). With `--hot-parent-fp` and no parent passphrase, a given card's seat of letter L whose real fingerprint differs is warned of, not refused (a given card may hold another seed of that letter), as seed-mode `create` warns, unless a seat of letter L on this card carries that fingerprint, which proves the parent is that seat's seed: ``warning: hot child 24/<i> is derived from seed <L> with no passphrase (master fingerprint <fp>, `--hot-parent-fp`), but set <id>'s seat @<j> has letter <L> with fingerprint <fp′>: if that is seed <L>, its passphrase is missing, and this child is the child of seed <L> with no passphrase, which its words spend: anyone holding seed <L>'s words or plate can derive it without the passphrase; derive the child from seed <L> with its passphrase and mint again``. A seat of letter L whose fingerprint is not known (`00000000`) is
warned of and the run goes on: ``seat @<j> has letter <L>, the hot
child's parent's, but no fingerprint known here: its seed cannot be told
from seed <L> online; the closing sitting checks it with seed <L>; to
check it now, mint with seed <L> in `--seeds` in place of `--hot-record` ``
(``… upgrade with seed <L> in `--seeds` …`` in `upgrade`). In `create`,
these texts name this card's seat as every `create` text does, ``seat
@<j> (typed @<k>)`` when the template was typed with another number for
it, as check (f)'s below; `upgrade` names the md1's seat by its number
alone. Not another
seed's keys: ``the record's fingerprint <fp> is seat @<j>'s (seed <K>):
these keys are seed <K>'s, not child <len>/<i> of seed <L>: load the
child on the signer before exporting its keys`` (``… is seed <L>'s own
(`--hot-parent-fp`) …`` when it is the field). The fingerprint
cross-check against the given cards: ``the record of hot child
<len>/<i> is the hot child of compromised set <id>: this is the thief's
key: make a new child above the floor`` (in `upgrade` ``…: `upgrade`
keeps the md1's hot child, which is the thief's key: move this md1's
funds with `create` now``), and ``the record of hot child <len>/<i> is
set <id>'s hot child <len′>/<i′> by <fingerprint|key>: these are that child's
words, not child <len>/<i>: give the words of child <len>/<i>`` ("by
key" when the given card carries no fingerprint at its hot seat and its
key there is the record's). The
letter test: ``hot child <len>/<i>'s letter <K> is seat @<j>'s on this
card: make child <len>/<next> on seed <L>'s signer, and give child
<len>/<i> too, as tried``, \<next\> being i + 1 (at i = 15, ``…: no
reserved index is left above 15: give another cold seed as parent``). The floor, every given card naming L counting:
``hot child <len>/<i> of seed <L> is at or below the floor <f> of seed
<L>'s children, both lengths (<cause>): make the child at an index above
<f> on seed <L>'s signer``.

There is no search and no words: the record's index must pass the letter
test and lie above the floor. The hot-child line reads ``(made on seed
<L>'s signer: this run has no words)``; the passphrase line ``this child
was made from seed <L> with no passphrase: if seed <L> has one, stop and
make the child again with it; the same seed with a passphrase gives
another child``; the device line names the tried children (``device
<name>: child <len>/<i> of seed <L>; sets <id>; tried, not used:
<L>/<len′>/<j>``). `create` prints ``hot child <len>/<i> of seed <L> taken
from its public record, not checked against seed <L>: run the closing
checklist's parentage check before the first deposit``, and, without
`--hot-on`, ``hot child <len>/<i>'s letter <X> comes from the word given
in `--hot-record` alone, which no online check can confirm: the closing
sitting's `mr verify --hot-record` with seed <L> checks it`` (with
`--hot-on` the named card's entry checks the letter online), and its closing
checklist gains the parentage step, with the record's values filled in
(each `--hot-key` record double-quoted, the tried values and word 1 left
out): ``before the first deposit, check hot child <len>/<i>'s parentage
with seed <L> at a sitting (child fingerprint <fp>, BIP 86 first address
<addr86>): `mr verify MR1_CARD --network <net> --seeds "SEEDS_YOU_HOLD"
--keys "KEY_1" "KEY_2" --hot-record <L>/<len>/<i> --hot-key "<record>" …
--hot-parent-passphrase <yes|no> [--hot-parent-fp <fp>] SIBLING_CARDS
<history values>` (MR1_CARD this card; SEEDS_YOU_HOLD seed <L> and every
other seed your role lists that has no passphrase of its own, a seed
with its own passphrase given by its seat's plate or record in `--keys`;
KEY_1, KEY_2 and so on the plate or record of each seat your kit holds
one for, the hot seat's mk1 plate included, one quoted value each;
SIBLING_CARDS this run's `--sibling` and `--bundle-card` cards, each
after its flag and with its `--sibling-keys`)``, SIBLING_CARDS and its
gloss left out when the run was given no `--sibling` or `--bundle-card`
card; with `--hot-parent-passphrase yes`, the gloss ends ``; give seed
<L>'s passphrase when `mr` asks for it, twice, or with `--passphrase
<L>=PASSPHRASE`, PASSPHRASE its passphrase, which then stays in the
shell's history, and type it again if `mr` asks``; from an `upgrade`
with `--hot`, `--against MD1_CARD` follows `--network <net>` and the
gloss adds ``MD1_CARD the md1 card this card upgraded;``. That check is `mr verify`'s parentage form.
A record run without `--hot-on` writes in its kit notes the ``newest hot
child of seed <L> known to this create: <len>/<i>, set <id>: …`` line.

**Sharing the cheap wallet.** `--hot-on <set-id>` without `--hot` (the
sharing form, `create` only; `upgrade` refuses it as a usage error)
names the given card's cheap hot wallet on the new card: the card takes
no hot seat and writes the named card's child entry, from seed L or from
a record. A named card with no hot seat is admitted by its entry
(``set <id> has no hot seat: its child <len>/<i> is taken as this one by
its entry alone, unconfirmed (a child letter matches 1 time in 16 by
chance); the closing sitting checks it with seed <L>``), once the
own-card question is answered yes; a no is refused, ``set <id> is not
counted as your own card: the sharing form names only a card that is
seed <L>'s history: answer yes only if it is, or drop `--hot-on <id>` ``.
A new card with no hot seat given a history card prints the hint ``to
name set <id>'s cheap hot wallet on this card in place of a new child,
pass `--hot-on <id>` (the sharing form)``.

### Heir card modes

Whoever holds the card and every seat's xpub can find every address and
balance of the set, and a card cut down to the paths one heir is in
cannot be made. So each heir role has a card mode, `--heir-card
<name>=sealed|executor:<helper>|open`, `sealed` when not given (`upgrade`
takes it too):

- **sealed** (the default): the heir's kit holds their ms1 plates, and a
  tamper-evident bag holds the card, the kit's mk1 plates and a sheet of
  the kit's re-mint lines. The kit listing says so (`sealed in a
  tamper-evident bag: the card, the mk1 plates above and a sheet of the
  re-mint lines`), prints the bag's label (``sealed: the mr1 card of set
  <id> (digest <d>) and 2 mk1 plates, for heir bob: open it only to
  recover this set's funds; first check that the seal is unbroken, and if
  it is broken tell the creator; once open, run `mr verify MR1_CARD
  --network <net> --digest <d>` first, then `mr export MR1_CARD --network
  <net> --seeds "THEIR_OWN_SEED" --keys THEIR_KIT_MK1_PLATES
  THEIR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS --height HEIGHT --time TIME` to find this set's
  funds, HEIGHT and TIME the chain tip's height and time when you run it,
  THEIR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS the `--seat-check` arguments on the sheet in this
  bag, each with its flag, as the sheet prints it (a seed with a passphrase cannot
  fill its seats in `export` yet, as `mr` takes no passphrase: leave it
  out of `--seeds`, and drop `--seeds` when every seed you hold has
  one)``) and the sheet's text, and the re-mint lines leave the kit
  notes. On an `upgrade` run the label reads "… and 2 mk1 plates and the
  (0, 0) md1 card, for heir bob …". When the bag holds no mk1 plate of a
  seat the heir's seeds fill (the card keys it with no xpub), the
  parenthesis goes on "; this bag holds no mk1 plate of seat @2: for each
  such seat a seed with a passphrase fills, give that seat's mk1 plate in
  `--keys`, the creator's extra plate of it if the creator kept one, with
  the value on its label, or the mk1 plate of it in a helper's, a
  co-signer's or another kit that holds one, with that kit's `--seat-check`
  value for the seat, returned to where you took it afterwards; without it
  `export` cannot find that seat's funds until `mr` takes passphrases". Its privacy line reads "whoever opens
  this kit's bag can see every address and balance of this set, now and
  in future, as with the heir file: open it only to recover". The
  creator's kit notes gain "heir bob's bag: seal it after your `mr
  verify` runs, write its serial here (________), and check the seal
  whenever you see that kit; if the seal is broken or the bag is lost,
  whoever opened or holds it can see every address and balance of this
  set, though it spends nothing: make a new bag (the card from your kit;
  the mk1 plates of the seats your seeds fill minted again from your
  seeds at an offline sitting with `mk encode --xpub XPUB
  --origin-fingerprint FP --origin-path "PATH" --policy-id-stub 0aeaa2ea`,
  XPUB and PATH from the `[fingerprint/path]xpub` record each seed's
  signer exports at its seat's path (the hot seat's from the hot child's
  record at that path, as `--hot-key` takes it), FP 472e815c for seat @0;
  or the plates copied from a helper's or co-signer's kit or an extra
  plate, never from another heir's sealed bag; a co-signer's seat's plate
  from that co-signer's own sitting; a new re-mint sheet, with the values the kit notes give that heir's kit
  (each untied-possible seat it holds no seed for, as `--seat-check
  <id>:@I=sc:X`), X as `mr verify --keys` prints it for the new plates,
  compared with the seat holder's), run `mr verify` on them, seal it,
  write its serial here, and if later deposits must stay hidden, move
  the funds to a new set (§7a)". The stub is the run's (0, 0) stub, filled in, the kit-plate lines' S; FP is filled in for each seat the creator's seeds fill, the hot seat included (the hot child's), as the fingerprint the card carries for it ("FP 472e815c for seat @0 and bb5fc9ca for seat @1"), `00000000` where the card keeps an md1's declared `00000000`, since only a plate carrying it fills that seat. On a card without fingerprints the command reads `mk encode --xpub XPUB --privacy-preserving --origin-path "PATH" --policy-id-stub 0aeaa2ea`, "XPUB and PATH from the …" with no FP clause (only the form that applies is printed). On an `upgrade` set the plates clause
  adds ", or, on an `upgrade` set, the plate of seats @0 and @1, which that set's (0, 0) md1 keys, with `mk encode --xpub tpub… --origin-fingerprint 472e815c --origin-path "m/48'/1'/0'/2'" --policy-id-stub 89296a23` for seat @0 and `mk encode --xpub tpub… --origin-fingerprint bb5fc9ca --origin-path "m/48'/1'/1'/2'" --policy-id-stub 89296a23` for seat @1, filled in from that md1" ("seat @0" for one; no clause when the md1 keys no seat, since a keyless md1 holds no xpub to mint from): each seat's command whole, the xpub the md1 carries and the fingerprint the card carries for the seat (`00000000` where the md1 declares it), as the kit plate lines print it, `--privacy-preserving` in its place only on a card without fingerprints, whatever the md1 declares. `mk` reads no md1 chunk set, and every keyed md1 is one, so the commands take the stub, never `--from-md1`, and the sheet "; and on an `upgrade` set
  the (0, 0) md1"; with no value for that heir the sheet is "a new
  re-mint sheet" alone. The creator's kit keeps no `mk encode` line for
  the bag's plates of its own seeds, which those seeds give again.
- **executor:\<helper\>**: the heir's kit holds their ms1 plates and no
  card and no mk1 plate (`card: none (the card and the mk1 plates are
  with executor carol)`), with the note "the card and the plates that
  find this set's funds are with executor carol: your seeds find and
  spend this set's funds only with that kit, or with the card of another
  kit and, unless the card carries every seat's xpub, its plates; with
  that kit, recovery is `mr verify MR1_CARD --network <net> --digest
  <d>`, then `mr export MR1_CARD --network <net> --seeds
  "THEIR_OWN_SEED" --keys EXECUTOR_KIT_PLATES EXECUTOR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS
  --height HEIGHT --time TIME`, MR1_CARD and EXECUTOR_KIT_PLATES the card
  and mk1 plates executor carol's kit holds for you,
  EXECUTOR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS the `--seat-check` arguments executor carol's
  kit notes give for set \<id\>, each with its flag, and HEIGHT and TIME the chain
  tip's height and time when you run it (a seed with a passphrase cannot
  fill its seats in `export` yet, as `mr` takes no passphrase: leave it
  out of `--seeds`, since that kit's plates fill its seats; when every
  seed you hold has one, give no `--seeds`: the plates still find this
  set's funds, and you spend them with your signer)" and the privacy
  line "this kit alone cannot find this set's funds: the
  card is with executor carol"; its digest line reads "card digest \<d\>:
  the card with this digest is with executor carol; when you have it,
  run `mr verify MR1_CARD --network <net> --digest <d>` on it first;
  card length: …" (every other kit's: "card digest \<d\>: this kit must
  hold the engraving with this digest; card length: …", the words after
  "card length: " on the run's own line);
  the helper's kit notes add "executor for
  heir bob, whose kit holds seeds and no card: their recovery starts
  with this kit: `mr verify MR1_CARD --network <net> --digest <d>`, then
  `mr export MR1_CARD --network <net> --seeds "THEIR_OWN_SEED" --keys
  EXECUTOR_KIT_PLATES EXECUTOR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS --height HEIGHT --time
  TIME`, MR1_CARD and EXECUTOR_KIT_PLATES this kit's card and mk1 plates,
  EXECUTOR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS the `--seat-check` arguments this kit's notes
  give for set \<id\>, each with its flag, THEIR_OWN_SEED the
  heir's seeds (one with a passphrase left out: these plates fill its
  seats; no `--seeds` when every seed has one, the plates still finding
  the funds) and HEIGHT and TIME the chain tip's height and time when it
  is run". The Kit plates lines and the total mk1 count name no
  plate for such an heir.
- **open**: the kit as before, card and plates loose, with the note "to
  find this set's funds, run `mr verify MR1_CARD --network <net> --digest
  <d>` first, then `mr export MR1_CARD --network <net> --seeds
  "THEIR_OWN_SEED" --keys THEIR_KIT_MK1_PLATES THEIR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS
  --height HEIGHT --time TIME`, MR1_CARD and THEIR_KIT_MK1_PLATES this
  kit's card and mk1 plates, THEIR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS the `--seat-check`
  arguments this kit's notes give, each with its flag, and HEIGHT and TIME
  the chain tip's height and time when you run it
  (…)", the parenthesis the bag label's passphrase clause.

A sealed or executor-mode heir's kit notes print, outside the bag, one
"seat key:" line per seat the heir's seeds fill (the hot seat when the
role lists `hot`), with the seat's origin path, its xpub and, when the
card carries fingerprints and `create` knows the seed's, its
fingerprint. The heir checks their seeds with `mr verify --network
<net> --seeds "THEIR_OWN_SEED" --seat-lines "THEIR_KIT_SEAT_LINES"` (see
`mr verify`), which reads no card. For a seed with a passphrase the
lines give the same offline test of passphrase guesses as the card's
fingerprints, and the kit says so: "this kit's card (in the bag) and
seat-key lines let a thief of this kit test passphrase guesses offline:
use a strong passphrase and never store it with the kit" in a sealed
kit, the same with "this kit's seat-key lines" alone in an
executor-mode kit or a sealed kit whose card does not count. An heir
whose role lists `hot`, in any card mode, is warned "heir bob's kit
holds hot child 24/0: it can watch and spend the cheap hot wallet
without opening the bag" (an executor-mode or open heir, who has no bag,
without " without opening the bag"). With `--no-fingerprints` the
consequences line ("nothing on the card confirms seats …") says after
"… or pass `--accept-unconfirmed-seeds`" that "nothing on it ties an mk1
plate to this wallet either: another wallet's plate of the same
template, at a seat's path, fills that seat, so `mr` reports a seat a
plate fills as filled, never confirmed, and `mr member` and `mr address`
refuse it without its `--seat-check`, which the kit notes give: compare
each plate's seat check with its holder's" (on an `upgrade` card keying
some seats, "nothing on it ties to this wallet an mk1 plate at a seat it
does not key: …"; left out on one keying every seat), and adds, per such
heir, "; heir bob's seat-key lines
still carry their seats' xpubs, an offline test of each passphrase to
whoever holds that kit (spec §1a)".

On a card with a seat it does not key (fingerprinted or not: a
fingerprint confirms a seed, never a plate), each kit that holds the card
gives, after its re-mint lines, the `--seat-check` value of each such
seat it holds no seed for
(a hot seat counting as held when the role lists its parent or its
child): ``set <id>, seat @1's seat check: `--seat-check <id>:@1=sc:…`
(give it to every `mr member`, `mr address`, `mr verify` and `mr
export`; compare it with the seat holder's before the first deposit)``;
a sealed heir's are on the bag sheet, after the re-mint note, and an
executor-mode heir's kit, with no card, has none. Each value is that
seat's seat check over every seat's key the run was given (see `mr
verify`), so its wallet half hashes the kit's own seeds' keys too: a kit
that holds a seed, whose notes carry such a value and which has no card
note above, adds "this kit's `--seat-check` values, with the keys this
kit holds, let a thief of this kit test passphrase guesses offline: use a
strong passphrase and never store it with the kit". Kit notes printed
before this release carry values of 20 hex digits, which every verb
refuses with a text that names the re-print (`create` or `upgrade` again,
from the kit's re-mint note, gives the same card and the new values). On a
`--no-fingerprints` card each co-signer's and heir's kit that holds a
seed adds "this card carries no fingerprints, so `mr` seats your seed by
the letter the card gives your seat alone: if `mr verify` with your seed
says of your seat "no seed and no plate given", of your seed "fills no
seat of this card", "derives no seat's key" or "give different keys", or
"no given seed fills a seat of this card", the card may give your seat a
wrong letter: tell the creator, who mints the card again with the right
`--seat-map` and replaces it in every kit; until then, recover with your
seat's mk1 plate from another kit that holds one", and, for each seat their seeds fill (the hot seat apart), "your own seat is confirmed only by your device record: export it from your signer at m/270028'/1'/1' (the `[fingerprint/path]xpub` record your signer gives) and give it to `mr verify` with `--keys`, beside this kit's mk1 plates", then once "your check: `mr verify MR1_CARD --network testnet --seeds "THEIR_OWN_SEED" --keys THEIR_KIT_MK1_PLATES "THEIR_DEVICE_RECORD" THEIR_KIT_SEAT_CHECKS`" with what each placeholder stands for (the card's strings, the holder's seed, this kit's mk1 plates, the signer's record, the `--seat-check` arguments this kit's notes give, each with its flag); `"THEIR_DEVICE_RECORD"` is printed only where the kit's notes give no `--seat-check` value, as the closing checklist gives that holder's command (a kit value confirms the holder's seat as the record does). Those two notes are for a holder with the card and plates loose, a co-signer or an open heir. A sealed heir's kit, whose card and plates are in the bag, gives instead, once, "your own seat is checked without opening the bag, by this kit's seat-key lines below: `mr verify --network testnet --seeds "THEIR_OWN_SEED" --seat-lines "THEIR_KIT_SEAT_LINES"`, THEIR_OWN_SEED your seed's words or its ms1 plate and THEIR_KIT_SEAT_LINES a file holding this kit's "seat key:" lines; for a seed with a passphrase, leave it out and compare the xpub your signer shows at each line's origin path with that line", and an executor-mode heir's kit the same with "checked without the card and the mk1 plates, which are with executor carol," in place of "checked without opening the bag,". When an executor's
kit holds the only mk1 plate of a seat (an owner-heir card whose heir is
in executor mode), the creator's kit notes add "executor carol's kit
holds the only mk1 plate of seat @0: if that kit is lost, mint another
with `mk encode …` and give it to executor carol", the seat's `mk
encode` line as `create` prints it, one line per such seat in seat
order; unless the kit's card note is already there, they then add, once,
"this kit's notes carry seat @0's key (its `mk encode` line) beside its
seed, which lets a thief of this kit test passphrase guesses offline:
use a strong passphrase and never store it with the kit" (for several
seats, "this kit's notes carry the keys of seats @0, @1 and @2 (their
`mk encode` lines) beside the seeds that fill them, which lets …").

A key value that begins "seat key:" (a seat-key line from a kit's
notes), or that is such a line copied from "seat @" on without its label, is refused by name in every verb (typed without quotes, its words arrive one by one, and `seat key:` or `seat @<n>` at the start is named a seat-key line to quote whole): ``key 1: this is a seat-key
line from a kit's notes, not a key: give it as the record
"@0=[fp/path]xpub"``, the record's `@j` the typed number in `create`,
the line's seat elsewhere. The record's fingerprint is the line's own. A line cut before its fingerprint takes the fingerprint its card declares for that seat only at a seat the card keys (its Pubkeys TLV gives the xpub, and the line's xpub must be that key), so the record offered back is confirmed by the xpub and never by the fingerprint alone. Everywhere else (no card in the run, a seat the card does not key, a line whose xpub is not the card's) it is refused and never given an invented `00000000` or offered as a record: ``key 1: this is a seat-key line from a kit's notes, not a key, and this line has no fingerprint: copy the whole line, which ends `, fingerprint` and 8 hex digits, unless its card was made with `--no-fingerprints`: a card made with `--no-fingerprints` prints its seat-key lines without one, so give such a line in a run on that card, or to `create` or `upgrade` with `--no-fingerprints` (`mr` takes no fingerprint for a line that it cannot check against a key on the card)``. A card made with `--no-fingerprints` prints none on its lines, and its records carry `00000000`, so there a cut line reads as the record with `00000000` (`create` and `upgrade` with `--no-fingerprints` too); such a record confirms nothing by itself. A line typed without quotes reaches `mr` as `seat`, `key:` and the rest, one value each, and is refused at `seat`: ``key 3: this is the start of a seat-key line from a kit's notes, which the shell split at its spaces: quote it whole, in one pair of quotes, and `mr` gives it as the record it spells``; a lone word that is no plate or record (`dave`) is refused as seed words (``this value looks like seed words (1 word, not echoed): …``). A sealed heir's bag sheet, when the kit's seeds
fill a cold seat, gives those records filled in, the xpub left out: ``…;
this kit's own seats' keys are its seat-key lines, given in `--keys` as
these records: `"@1=[4b79d969/270028'/1'/1']XPUB"`, `"@2=[6d4a0b8f/270028'/1'/2']XPUB"`,
