`--from` takes `coins` (237 coin flips, H or T), `dice` (at least 92 dice rolls, 1 to 6, 99
recommended) or `pills` (22 Entropia pill draws, one pill tossed per draw).

## `mr child`

Re-find a root's children, on the offline computer. It reads the root, its 24 words or its ms1
string, from stdin (a prompt on a terminal), never from an argument, by the grammar of `mr
root` and `create --root`: to the first empty line, the root-form refusal, T4, T4m and the
published-tail refusal before any passphrase prompt, then the passphrase and its repeat (T22's
root form after a non-empty one). A read verb: it mints nothing and the root is given to no
other read verb. It prints the root's fingerprint with this passphrase and the network and base
index on stderr only, reads the root's mask (`--letters` defaults to every mask letter; a
letter outside the mask is a usage error), prints ``this root makes letters <list>``, and
derives the whole chain for the network's coin.

**With a card** it prints neutral per-seat lines, no pointer, then for each `--letters` letter
its 24 words if matched (with `--check`, ``letter <L>: matched`` and no words; `--check` needs a
card), ``letter <L> is on no seat of this card`` (T17) or ``letter <L> (seat @<i>): not a child of
this root as read: no words printed`` if it failed, or its words under the "not checked against
this card" heading if the seat carries no fingerprint. A letter is matched when some seat with that
letter matches and no seat with that letter is checked and fails. **Without a card** it prints
each letter's words under "not checked against any card" (both cases always print: `mr child`
cannot tell a first card's sitting from another). Every words print goes to stderr only and ends
with the line that the children carry no passphrase: load them with none; every run ends with the
procedure-end wipe clause (and a `--check` run with the lone-check wipe line).

**Exit codes.** 0 when every letter checked matched (or, with no card, every letter's words were
printed); 1 when a letter failed, when nothing could be checked (``nothing to check: this card
carries no fingerprints``) or when no seat matched at all (the root words or passphrase differ
from those used at the sitting, or the network, with T23's sentence); 2 usage (a letter outside
the mask, `--check` without a card, a root or passphrase given as an argument). It has no
`--index`, `--seat` or `--expect-fp`, and no record. It is used at the sitting, for T8, for a
lost or replaced signer of the creator's (the J11 line) and for a re-mint with no card.

### Synopsis

```sh
mr child [OPTIONS] [MR1_CARD]...
```

### Flags

| Flag | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `[MR1_CARD]...` | The card (its mr1 strings), to check the children against |
| `--check` | Check the root's children against the card's seats and print no words (needs a card) |
| `--network <NETWORK>` | Network: main (default; `mainnet` is accepted too), signet, testnet or regtest [default: main] |
| `--letters <L,…>` | The letters to print or check, comma-separated (default: every letter of the root's mask) |

## Root-mode texts

The texts below are the ones the root-mode verbs print, word for word, each with the verb and
stream that print it. `<n>` is a root's place on the command line (`create`; 1 for `mr root` and
`mr child`), `<L>` and `<M>` letters, `<i>` and `<j>` seats, `<net>` the network and `<b>` the
base index. The examples in this chapter use no root: a published test root is refused by `mr`,
and a root typed to print one would be one anyone could copy.

### Lines every root-mode run shares

Each is printed word for word. A ``see "…" in `mr --help` `` ending is a procedure pointer: the name is one of the thirteen procedures above.

**The history-off line** (`mr root`, first; the kit notes, before the first `mr child` line):

> on the offline computer, before typing a root or any words: start a shell that keeps no history (bash or zsh: `unset HISTFILE` as its first command; fish: start it as `fish --private`; dash keeps none), or use a live system with no persistent storage

**The wipe line** (the last line of a sitting and the last step of every standalone procedure):

> wipe the offline computer: if its shell was not started with history off, close every shell first and only then delete its history file (bash writes the session back on exit); then delete every file this sitting wrote; a live system with no persistent storage is wiped by shutting it down

**The destroy line** (`mr root`; the last line of a `--root` closing checklist):

> destroy nothing until T8 has passed on each root's kept plate and the closing checklist's `verify` lines pass; then destroy this paper root, every paper copy of its children's words, the record of your flips, rolls or draws, for each root every plate of it but the one that passed T8, every heir plate that failed its check (grind or cut through every character), and any copy of an ms1 string `create` printed that is written on paper, photographed or saved in a file (never a plate this line keeps: each root's plate that passed T8, each heir's plate that passed its check, and the hot child's plate where a role lists `hot`): each is this root or a seed it made; see "The first sitting" in `mr --help`

**The procedure-end wipe clause** (the end of every `mr child` run, refused or not):

> do not wipe the offline computer in the middle of a procedure: at a first card's sitting it is wiped once, at its end, after the closing checklist; see "The offline computer" in `mr --help` says when each other procedure ends

**The lone-check wipe line** (a `mr child --check` run adds it to the procedure-end clause):

> after this check on its own (the yearly rehearsal): now: wipe the offline computer: if its shell was not started with history off, close every shell first and only then delete its history file (bash writes the session back on exit); then delete every file this sitting wrote; a live system with no persistent storage is wiped by shutting it down

**The children carry no passphrase** (the end of every print of a child's words):

> these words carry no passphrase: load them with none, and export their keys with none

**T22, root form: the root passphrase warning** (stderr, whenever a non-empty root passphrase is typed and its repeat matches (`mr root`, `mr child`, `create --root -`)):

> warning: passphrases are hard to remember over years. Forget this one and the root plate restores nothing: the seeds it made then live only on your signers (and heirs' plates), and are lost when those signers are; nothing can recover it, not `mr`, not a plate, not a kit. `mr` writes it nowhere: keep it apart from every plate and every kit, and rehearse typing it every year (your kit's yearly `mr child --check` line tests it)

**T22, seed form: the seed passphrase warning** (stderr, whenever `create` or `upgrade` is given a seed's own non-empty passphrase):

> warning: passphrases are hard to remember over years. Forget this one and seed \<n\>'s plate restores nothing (another wallet), and on most signers, which ask for it at each start, this seat can no longer sign: its coins then spend only through the card's other paths, while they can; nothing can recover it, not `mr`, not a plate, not a kit. `mr` writes it nowhere: keep it apart from every plate and every kit, and rehearse it every year on the signer that holds the seed, never wiping or re-importing the seed for this check: your kit's yearly line gives the reference to check it against; see "Rehearsing a seed's passphrase" in `mr --help`

**T23: the mismatch possibility** (every root mismatch message names it):

> if this root was made with a passphrase, run again and type it; a mistyped one gives these same letters with other keys

**The root-refusal tail** (ends T6, T11m, the disjoint-mask and `--seeds`-in-mask refusals, T20c and T20r):

> nothing is minted: keep this root's paper, destroy nothing, and never wipe a signer that holds someone else's seed; see "When create refuses a root" in `mr --help`

### `mr root` texts

**The prompt, coins** (stderr):

> type your 237 coin flips (H or T), on as many lines as you like, then an empty line to end:

**The prompt, dice** (stderr):

> type your dice rolls (1 to 6; at least 92, 99 recommended), on as many lines as you like, then an empty line to end:

**The prompt, pills** (stderr):

> type the 22 words that landed face up, one draw at a time, on as many lines as you like, then an empty line to end:

**The pills draw rule** (`--from pills`, printed first):

> ONE DRAW = ONE PILL TOSSED: shake the bag, take one pill, toss it in your hand, type only the word facing up (never the other side), put the pill back; 22 draws

**The passphrase prompts** (stderr, printed only after the draws are accepted):

> this root's passphrase (empty for none; not shown):  
> this root's passphrase again:

**The root fingerprint** (stderr):

> root fingerprint with this passphrase: \<fp\> (screen only: compare it with what `mr child` and `create` print; never write it down)

**The mask line** (stdout):

> this root makes letters \<list\>

**The engrave line** (stdout):

> engrave this root only after `create` accepts it, as the ms1 string `create` then prints for it; until then the words live on paper at this sitting

**The draw refusals** (a flip count is exit 2, the others exit 1; each names a draw by its position only and never echoes what was typed):

> coins: give exactly 237 flips (H/T), you gave \<n\>  
> coins: flip \<n\> is not H or T (or 1 or 0)  
> dice: at least 92 rolls, you gave \<n\>  
> dice: roll \<n\> is not a digit 1 to 6  
> pills: give exactly 22 draws, you gave \<n\>  
> pills: draw \<n\> is not a word of the BIP-39 English list  
> every source gives mixed bits: this input is a test pattern or a mistake

### `mr child` texts

**The prompt** (stderr):

> root: type its 24 words or its ms1 string, on one line or several, then an empty line to end:

**The passphrase prompts** (stderr, printed only after the root is accepted):

> the root's passphrase (empty for none; not shown):  
> the root's passphrase again:

**The root fingerprint** (stderr only):

> root fingerprint with this passphrase: \<fp\>; network \<net\>, base index \<b\>

**The mask line** (stdout):

> this root makes letters \<list\>

**The per-seat lines** (stdout, when a card is given):

> seat @\<i\> letter \<L\>: child of this root at index \<n\>: matches  
> seat @\<i\> letter \<L\>: not a child of this root  
> seat @\<i\> letter \<L\>: not a child of this root: its fingerprint is this root's child \<M\>  
> seat @\<i\>: no fingerprint on the card (or `00000000`): not checked

**The per-letter lines** (stdout, when a card is given):

> letter \<L\>: matched   (with --check)  
> letter \<L\> is on no seat of this card  
> letter \<L\> (seat @\<i\>): not a child of this root as read: no words printed

**The words heading, no card** (stderr, before the first set of words):

> not checked against any card: at a first card's sitting, `create --root` checks the keys exported from them before any card exists; otherwise check them, on the offline computer, before any use: see "Restoring from the root plate" in `mr --help`

**The words heading, a letter without a fingerprint** (stderr, before that letter's words):

> not checked against this card (no fingerprint): check with `mr verify MR1_CARD --network <net> --seeds <these words> --keys <that seat's extra plate or exported key>`, or on a card of this set that carries fingerprints

**The refusals** (the first three are usage errors, exit 2; the others exit 1):

> this root's mask does not list \<L\>  
> error: the following required arguments were not provided: [MR1\_CARD]...   (`--check` without a card; the argument parser's own text)  
> a root's passphrase is typed at the prompt, never on the command line  
> the root words or passphrase given differ from those used for `mr child` at the sitting, or the network: this root made no seat of this card; if this root was made with a passphrase, run again and type it; a mistyped one gives these same letters with other keys  
> nothing to check: this card carries no fingerprints

### Root refusals shared by `mr root`, `mr child` and `create --root`

**T3, the chain** (a chain that cannot find a mask letter in its window):

> root \<n\>'s chain passes index \<b + 4095\> without letter \<L\>

**T4, the root's letter**:

> root \<n\> has letter \<L\>: a root is made with letter A

**T4m, the empty mask**:

> root \<n\> makes no letter: its mask is empty

**The root-form refusal**:

> root \<n\> is not a 24-word English root (words, or its ms1 string): `mr` takes English roots only

**The published-root refusal** (every network, any mask):

> root \<n\> is made from a published test vector's draws: its words are guessable from the spec; make a new root from new draws

**T7, the passphrase repeat** (mints nothing):

> root \<n\>'s passphrase and its repeat differ

### `create --root` texts: prompts, refusals and key checks

**The prompts** (stderr; the passphrase prompts follow only after the root passes the root-only checks):

> root \<n\>: type its 24 words or its ms1 string, on one line or several, then an empty line to end:  
> root \<n\>'s passphrase (empty for none; not shown):  
> root \<n\>'s passphrase again:

**A value after `--root`** (usage, exit 2; the value is named by its position only):

> `--root` value \<n\>: type the root at its prompt, never on the command line

**`--root-label-set-id` without `--root -`** (usage, exit 2):

> `--root-label-set-id` labels a root plate: give it with `--root -`

**A passphrase on the command line** (usage, exit 2):

> a root's passphrase is typed at the prompt, never on the command line

**T16, the seat map is required** (usage, exit 2):

> a `--root` run takes `--seat-map` with every cold seat's letter (`hot` for the hot seat: `create` prints the letter the search took)

**T2, `--no-fingerprints`**:

> root mode needs fingerprints on the card: a wrong root passphrase gives children with the right letters and other keys

**T5, a root fills a seat**:

> root \<n\> fills seat @\<i\> of \<card or key record\>: a root may go on no signer; choose another root

**T6, a mask letter on a co-signer's seat** (when the letter is the root's only one, "\<L\> is root \<n\>'s only letter." is added before the tail):

> seat @\<i\> (letter \<L\>) is co-signer \<name\>'s, and root \<n\>'s mask lists \<L\>: a root makes no co-signer's seed. nothing is minted: keep this root's paper, destroy nothing, and never wipe a signer that holds someone else's seed; see "When create refuses a root" in `mr --help`

**T11m, a mask letter no seat takes**:

> root \<n\>'s mask lists \<L\>, which no seat of this card takes: a root makes exactly the letters its first card uses; root \<n\> makes \<list\>. nothing is minted: keep this root's paper, destroy nothing, and never wipe a signer that holds someone else's seed; see "When create refuses a root" in `mr --help`

**T11m, no seat is the root's**:

> no seat of this card is root \<n\>'s (it makes \<list\>). nothing is minted: keep this root's paper, destroy nothing, and never wipe a signer that holds someone else's seed; see "When create refuses a root" in `mr --help`

**Two roots, one letter**:

> roots \<n\> and \<m\> both make letter \<L\>: two roots never make one letter. nothing is minted: keep this root's paper, destroy nothing, and never wipe a signer that holds someone else's seed; see "When create refuses a root" in `mr --help`

**A seed that is not the root's child**:

> seed \<n\> has letter \<L\>, which root \<m\>'s mask lists, and is not root \<m\>'s child \<L\>: one letter is one seed. nothing is minted: keep this root's paper, destroy nothing, and never wipe a signer that holds someone else's seed; see "When create refuses a root" in `mr --help`

**T20c, the day-0 signer check** (printed only beside a matching child seat of the same root; the fact line follows when the seat's key is another child of the root):

> seat @\<i\> (letter \<L\>) is root \<n\>'s child \<L\>, but the key given is not its key at \<path\>. Seat @\<j\> (letter \<M\>) matched with this root (fingerprint \<fp\>), its passphrase and the network (\<net\>, base index \<b\>), so none of them is the cause: a mistyped passphrase gives these same letters with other keys on every seat. nothing is minted: keep this root's paper, destroy nothing, and never wipe a signer that holds someone else's seed; see "When create refuses a root" in `mr --help`  
> the fact line: seat @\<i\>'s key is root \<n\>'s child \<M\>'s; with this `--seat-map`, seat @\<i\> (path \<path\>) is letter \<L\>, listed by \<name\> (\<role\>), and letter \<M\> is at seat @\<j\> (path \<path\>), listed by \<name\> (\<role\>)

**T20r, no child seat matches** (replaces the per-seat lines when no given child seat of root \<n\> matches; the fact line is added for a seat whose key is another child of the root):

> seat(s) @\<i\>, @\<j\> … (root \<n\>'s children \<L\>, \<M\> …) do not match the keys given. This run's root fingerprint is \<fp\>, network \<net\>, base index \<b\>: compare them with what `mr child` printed. If this root was made with a passphrase, run again and type it; a mistyped one gives these same letters with other keys. [\<fact line\>. ]Do not wipe the offline computer yet: at this sitting it is wiped once, at its end, after the closing checklist. nothing is minted: keep this root's paper, destroy nothing, and never wipe a signer that holds someone else's seed; see "When create refuses a root" in `mr --help`

**A 00000000 record replaced**:

> seat @\<i\> (typed @\<j\>): the key's fingerprint 00000000 is replaced by root \<n\>'s child \<L\>'s \<fp\>; mint any mk1 plate of this seat with `mk encode --origin-fingerprint <fp>`, as the `mk encode` lines printed here do, never from the record; in the closing checklist's `verify` line, write \<fp\> in place of 00000000

**The has-no-key refusal's root tail** (a seat whose letter a mask lists):

> `mr` never generates a seed: on the offline computer, `mr child --network <net>`, root \<n\> typed at its prompt, prints root \<n\>'s child \<L\>'s words; load them on this seat's signer with no passphrase and export the key at \<path\>; do not wipe the offline computer yet: at this sitting it is wiped once, at its end, after the closing checklist (the wipe line, its last step)

**Root on no signer**:

> root \<n\> is on no signer: if you made it on one, wipe it there before you deposit

**The root fingerprint** (stderr only, before the checks):

> root \<n\>'s fingerprint with this passphrase: \<fp\>; network \<net\>, base index \<b\>

**The passphrase line** (every `--root` run, once per root):

> if root \<n\> has a passphrase, it is your secret alone; no heir needs it (heirs' plates carry no passphrase); with the root plate it spends every shape at once; keep it apart from every plate and every kit

### `create --root` texts: the report and the plates

**The report: which seats a root makes** (per shape, where true):

> root \<n\>'s children alone spend shape \<k\> at once  
> after \<delay\>, root \<n\> alone spends shape \<k\>'s \<path\>

**The one-root line** (with one root; the report and the first card's kit notes):

> every seed of yours on this card comes from root \<n\>: whoever holds its plate and passphrase spends every shape, every hot wallet of these seeds included; keep it as you would keep all your seeds together, in your kit at a place apart from the card and from the first card's notes, which stay with a card copy

**The root made letters** (the report):

> root \<n\> has now made letters \<list\>; it makes no other card

**The root plate's string** (stderr's seed-print channel only, after every check has passed):

> root \<n\>'s plate: engrave this ms1 string: \<ms1 string\>

**A root-made seed's plate** (stderr's seed-print channel only: an heir's child and the hot child where a role lists `hot`):

> kit \<name\>, ms1 plate of \<L\>: engrave this ms1 string: \<ms1 string\>

**The letter-A line**:

> seed \<n\> (seat @\<i\>) has letter A, like root \<m\>: tell the plates apart by their labels

**The default label of a root plate** (the plate's label):

> may hold funds at nonstandard derivation paths; keep it; never load it on a signer or online device

**The plain label of a root plate** (the plate's label under `--root-label-set-id`):

> root \<n\>, letter A, of seed set \<set id\>: a seed-set root; it holds no coins itself and rebuilds the set's seeds, by `mr child` on the offline computer; keep it while any card uses them; it is no card's plate and is never retired with one; never load it on a signer or hot device, and never decode it into one: use `mr child`; this plate restores keys, not the wallet: a card copy (any kit's) or this kit's re-mint sheet is also needed; first card only (a re-run at the first card's sitting, after a failed check or to print a plate's string again, is the same first card): never give this to `create` again

**T24, the label warning** (stderr, with `--root-label-set-id`):

> warning: `--root-label-set-id` puts this card's set id on root \<n\>'s label and says plainly that the plate is a seed-set root that holds no coins itself. The label then destroys the root plate's plausible deniability: it tells a finder what the plate is, and the set id moves with the root's passphrase, so anyone holding the plate and its label can test a guessed passphrase offline, and learn whether it has one. It might help with future spending if the information elsewhere (every card copy and the first card's notes) is lost. Without this flag the label is ambiguous ("may hold funds at nonstandard derivation paths; keep it; never load it on a signer or online device") and carries no set id, and only the bare plate is deniable

**A creator's own letter-A plate** (its label in a `--root` run):

> seed A (your own seed, not a root)

**Two letter-A plates** (added when the hot child lands on A and a role lists `hot`):

> two letter-A plates in this kit: only the hot child's goes on a hot device

### Root-mode texts in the kit notes

**The root-plate note** (the creator's first card's kit notes, once per root):

> root \<n\>'s plate is an ms1 string but is no signer's seed: never decode it into a signer, never type its words into one, and never use a signer's codex32 import (a Coldcard's Import Codex32 or any other) on this plate (the ms1 restore note in these notes, if any, is for your other ms1 plates, never this one). On the offline computer, `mr child`, the plate typed at its prompt, prints the words of the seeds it made, which are what you load: see "Restoring from the root plate" in `mr --help`; without `mr`: see "Recovery without mr", the sheet printed in these notes (also in the toolkit manual's `mr` chapter). A wallet that reads root \<n\>'s plate at standard paths shows nothing: never judge it worthless from that. Never give it to `create` after this first card's sitting (a re-run at that sitting, after a failed check or to print a plate's string again, is the same first card)

**The index line** (the first card's kit notes):

> root \<n\> made letters \<L\> (BIP-85 BIP-39 application, English, 24 words, index \<i\>, from the root's words and root \<n\>'s passphrase, typed from memory, none if it has none; it is written in no kit), …

**The T8 line** (the first card's kit notes; the sitting's report):

> on the offline computer, with root \<n\> read from its plate, run `mr child --check --network <net> MR1_CARD` at this sitting, typing the plate's ms1 string at its prompt; it exits 0 only when every letter the plate's mask lists matches its seat (it prints the per-seat lines, no words). If it passes: go on to the closing checklist, without wiping the offline computer or destroying anything yet. If it does not: deposit nothing, destroy nothing and keep the paper root; see "When a day-0 check fails" in `mr --help`

**The root rehearsal line** (the first card's kit notes):

> rehearse yearly, on the offline computer: `mr child --check --network <net> MR1_CARD`, typing root \<n\>'s plate (its ms1 string) at its prompt and its passphrase from memory (none if it has none), must exit 0 (it prints no words); it tests root \<n\>'s passphrase too, if it has one. If it fails, or you cannot recall the passphrase: keep every signer, wipe none of them and deposit nothing more to this card's shapes; see "Rehearsing the root" in `mr --help`. Then wipe the offline computer: wipe the offline computer: if its shell was not started with history off, close every shell first and only then delete its history file (bash writes the session back on exit); then delete every file this sitting wrote; a live system with no persistent storage is wiped by shutting it down

**The passphrase layout note** (the first card's kit notes):

> if root \<n\> has a passphrase: never write it, any part of it or a note of how its characters are typed (its keyboard layout) in a kit; keep any such note with the passphrase itself, never in a kit; see "Choosing a root passphrase" in `mr --help`

**The offline-guessing note** (the first card's kit notes; with `--root-label-set-id` it adds "this run put the set id on root \<n\>'s label: the labelled plate is not deniable either; " before the pointer):

> any copy of this card (every kit holds one), these notes (each child's index, the set id and the digest move with the passphrase), a saved `create` report or `mr child` output, the extra plates' seat checks and any other plate of a seed root \<n\> made (an heir's or the hot child's ms1 plate, the mk1 plate of a root seat) let a holder of root \<n\>'s plate learn whether it has a passphrase and test any guess offline: only the bare root plate is deniable. Keep these notes with a card copy and every one of these apart from root \<n\>'s plate; if root \<n\> has a passphrase, keep it apart from every plate and every kit; see "Where each item is stored" in `mr --help`

**The J11 line** (the creator's kit notes):

> if every signer of yours is lost or broken: your restore line is `mr child --network <net> --letters <her letters> MR1_CARD`, on the offline computer, the root plate typed at its prompt; it prints your own letters' words only, never an heir's; see "Restoring from the root plate" in `mr --help`

**The later-card line** (the creator's kit notes):

> later cards follow today's rules; root-made seeds are ordinary seeds given by their words (`mr child` on the offline computer prints them from the root plate: see "Restoring from the root plate" in `mr --help`) or their keys

**The key-source gloss** (wherever a print in the creator's kit names her root-made seed as a key source):

> the ms1 plate of \<L\>, or the plate of the root that made it (its words: `mr child` on the offline computer, the plate typed at its prompt; see "Restoring from the root plate" in `mr --help`)

**The export line** (the kit notes):

> export each root child's key with no passphrase

**The second card copy** (the creator's kit notes):

> a second copy of this card: at the extras' place, apart from the card kept with these notes and from every root plate

**The extras' storage line** (every extra plate in a `--root` run):

> store it at the extras' place, apart from every root plate and every ms1 plate of yours

**The seed rehearsal line** (the notes of every kit that holds an ms1 plate of a seed other than a root plate, on every card; the clause in parentheses prints in a `--root` run only):

> if a seed in this kit (never a root plate: the root's check is `mr child --check`) has a passphrase of its own, rehearse it every year on the signer that holds the seed, never wiping or re-importing the seed for this check; check it against \<REFERENCE\>. If you cannot recall it: deposit nothing more to this card's shapes, and never power off or update a signer that still has it; see "Rehearsing a seed's passphrase" in `mr --help`

**The seed rehearsal line's reference** (\<REFERENCE\>, by kit mode and card):

> a kit that holds its card loose, on a card with fingerprints: the master fingerprint `mr decode MR1_CARD` lists for its seat (a hot child's parent that fills no seat: the one these notes give for it)  
> a sealed or executor-mode heir's kit, on a card with fingerprints: the master fingerprint this kit's seat-key line for its seat gives, never the card in the bag (or with the executor)  
> a sealed or executor-mode heir's kit, on a card without fingerprints: the xpub the signer shows at each of this kit's seat-key lines' origin path, matched against that line  
> a kit that holds its card loose, on a card without fingerprints: the record the signer exports at the seat's origin path (this kit's 'seat @\<i\> (letter \<L\>): origin path' line) with the passphrase entered, given to `mr verify MR1_CARD --network <net>` with `--keys`, beside this kit's mk1 plates and the `--seat-check` values these notes give: it passes when it prints a line beginning `record <k> → seat @<i>`

### Root-mode texts in the closing checklist

**The first line, in a `--root` run** (the closing checklist):

> this set is unrecoverable until this card is engraved and the `verify` line below passes

**The words prefix** (printed before every closing-checklist line that types words):

> on the offline computer, in the shell you started with history off (bash or zsh: `unset HISTFILE` first; fish: `fish --private`), never on your everyday computer: this line types seeds' words

**T20, the `verify` line** (the closing checklist, after the words prefix):

> after `create`, export each seat's key again now from its loaded signer (a co-signer exports again from theirs; not the file given to `create`, which T20c already checked; a seat whose signer is not at this sitting: its mk1 plate) and run `mr verify MR1_CARD --network <net> --set-id <set id> --seeds "SEEDS_YOU_HOLD" --keys "KEY_1" "KEY_2"`, MR1\_CARD being this card's strings, SEEDS\_YOU\_HOLD every word set `mr child` printed for this card, and every other seed your role lists that has no passphrase of its own: its ms1 plate or its words, and KEY\_1, KEY\_2 and so on those fresh records and plates, one quoted value each (a seed with its own passphrase is given by its seat's record in `--keys`): it passes when it exits 0 and prints, for your seat, a line beginning `record <k> → seat @<i>` (a `, sc:…` suffix follows; it is the seat check, not a failure). If your signer's export has no fingerprint (you wrote `00000000` for `create`), write in its place the fingerprint `create` printed for that seat, as `[<fp>/<path>]XPUB`: a record left at `00000000` always fails this line. Deposit nothing until it passes. Never leave a seed out of `--seeds` to make this line pass. If it fails, never wipe a signer that holds someone else's seed (an heir's, a co-signer's, or yours from before); for a word set `mr child` printed (`mr child`, on the offline computer, and its prompt are where the root's passphrase is typed, never this line or a signer): if this root was made with a passphrase, run again and type it; a mistyped one gives these same letters with other keys; see "When a day-0 check fails" in `mr --help`

**The heir plate check** (the closing checklist, one per heir; the placeholders are \<NAME\>\_PLATE (the heir's plate string) and \<NAME\>\_BAG\_MK1\_PLATES (that bag's mk1 plates)):

> before sealing \<name\>'s bag: `mr verify MR1_CARD --network <net> --set-id <set id> --seeds "<NAME>_PLATE" --keys "<NAME>_BAG_MK1_PLATES"`

**The heir plate re-run branch** (ends the heir plate check):

> if `create`'s print for kit \<name\> is gone, or this check fails: keep the paper root and destroy nothing yet; see "When a day-0 check fails" in `mr --help`

**The signer-screen line, per shape** (printed once per shape, on every card; "shape \<k\> (id \<id\>)" is the shape's display id):

> before the first deposit to shape \<k\> (id \<id\>), register shape \<k\>'s policy on a signer and check its first `mr address --shape <k>` on the screen

### Root-mode texts in a re-mint sheet

**The creator's first line, a `--root` card**:

> a re-mint replaces only the card: check its digest, then keep your kit exactly as the first card's notes list it; engrave nothing else this run names: no ms1 plate of a root child (the root plate stands in for them) and no new extra plate (the first card's extras stay at the extras' place)

**The first line of every other re-mint sheet of a shapes or root-mode card**:

> a re-mint replaces only the card: check its digest; engrave nothing else this run names

**`--root` is never given to a re-mint** (the notes say so):

> `--root` is never given to a re-mint

**A root child's seat source** (the creator's re-mint sheet):

> child of root \<n\>: words from `mr child --network <net> --letters <L>` on the offline computer, the root plate typed at its prompt, given in `--seeds`; key: the seat's extra plate, or a signer loaded with those words (no passphrase), exported at \<path\>; see "Re-minting a lost card" in `mr --help`

**The mismatch line** (the creator's re-mint sheet):

> if the digest differs or `create` refuses a child's words: never drop the seed, and wipe nothing before the digest matches; for the words `mr child` printed (`mr child`, on the offline computer, and its prompt are where the root's passphrase is typed, never `create`: these words carry none): if this root was made with a passphrase, run again and type it; a mistyped one gives these same letters with other keys; this re-mint's network is \<net\>, base index \<b\>; see "Re-minting a lost card" in `mr --help`
